[[sep-vplan-area-4]]
==== 4. SPI and DMA

[[sep_dma_hash_test]]
===== 4.1 `sep_dma_hash_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_dma_hash_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address)

*Objective*: Drives the secure DMA engine from real firmware to copy a block from SRAM into the CPU's data tightly-coupled memory with the inline hash engine enabled, then proves two things independently: the copied data is identical to the source, and the hardware digest is correct. Completion is delivered as an interrupt to a registered service routine, so the interrupt path is proven as well as the datapath. The digest comparison is the strong part: copy correctness alone would not show the inline hash engine consumed the same bytes that were copied. Two digest lengths are walked. SHA-256 runs over build-varying data (the firmware never seeds `rand()`, so the byte vector is fixed within one build and changes when the firmware is rebuilt) against a software SHA-256 computed in firmware over the same bytes. SHA-384 has no 64-bit SHA-2 core in this firmware, so it runs over a fixed message against the published FIPS 180-4 digest for that message; `OpcSha384` is a supported DMA opcode alongside `OpcSha256`.

*Test Coverage*: Firmware DMA SRAM to DCCM with inline SHA-256 and SHA-384, digest compare, copy, and IRQ.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Initialise the fabric filter; register the DMA done and error service routines.
. Program the enabled memory range and confirm the configuration lock is open.
. Fill the SRAM source region with random data.
. Program source, destination, size, transfer width and increment modes.
. Start the transfer with the SHA-256 opcode and digest-swap set.
. Wait on interrupt; the service routine clears status.
. Read the hardware digest; compute the software digest; compare. Compare source against destination word for word.
. Then stage the fixed FIPS 180-4 56-byte test message in the same SRAM region, reprogram only the length and the opcode, and start a second transfer with the SHA-384 opcode.
. Wait on the interrupt again; read the twelve digest words; render them as hex and compare against the published vector. Compare the copied message against the source bytes.
. Disable the DMA interrupt enables, then carry the same 56-byte message as two 28-byte chunks, pacing the chunks on `STATUS.chunk_done` and counting them. Compare the digest against the published SHA-256 vector for the whole message.
. Finally run the same single-chunk SHA-256 twice, once with `DIGEST_SWAP` set and once clear, and compare the two digest word arrays against each other.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CFG
|The DMA configuration lock is open before programming. A wrong value is counted as an error: the firmware emits `ERROR: CFG_REGWEN not unlocked`, increments its error count, and that count gates the mailbox PASS magic.
|CHK-COMPLETE
|The transfer completes and the interrupt reaches the service routine.
|CHK-DIGEST
|The hardware digest equals an independent software SHA-256 over the same data. The eight hardware digest words are read through the array-indexed accessor `SHA2_DIGEST_0_BASE_ADDR(i)`, so a wrong array stride or a wrong register symbol shifts the assembled digest and the `memcmp` below fails.
|CHK-COPY
|Destination matches source word for word.
|CHK-NONVAC
|For the SHA-256 pass: the software digest is recomputed from the actual SRAM contents on every run and no SHA-256 digest literal exists anywhere in the firmware or the testbench, so the comparison cannot be satisfied by a constant. `rand()` is never seeded, so the byte vector is the same for every seed within one firmware build -- the seed randomises only the clock period -- but it is not stable across builds.
|CHK-SHA384
|The twelve hardware digest words, rendered big-endian as hex, equal the FIPS 180-4 digest for the 56-byte message the firmware staged. Unlike the SHA-256 pass this compares against a literal, so its non-vacuity argument is different and is stated rather than inherited: the message is the published input and the vector is the published output, neither is read back from the engine, and a DUT that returned a constant, a zero digest, or the SHA-256 of the same message fails. The 56-byte message is used in place of the one-block "abc" vector because the DMA transfers whole 4-byte words. `OPCODE_ERROR` on this transfer is reported as a failure, not skipped, so a build that does not implement `OpcSha384` fails here rather than passing quietly.
|CHK-SHA384-COPY
|The same SHA-384 transfer also copied the message to the destination, checked byte for byte. A digest engine fed correctly while the copy path is broken fails here.
|CHK-MULTICHUNK
|The same 56-byte message carried as two 28-byte chunks produces the published FIPS 180-4 SHA-256 digest for the whole message. A hash that restarted per chunk, or that dropped the tail, produces a different digest and fails. The observed chunk count is required to be at least two before the digest is compared, so a transfer that silently completed in one chunk fails rather than passing a single-chunk digest under a multi-chunk name. A chunk counts only when its pacing poll ends on `STATUS.chunk_done` or `STATUS.done`; a poll that runs out of budget fails the pass and is never counted. Interrupts are disabled for this pass because the service routine clears `STATUS.chunk_done`, which the pacing loop has to read.
|CHK-DIGEST-SWAP
|Every digest word produced with `DIGEST_SWAP` clear is the byte-reverse of the corresponding word produced with it set, over the same message. Every other pass in this entry sets the bit, so none of them can tell a working `DIGEST_SWAP` from a DMA that ignores it; this is the only entry that grades the bit. All-words-identical is failed explicitly, since a palindromic digest is the one way a byte-reverse comparison could be satisfied while the bit does nothing.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: CHK-NONVAC is a property of the sources rather than a run record -- no SHA-256 digest literal exists in the firmware or the testbench, so the comparison cannot be satisfied by a constant. Every other row is logged by the test: CHK-DIGEST compares the two digests the console carries, and CHK-CFG, CHK-COMPLETE, CHK-COPY, CHK-SHA384-COPY, CHK-SHA384, CHK-MULTICHUNK and CHK-DIGEST-SWAP each gate on the firmware line that scores them.

'''''

[[sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test]]
===== 4.2 `sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip (`OcahSpiFlash` on all four data lanes in `quad_io` mode, `dq_out` = `spi_sd_o` and `dq_in` = `spi_sd_i`, with `+sep_spi_four_lane` in the testlist args; `OcahSpiMonitor` on the pad ports and the lane taps through `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_wire_monitor.py`; `SepSpiScoreboard`, `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`), CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address)

*Objective*: Proves the hardware handshake between the SPI host and the secure DMA engine, and that the flash data of a read at x1, x2 and x4 reaches SRAM unchanged. Lane n is `sd[n]`, and in standard mode DATA[0] is the host output and DATA[1] the host input (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, "SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment"). Each byte moves most significant bit first, the least significant bit of each pair (x2) or nibble (x4) is on `SD[0]`, and with `ByteOrder = 1` the first byte received lands in RXDATA[7:0] (OpenTitan SPI_HOST theory of operation, "Input and Output Byte Ordering").

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed (RAND-REP). One seed per run (no `reseed`). The seed draws the order of the three reads, the x2 opcode (0x3B or 0xBB), the x4 opcode (0x6B or 0xEB), a distinct flash sector per read and the 64 data bytes of each read. Byte 0 of each read is its index and byte 1 is byte 0 XOR 0xA5, so every pattern holds at least two byte values, no two reads start alike, and no pattern starts with the erased value 0xFF.

*Test Coverage*: SPI-FIFO to DMA to SRAM handshake at x1 (0x03), x2 (0x3B or 0xBB) and x4 (0x6B or 0xEB), each read with its own seeded pattern.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The leaf draws the three reads, preloads each read range into the flash model and patches the reads into the firmware block `g_spi2_params` (`SPI2_PARAM_MAGIC`) of the staged DTCM image.
. Initialise the fabric filter. Configure the SPI host: receive watermark, clock divider, enable, watermark event.
. For each staged read, in the drawn order: fill the SRAM destination with a marker word; program the DMA (source is the receive register in wrapping mode, destination is SRAM incrementing, hardware handshake enabled) and start it; issue the flash read at the width of its opcode: the opcode and the address in standard TX segments, the address at the read width for 0xBB and 0xEB, the dummy cycles of the opcode, then a 64-byte RX segment at the read width. Each TX segment has its own TXDATA words.
. Poll to completion, read the status again and require done still set, then write-one-to-clear the status bits.
. Check every DMA-written SRAM word of the read against the staged words, and print one PASS line per read with its opcode, width and byte count.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DATAPATH
|For each read, every word the DMA wrote to SRAM equals the staged word at its position, proving the chain from the flash model through the receive queue and the trigger to SRAM at that width. The staged pattern holds at least two byte values, so a stuck lane, the idle all-ones line, the marker word and a partial transfer all fail. The firmware rejects a staged pattern of one byte value. The leaf requires exactly one firmware PASS line per staged read, in order, with its opcode and width. Evidence: `CHK-DATAPATH PASS: op=<op> width=x<w> bytes=<b>` per read.
|CHK-RW1C
|The DMA done status bit still reads set on a second read after the poll, so a read does not clear it and it does not drop by itself; it then clears on write-one-to-clear and reads back clear. Both status values are logged. `ERROR` and `CHUNK_DONE` are never set in hardware-handshake mode, so their clear is not exercised.
|CHK-NOERR
|The DMA reports no error and the SPI controller reaches idle with clean error status.
|CHK-SPI-WIRE
|The shared SPI monitor `OcahSpiMonitor` (`ocah_spi_vip`, attached through `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_wire_monitor.py`) grades the pad ports `spi_cs_n_o`, `spi_sck_o` and `spi_mosi_o` in SPI Mode 0 on every frame: SCK is at the CPOL level at each chip-select edge and moves only to that level while chip select is high; while chip select is low, MOSI does not change on a sample edge, nor after a sample edge and before the next shift edge; while chip select is low, chip select, SCK and MOSI resolve to 0 or 1. The monitor also grades the monitor-only taps `spi_sd_o`, `spi_sd_oe_o`, `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` against the output-enable rules of `doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc` ("SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment": lane n drives `sd[n]` with output enable `sd_oe[n]`, chip select and clock have `cs_oe` and `sck_oe`, and the host drives a data lane only while chip select is asserted): no data lane output enable rises, or is on at an SCK edge, while chip select is high (`oe_idle`); at each sample edge the data lane output enables are none, lane 0, lanes 1:0 or lanes 3:0 (`oe_lanes`); and the chip-select and clock output enables are on at each sample edge (`oe_ctrl`). The MOSI edge and X/Z rules apply to every data lane whose output enable is on. A host that drives a data lane while chip select is high, enables another lane pattern, or turns off the chip-select or clock output enable in a frame fails. At least one Mode 0 frame is graded. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-WIRE PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> per_mode=00:<frames> wire_errors=0`.
|CHK-SPI-FRAME-SHAPE
|The flash model refuses no frame for its shape (`FRAME_SHAPE_REASONS`, `hw/common/dv/vip/ocah_spi_vip/README.md`): no frame ends inside the command byte, the address, the dummy phase or a payload byte, every PAGE PROGRAM carries a payload byte, and no WRITE ENABLE, WRITE DISABLE or SECTOR ERASE carries a bit after its last command or address bit. A controller that truncates or pads a frame fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-FRAME-SHAPE PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> refused=0`.
|CHK-SPI-LEDGER
|The flash model records and the monitor frames match one for one, in order, with the same command byte and the same input clock count. A frame on the pads that the model did not record, or a model record with no frame on the pads, fails. Evidence: the `CHK-SPI-LEDGER` checker record.
|CHK-SPI-SB-RX
|For each read, the SPI data scoreboard (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`) compares three copies of the payload byte for byte: the seeded bytes the leaf preloaded at the read address, the flash record's `data_out`, and the monitor's own decode of the payload phase on the lanes at the read width (x1: `sd[1]`; x2 and x4: lanes 1:0 and 3:0, the least significant bit of each pair or nibble on `sd[0]`; most significant bit first; `doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, "SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment"; OpenTitan SPI_HOST theory of operation, "Input and Output Byte Ordering"). The flash record is the first unused record with the read opcode, and the monitor frame is the one at the same position (CHK-SPI-LEDGER lines the two lists up). A flash model that drives the wrong lane or reverses the bit order of a pair or nibble, and a host that reads at another width, fail. All three copies are taken before the host samples the lanes, so a host that samples the wrong lane or reverses a pair or nibble does not fail this check; it delivers wrong words to SRAM and fails CHK-DATAPATH. Evidence: one `SPI-SB RX` line per read and `CHK-SPI-SB-RX PASS seed=<n> frames=3 bytes=192`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_dma_cpu_contention_test]]
===== 4.3 `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address)

*Objective*: Prove CPU and DMA SRAM traffic is correct under real local-crossbar contention. A TB monitor counts cycles where both masters present an SRAM request on the same AXI address channel; DMA BUSY is the independent proof that the job spans the CPU store loop.

*Test Coverage*: CPU and DMA as concurrent SRAM masters.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Inherit the global side-effect region marking from startup code; this test performs no per-test region write of its own.
. Seed the source pattern; clear the destination and the CPU's own region.
. Start the DMA copy and do not wait.
. Immediately run the CPU store loop.
. Sample DMA status at store-loop exit, then poll to completion. After firmware completion, require the TB's same-channel CPU/DMA SRAM-request overlap count to be nonzero.
. Check both regions; read the status again and require done still set, then clear the status bits.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-IN-FLIGHT
|A status sample at store-loop exit shows the DMA busy and not done, proving the DMA job spans the CPU store loop.
|CHK-OVERLAP
|The CPU-LSU and DMA masters simultaneously present SRAM requests on the same local-crossbar AW or AR channel for at least one cycle. The monitor filters both addresses to the generated SRAM aperture, so a concurrent DMA-CSR status read cannot satisfy this checker.
|CHK-NOERR
|The DMA completes with no error.
|CHK-RW1C
|The done status bit still reads set on a second read after the poll, so a read does not clear it and it does not drop by itself; it then clears on write-one-to-clear. Both status values are logged. Chunk-done is never raised in this single-chunk configuration, so its clear is not exercised.
|CHK-DMA-DATA
|The DMA destination matches its source.
|CHK-CPU-DATA
|The CPU's own region holds exactly what the CPU wrote — its traffic was not corrupted by the competing master.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test]]
===== 4.4 `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, firmware `spi_ot_dma_tx_test`

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address), observation-only probes `spi_tx_qd_probe_o`, `spi_lsio_trigger_probe_o` and `dma_busy_probe_o`, and `OcahSpiMonitor` (`ocah_spi_vip`) on the SPI pad ports and the lane taps through `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_wire_monitor.py`. `OcahSpiFlash` sits on all four data lanes (`quad_io` mode, `dq_out` = `spi_sd_o`, `dq_in` = `spi_sd_i`), with `+sep_spi_four_lane` in the testlist args

*Objective*: Drives SRAM through the secure DMA into the OpenTitan SPI host transmit FIFO and on to the flash BFM, then reads the flash back and compares against the cocotb-seeded random source data. Each case programs at one lane width: PAGE PROGRAM 0x02 (one standard TX segment) or QUAD PAGE PROGRAM 0x32 (the opcode and the address in a standard TX segment with chip select held, then the data in a quad TX segment). After the walk, `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW` must still be clear when `STATUS.TXFULL` first reads 1, the next TXDATA write must latch exclusive `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW`, and the host must run again after recovery.

*Test Coverage*: DMA SRAM to SPI TX to flash at x1 and x4, readback compare, and TX overflow at TXFULL and after the write past it, with recovery. The flash model has no dual program command, so x2 TX data is graded in <<sep_spi_pad_speed_dir_matrix_test,entry 4.7>>, <<sep_spi_pad_timing_cfg_rand_test,entry 4.8>> and <<sep_spi_fifo_stall_watermark_rand_test,entry 4.9>>.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk the seeded length and trigger cells, one case per cell: DMA SRAM into the OpenTitan SPI TX FIFO, page-program the flash BFM with the opcode of the case (one case 0x02, one case 0x32, the third drawn, in a seeded order), read the flash back and compare to the SRAM source; read the DMA status again and require done still set, then W1C-clear DMA done. While DMA `STATUS.busy` is set, cocotb samples the DMA trigger input and the transmit FIFO depth on each clock.
. Write TXDATA until `STATUS.TXFULL` reads 1; read `ERROR_STATUS` and expect OVERFLOW clear. Write one more TXDATA word; expect exclusive `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW`; `CONTROL.SW_RST` plus write-one-to-clear; a flash status read must complete.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TRIGGER
|The transmit watermark tracks FIFO occupancy across the threshold, with the exact polarity asserted and the observed values logged. `EVENT_ENABLE.TXWM` is programmed.
|CHK-DMA-TX
|The flash content equals the SRAM source, compared against cocotb-seeded random data. The flash model accepted each program, so each frame ended on a byte boundary. One firmware PASS line per case names its opcode, width and byte count; the leaf requires them in case order, with at least one case at x1 and one at x4. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-TX PASS seed=<n> case[<i>] op=0x<op> width=x<w> bytes=<b>`.
|CHK-DMA-DONE
|The DMA done bit still reads set on a second read after the poll, so a read does not clear it and it does not drop by itself; it then clears on write-one-to-clear and reads back clear. Both status values are logged. Handshake mode does not raise `STATUS.chunk_done` (RTL: chunk_done is the inverse of cfg_handshake_en); that status is `sep_dma_basic_test`.
|CHK-SPI-IDLE
|The SPI host returns to idle with a clean error status.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The flash readback holds content that is not the erased 0xFF pattern, so the content compare above is not erased-against-erased. Scanned on the value the flash returned, not on the staged source, which the config forces non-erased and could never fail.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|The cocotb golden walked the deterministic length and trigger cells, requiring a literal floor of write-enable and page-program transactions on the bus and a per-case address and data match.
|OBS-MULTICHUNK
|Logged, not graded: no source states one trigger per chunk in the transmit direction or a transmit FIFO depth bound (`secure_dma.adoc` states only that in hardware handshake mode the DMA reads in chunks from the peripheral). Per case, during DMA `STATUS.busy`, the leaf logs the DMA trigger assertions against the 16-byte chunk count (floor 2) and the transmit FIFO depth peak against `TX_WATERMARK` - 1 + one chunk (7 words), from DUT probes. Control: the probe shows one busy window per case, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `OBS-MULTICHUNK case[<i>]: chunks=<c> ... assertions=<a> per_chunk_floor=<f> peak_txqd=<q> paced_bound=7`.
|CHK-ERR-OVERFLOW
|`ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW` reads 0 when `STATUS.TXFULL` first reads 1, and the next TXDATA write latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW` (`spi_controller.adoc`, `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW`, `STATUS.TXFULL`); `CONTROL.SW_RST` plus write-one-to-clear then lets a flash status read complete. A host that sets OVERFLOW one write early fails the read at TXFULL; a host that does not set it fails the read after the extra write. The leaf requires the firmware line `CHK-ERR-OVERFLOW PASS: OVERFLOW clear at STATUS.TXFULL ...`; the firmware also logs `ERROR_STATUS at TXFULL=<v>`.
|CHK-SPI-WIRE
|The shared SPI monitor `OcahSpiMonitor` (`ocah_spi_vip`, attached through `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_wire_monitor.py`) grades the pad ports `spi_cs_n_o`, `spi_sck_o` and `spi_mosi_o` in SPI Mode 0 on every frame: SCK is at the CPOL level at each chip-select edge and moves only to that level while chip select is high; while chip select is low, MOSI does not change on a sample edge, nor after a sample edge and before the next shift edge; while chip select is low, chip select, SCK and MOSI resolve to 0 or 1. The monitor also grades the monitor-only taps `spi_sd_o`, `spi_sd_oe_o`, `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` against the output-enable rules of `doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc` ("SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment": lane n drives `sd[n]` with output enable `sd_oe[n]`, chip select and clock have `cs_oe` and `sck_oe`, and the host drives a data lane only while chip select is asserted): no data lane output enable rises, or is on at an SCK edge, while chip select is high (`oe_idle`); at each sample edge the data lane output enables are none, lane 0, lanes 1:0 or lanes 3:0 (`oe_lanes`); and the chip-select and clock output enables are on at each sample edge (`oe_ctrl`). The MOSI edge and X/Z rules apply to every data lane whose output enable is on. A host that drives a data lane while chip select is high, enables another lane pattern, or turns off the chip-select or clock output enable in a frame fails. At least one Mode 0 frame is graded. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-WIRE PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> per_mode=00:<frames> wire_errors=0`.
|CHK-SPI-FRAME-SHAPE
|The flash model refuses no frame for its shape (`FRAME_SHAPE_REASONS`, `hw/common/dv/vip/ocah_spi_vip/README.md`): no frame ends inside the command byte, the address, the dummy phase or a payload byte, every PAGE PROGRAM carries a payload byte, and no WRITE ENABLE, WRITE DISABLE or SECTOR ERASE carries a bit after its last command or address bit. A controller that truncates or pads a frame fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-FRAME-SHAPE PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> refused=0`.
|CHK-SPI-LEDGER
|The flash model records and the monitor frames match one for one, in order, with the same command byte and the same input clock count. A frame on the pads that the model did not record, or a model record with no frame on the pads, fails. Evidence: the `CHK-SPI-LEDGER` checker record.
|CHK-SPI-SB-TX
|For each case, the SPI data scoreboard (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`) compares three copies of the program payload byte for byte: the expected bytes (the case's TXDATA source words, least significant byte of each word first, as ByteOrder 1 sends them), the monitor's own decode of the payload phase on the lanes at the width of the case (0x02: `sd[0]`; 0x32: lanes 3:0, the least significant bit of each nibble on `sd[0]`, most significant bit first; OpenTitan SPI_HOST theory of operation, "Input and Output Byte Ordering"), and the flash record's `data_in`. The flash record is the first unused PAGE PROGRAM record at the case address, and the monitor frame is the one at the same position (CHK-SPI-LEDGER lines the two lists up). The flash model must accept the frame. A controller that drops, reorders or corrupts a payload byte, or sends the bytes of a word in the opposite order, fails; so does a case with no matching record. Evidence: one `SPI-SB TX` line per case with the expected, wire and device bytes, and `CHK-SPI-SB-TX PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> bytes=<n>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0. The flash BFM logs no error (`SPI-BFM FAIL`), and `flash.stop()` raises no exception that the BFM protocol engine kept.

'''''

[[sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test]]
===== 4.5 `sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, firmware `spi_ot_flash_cmd_test`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip (`OcahSpiFlash` on the SPI pad ports, and `OcahSpiMonitor` on the same ports through `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_wire_monitor.py`), CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address)

*Objective*: JEDEC, WREN/RDSR, WRDI, page program, FAST_READ, and sector erase against the flash BFM, with a neighbour-sector intact check, write-protect and status-register breadth, and the three host `ERROR_STATUS` classes with recovery. A lane leg then runs the dual and quad flash commands 0x3B (1-1-2), 0x6B (1-1-4), 0xBB (1-2-2), 0xEB (1-4-4) and the quad page program 0x32 (1-1-4) against the flash model bound to all four data lanes (`quad_io` mode, `dq_out` = `spi_sd_o`, `dq_in` = `spi_sd_i`; `+sep_spi_four_lane` in the testlist args). Each command segment runs at its own width (`hw/sys/sep/doc/spi.adoc`, Features), and the lane map and bit order are those of `doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc` ("SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment") and the OpenTitan SPI_HOST theory of operation ("Input and Output Byte Ordering").

*Test Coverage*: JEDEC, program, read, erase, write-protect, host error-status recovery, and the five lane commands with their read data and program data graded on the wire, in the flash record and in firmware.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. READ ID, then prove WEL set by WREN and cleared by WRDI.
. Program, FAST_READ, and sector-erase with neighbour intact.
. After the erase: prove WEL consumed; the controller delivers a page program with WEL clear (the device model refuses it); opcode `0x35` returns the seeded status-register-2 value.
. `ERROR_STATUS` is zero after that sequence. Then inject underflow, reserved `COMMAND.SPEED`, and an out-of-range chip-select, each exclusive; after each, pulse `CONTROL.SW_RST`, read `ERROR_STATUS` and require the class still set, then write-one-to-clear and require 0; READ ID works again.
. Lane leg. The leaf draws the order of the five lane commands, a distinct sector in 32 to 4095 for each (outside the sectors of the standard leg), a page in that sector, a word count (1 to 16) and the data words; the first byte of each range is the entry index. It preloads each read range into the flash model and patches the entries into `g_spi1_params` before the firmware starts. For each entry the firmware runs the command with the shared helpers of `fw/drivers/sep_spi.h`: a read (0x3B, 0x6B: opcode and address in one standard TX segment, 8 dummy cycles, data at x2 or x4; 0xBB, 0xEB: opcode in a standard TX segment, the address in a TX segment at x2 or x4, 4 or 6 dummy cycles, data at x2 or x4), or WREN, the quad page program 0x32 (opcode and address in a standard TX segment, data at x4) and a standard READ of the same range. It compares the words, requires `ERROR_STATUS` 0 and prints one PASS line per entry.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PROGRAM / CHK-READ
|A page program followed by a read returns the exact random pattern at the exact random address.
|CHK-ERASE
|A sector erase leaves the programmed region reading all-ones and leaves the adjacent 4 KiB neighbour sector intact. The region is proven non-erased earlier in the walk, by the page program and read-back pair that `CHK-PROGRAM`/`CHK-READ` covers, so the all-ones result is a change of state rather than a region that was never written.
|CHK-NO-ERROR
|The SPI host error status is zero after the sequence.
|CHK-JEDEC
|READ ID returns the BFM JEDEC identity.
|CHK-RDSR
|RDSR sees WEL set after WREN. A controller timeout is a distinct failure, not a status byte that looks like WEL.
|CHK-WRDI
|WRDI runs on a WEL that RDSR just proved set, then RDSR sees WEL clear.
|CHK-FAST-READ
|FAST_READ returns the programmed pattern.
|CHK-WEL-AUTOCLR
|The erase consumes the write-enable latch. The latch is state of the SPI flash device model, not of SEP: what this grades is that the controller drove a well-formed erase the device accepted and retired, and it fails if the controller leaves the device armed. It is not RTL coverage of a SEP register, and takes no feature credit as such.
|CHK-WP-PP
|The controller delivers a page program issued with the write-enable latch clear: the firmware command completes, and the golden requires a third page-program transaction at the device with the full address phase at the random address. It fails if the controller drops, truncates or mis-addresses the command. The refusal itself is device-model behaviour, not SEP: the latch is state of the SPI flash device model, which drops any page program while the latch is clear, whatever the controller drives. The firmware all-ones read-back, the empty payload record and the direct read of the model's stored array are therefore model self-checks and take no feature credit, the same as `CHK-WEL-AUTOCLR`.
|CHK-RDSR2
|Opcode `0x35` returns the seeded non-zero status-register-2 value. A decode that folds `0x35` onto `0x05` returns the write-enable SR1 encoding (`0x02`) and fails. A receive path that returns all-zero also fails. The firmware does not re-read SR1 on this step; the fail cases are the two wrong bytes, not a live SR1 compare.
|CHK-ERR-UNDERFLOW
|Reading `RXDATA` with the receive FIFO empty latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.UNDERFLOW`. It still reads set after the `CONTROL.SW_RST` pulse, which clears internal state and not registers, so the write-one-to-clear that follows is what releases it.
|CHK-ERR-CMDINVAL
|A command with the reserved speed encoding latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.CMDINVAL`. It still reads set after the `CONTROL.SW_RST` pulse, which clears internal state and not registers, so the write-one-to-clear that follows is what releases it.
|CHK-ERR-CSIDINVAL
|A segment whose chip-select is outside `NumCS` — driven at the top of the 32-bit `CSID` field, so the stimulus assumes no particular `NumCS` — latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.CSIDINVAL`. It still reads set after the `CONTROL.SW_RST` pulse, which clears internal state and not registers, so the write-one-to-clear that follows is what releases it.
|CHK-ERR-RECOVER
|READ ID completes with the BFM JEDEC identity after the three injections, so the host was released rather than left disabled.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|The seed varies the programmed address, word count and payload, and the order, sectors, pages, word counts and data of the lane leg. The opcode sequence of the standard leg is fixed, the lane opcodes follow in the drawn order, and the BFM golden requires that exact observed order.
|CHK-SPI-WIRE
|The shared SPI monitor `OcahSpiMonitor` (`ocah_spi_vip`, attached through `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_wire_monitor.py`) grades the pad ports `spi_cs_n_o`, `spi_sck_o` and `spi_mosi_o` in SPI Mode 0 on every frame: SCK is at the CPOL level at each chip-select edge and moves only to that level while chip select is high; while chip select is low, MOSI does not change on a sample edge, nor after a sample edge and before the next shift edge; while chip select is low, chip select, SCK and MOSI resolve to 0 or 1. The monitor also grades the monitor-only taps `spi_sd_o`, `spi_sd_oe_o`, `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` against the output-enable rules of `doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc` ("SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment": lane n drives `sd[n]` with output enable `sd_oe[n]`, chip select and clock have `cs_oe` and `sck_oe`, and the host drives a data lane only while chip select is asserted): no data lane output enable rises, or is on at an SCK edge, while chip select is high (`oe_idle`); at each sample edge the data lane output enables are none, lane 0, lanes 1:0 or lanes 3:0 (`oe_lanes`); and the chip-select and clock output enables are on at each sample edge (`oe_ctrl`). The MOSI edge and X/Z rules apply to every data lane whose output enable is on. A host that drives a data lane while chip select is high, enables another lane pattern, or turns off the chip-select or clock output enable in a frame fails. At least one Mode 0 frame is graded. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-WIRE PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> per_mode=00:<frames> wire_errors=0`.
|CHK-SPI-FRAME-SHAPE
|The flash model refuses no frame for its shape (`FRAME_SHAPE_REASONS`, `hw/common/dv/vip/ocah_spi_vip/README.md`): no frame ends inside the command byte, the address, the dummy phase or a payload byte, every PAGE PROGRAM carries a payload byte, and no WRITE ENABLE, WRITE DISABLE or SECTOR ERASE carries a bit after its last command or address bit. A controller that truncates or pads a frame fails. The page program issued with the latch clear is refused as `wel_clear`, which is not a shape reason. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-FRAME-SHAPE PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> refused=0`.
|CHK-SPI-LEDGER
|The flash model records and the monitor frames match one for one, in order, with the same command byte and the same input clock count. A frame on the pads that the model did not record, or a model record with no frame on the pads, fails. Evidence: the `CHK-SPI-LEDGER` checker record.
|CHK-SPI-SB-TX
|For the primary and the neighbour page program, the SPI data scoreboard (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`) compares three copies of the payload byte for byte: the expected bytes (the seeded words the firmware programs, least significant byte of each word first, as ByteOrder 1 sends them; the neighbour words are the inverse of the primary words), the monitor's own decode of the payload phase on the pads, and the flash record's `data_in`. The flash record is the first unused PAGE PROGRAM record at that address, and the monitor frame is the one at the same position (CHK-SPI-LEDGER lines the two lists up). The flash model must accept the frame. A controller that drops, reorders or corrupts a payload byte, or sends the bytes of a word in the opposite order, fails; so does a program with no matching record. The quad page program 0x32 of the lane leg is graded the same way, with the wire decode at x4 (lanes 3:0, the least significant bit of each nibble on `sd[0]`, most significant bit first; OpenTitan SPI_HOST theory of operation, "Input and Output Byte Ordering"). A run with `+spi1_directed` stages no seeded payload and registers no expectation, so the scoreboard does not run. Evidence: one `SPI-SB TX` line per program with the expected, wire and device bytes, and `CHK-SPI-SB-TX PASS seed=<n> frames=3 bytes=<n>`.
|CHK-SPI-SB-RX
|For each lane read (0x3B, 0x6B, 0xBB, 0xEB), the SPI data scoreboard compares the seeded words the leaf preloaded (least significant byte first, as ByteOrder 1 packs them), the flash record's `data_out`, and the monitor's decode of the data phase on lanes 1:0 (x2) or 3:0 (x4), the least significant bit of each pair or nibble on `sd[0]` (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, "SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment"; OpenTitan SPI_HOST theory of operation, "Input and Output Byte Ordering"). The flash record is the first unused record with that opcode; each lane opcode runs once. The primary READ (0x03) after the primary program is graded the same way at x1: the seeded program words against `data_out` and the monitor's decode of lane 1; it is the first READ record of the run. A flash model that drives the wrong lane or reverses a pair or nibble, and a host that reads at another width, fail. All three copies are taken before the host samples the lanes, so a host that samples the wrong lane or reverses a pair or nibble does not fail this check; its RXDATA words differ from the seeded words and CHK-SPI-LANE-READ fails (CHK-READ for the primary READ). Evidence: one `SPI-SB RX` line per read and `CHK-SPI-SB-RX PASS seed=<n> frames=5 bytes=<n>`.
|CHK-SPI-LANE-READ
|Firmware: each dual or quad read returns, word by word, the seeded words that the parameter block names for its address, and `ERROR_STATUS` stays 0. The firmware fills its buffer with the inverse of each expected word first, so a read that writes nothing fails. This is the RXDATA side of the read: the words the host delivered. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-LANE-READ PASS: op=<op> width=x<w> words=<k> bytes=<b>` per read.
|CHK-SPI-LANE-PROGRAM
|Firmware: after WREN and the quad page program 0x32, a standard READ of the range returns the seeded words, and `ERROR_STATUS` stays 0. A host that drops, reorders or corrupts a nibble of the x4 payload fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-LANE-PROGRAM PASS: op=0x32 width=x4 words=<k> bytes=<b>`.
|CHK-SPI-LANE-FW
|The firmware console holds exactly one CHK-SPI-LANE-READ or CHK-SPI-LANE-PROGRAM PASS line per lane entry, in the drawn order, with its opcode, width and word count. A missing, extra or reordered line fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-LANE-FW PASS seed=<n> entries=5 order=<opcodes>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0. The flash BFM logs no error (`SPI-BFM FAIL`), and `flash.stop()` raises no exception that the BFM protocol engine kept.

*Scope*: the transaction-order golden requires the BFM-visible opcode sequence
(JEDEC, WREN, RDSR, WRDI, program/read, FAST_READ, neighbour program/read, erase,
primary and neighbour read, post-erase status, the ignored write-protect program,
RDSR2, and the recovery READ ID), then the opcodes of the lane leg in the drawn order. The three
`ERROR_STATUS` injections put nothing on the bus: the host disables its core the cycle the error
latches. The scoreboard grades the primary READ at x1 (CHK-SPI-SB-RX); the other standard-mode reads of
this leaf are graded in firmware (CHK-READ, CHK-FAST-READ) only. The flash model has no dual program command, so x2 TX data is graded in
<<sep_spi_pad_speed_dir_matrix_test,entry 4.7>> and not here.

'''''

[[sep_dma_basic_test]]
===== 4.6 `sep_dma_basic_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, firmware `dma_basic_test`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_dma_basic_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address)

*Objective*: Secure-DMA control and copy breadth over the CPU LSU: documented reset values, the configuration lock reading locked while the engine is observed busy, the range-valid gate and its rw0c lock each with a positive control, FIXED/INCR/WRAP against a per-mode golden, 1/2/4-byte widths, `STATUS.done` RW1C, one invalid-opcode error with recovery, the address, address-space-id and transfer-width refusals, the inverted-range refusal, a copy that leaves SRAM for the instruction memory and back, a DMA-issued host-path command-integrity inject, and a fabric DECERR dest that raises `host_path_err` with the pin low. The mode and width walk is directed.

*Test Coverage*: Secure-DMA locks, address modes, widths, errors, and host-path integrity.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read DMA config registers at documented reset, then prove CFG_REGWEN lock while BUSY.
. While the range registers are still writable: `RANGE_VALID=0` raises `range_valid_error`, and an enabled range whose limit sits below its base raises exclusive `base_limit_error` with `STATUS.done` clear. Then lock `RANGE_REGWEN`.
. Walk INCR / FIXED / WRAP × 1B/2B/4B; consume the seed-patched SCENARIO line.
. Provoke one invalid opcode, then four exclusive address-alignment refusals; a clean copy after each error class proves the engine recovers.
. With `dma_host_intg_inject_i` high, one legal SRAM copy raises exclusive `host_path_err` and aggregator bit 41. `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` returns STATUS and bit 41 to 0. A legal copy with the pin low then matches the source snapshot.
. With the pin low, one copy whose destination is the first word past the DMA CSR window raises exclusive `host_path_err` from the fabric DECERR. CLEAR and a legal recovery copy follow.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET
|The DMA configuration registers read their documented reset values.
|CHK-CFG-REGWEN
|The model check in this entry: the configuration lock reads locked while the engine is busy — with busy actually observed, failing loudly if the copy finished too fast — and the *same* write is rejected while locked and lands while unlocked.
|CHK-RANGE-REGWEN
|The range-valid gate produces the documented error, an enabled range whose limit sits below its base raises `base_limit_error` *exclusively* and sets no `STATUS.done`, and the range base register rejects a write once locked. A positive control precedes the lock — a non-reset value is written and proven to land first — so "unchanged after the lock" cannot pass identically for a read-only register or a missing decode.
|CHK-COPY-MODE
|Increment, fixed-source, fixed-destination and wrap addressing each produce their expected image against an independent local snapshot of the source fill, with a sentinel neighbour check per iteration.
|CHK-WIDTH
|Each transfer-width encoding is written *and read back*, and the copy is byte-exact. Without the read-back the three iterations are indistinguishable — 16 bytes is a whole multiple of 1, 2 and 4, so a register stuck at its reset produces identical output.
|CHK-DONE-RW1C
|The done bit is observed set and then cleared to zero.
|CHK-ERR-OPCODE
|An invalid opcode raises exactly the opcode error — asserted *exclusively*, so no other error bit may be set — with the done bit required clear, then cleared by write-one-to-clear. The recovery copy after the error matches the independent source snapshot.
|CHK-ERR-ADDR
|The address validation the engine applies before it moves a byte. Four refused descriptors, each asserting its `ERROR_CODE` bit *exclusively*: a 4-byte transfer whose source is not 4-byte aligned raises `src_addr_error`, the same on the destination raises `dst_addr_error`, a 2-byte transfer on an odd source raises `src_addr_error`, and a 2-byte transfer on an odd destination raises `dst_addr_error`. None may set `STATUS.done`, and a clean copy after the four proves the engine recovers. The inverted-range leg sits in `CHK-RANGE-REGWEN`, which must run before the one-way range lock closes.
|CHK-ERR-ASID
|An address-space id outside the three encodings the IP register specification enumerates (`OT_ADDR` 0x7, `SYS_ADDR` 0x9, `SOC_ADDR` 0xa) is refused. Walked on the source field and on the destination field separately, each asserting `asid_error` *exclusively* with `STATUS.done` clear, then cleared by write-one-to-clear, then a clean copy at the legal id. The legal set is a DV-owned table in `fw/drivers/sep_dma.h`, since the register description carries only the reset value.
|CHK-ERR-SIZE
|A transfer width outside the 1/2/4-byte encodings raises `size_error` *exclusively* with `STATUS.done` clear, then clears, then a clean copy at a legal width. Distinct from `CHK-ERR-ADDR`, where the width is legal and the address is not: this is the width decode itself refusing.
|CHK-ICCM
|A copy that leaves SRAM. Every other transfer in this entry is SRAM to SRAM, so an engine that cannot reach the instruction memory passes all of them. The data goes SRAM to ICCM and back to a second SRAM region, and the returned words are compared against the source over a sentinel pre-fill, with the count of words that changed required to be non-zero so a transfer that moved nothing fails. The round trip is used because the core takes a store access fault on a direct write to ICCM, so the processor cannot pre-fill or read back that memory; only the DMA touches it, which is what the checker is about.
|CHK-HOSTINTG
|A DMA-issued host command under `dma_host_intg_inject_i` raises exactly `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS.host_path_err`. Control: with the pin already high and no DMA command issued, `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` still reads 0 before GO, so the pin alone does not set the latch. Aggregator bit 41 is 1 and bit 40 stays 0. `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` returns STATUS and bit 41 to 0. A legal copy with the pin low then matches the independent source snapshot. Each leg also requires the transfer to terminate, so a wedged engine cannot pass on a latch raised from its first command.
|CHK-HOSTINTG-PIC
|While the injected DMA command runs, the aggregated interrupt vector shows bit 41 (host path) at 1 and bit 40 (register path) at 0. An X or Z on either bit fails. Anchor: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`. Evidence: `CHK-HOSTINTG-PIC PASS: sep_internal_interrupts[41]=1 exclusive (vec=0x<v>)`.
|CHK-HOSTINTG-CLR
|After the firmware writes `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR`, bit 41 of the aggregated interrupt vector reads a known 0 within the bounded poll. Anchor: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`. Evidence: `CHK-HOSTINTG-CLR PASS: sep_internal_interrupts[41] resolved 0 after DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR`.
|CHK-HOSTFABRIC
|A DMA transfer whose destination is the first word past the DMA CSR window, with the inject pin low, raises exactly `host_path_err`. `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` returns STATUS to 0. The CLEAR register is a write-only single-pulse field that always reads 0, so its read value is not graded. A legal copy then matches the independent source snapshot.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Mode and width cells walk every seed; src/dst/length/fill come from the run seed.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: `CHK-HOSTINTG-ARM`, `CHK-HOSTINTG-RELEASE` and `CHK-HOSTFABRIC-ARM` are firmware handshake markers: the host drives `dma_host_intg_inject_i` high from the first to the second and requires it low at the third. `CHK-HOSTINTG-PIC` (aggregator bit 41 set, bit 40 clear) and `CHK-HOSTINTG-CLR` (bit 41 back to 0 after the clear) are the host-side records of CHK-HOSTINTG.

*Not claimed*: lockstep punch-through. The register path (`reg_path_err`) is not
exercised by this leaf at all; the unmapped-window term is proven by
<<sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test>>. Persistence across a CPU-only reset, and
the rule that a fault arriving in the clear cycle still latches — both stated in
the register description — are unproven here.

'''''

[[sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma]]
==== 4. SPI and DMA, Phase 3

This section holds the Phase 3 entries of the SPI and DMA area. Entry 4.10
extends an open test. Every entry names the open test it builds on and states
only the cells that the open test does not grade. The contracts that the
specification leaves open are in <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>.
A check that needs a value that the specification does not state is blocked and is marked `Blocked: <value>`
(<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). A blocked check does not run and is not counted as reached.
Every contract below comes from the specification and the register description.
No contract comes from the RTL.

*Sources*. Short names used in this section:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Short name |Path
|`spi.adoc`, `dma.adoc`, `cpu.adoc`, `fabric.adoc`, `interrupts.adoc`, `reset_controller.adoc`, `rom.adoc`, `lifecycle_controller.adoc`, `clk_rst.adoc`, `port_table.adoc`
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/<name>`
|`memory_map.adoc`
|`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`
|`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`
|`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`
|`spi_controller.adoc`
|`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/spi_controller/regs/gen/adoc/spi_controller.adoc`
|`secure_dma.adoc`
|`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/secure_dma/regs/gen/adoc/secure_dma.adoc`
|`secure_dma.rdl`
|`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/secure_dma/regs/secure_dma.rdl`
|`dma.hjson`
|`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/dma/data/dma.hjson`
|`spi_host.hjson`
|`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/spi_host/data/spi_host.hjson`
|`spi_controller.rdl`
|`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/spi_controller/regs/spi_controller.rdl`
|`sep-programming.adoc`
|`doc/programmer/src/sep-programming.adoc`
|`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|`hw/sys/sep/dv/docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|`smu-sep.adoc`
|`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`
|OpenTitan SPI_HOST theory of operation
|https://opentitan.org/book/hw/ip/spi_host/doc/theory_of_operation.html (the vendor tree holds only `spi_host.hjson`)
|===

A citation names the file and the section or register of that file.

*Rules for every entry in this section*. An entry states only what differs.

* Seed. Every random value, and every per-seed choice of a leg, an order or an
enable value, is a pure function of the seed. No stimulus depends on a seed
index. `--stage sim --seed N` replays the leaf. The generator is not a CSPRNG.
All drawn data is test data. No document quotes a per-seed value as evidence.
* Log shape. Each check logs `CHK-<NAME> PASS|FAIL seed=<n> <values>`. The leaf
ends with `RESULT <test> seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>`, where k counts the checks
that run. PASS needs positive evidence in `results.xml`. A clean simulator exit
is not evidence. A leg whose control shows no cause logs `CTRL-MISSING` and
fails. An `OBS-` line records a value and grades nothing. A check marked
Blocked does not run, does not count in k and is not a reached check.
* Probes and injects. Each entry names the probes it reads. A probe is
"approved" when `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` lists it or `tb_top.sv` has it. A probe
outside the closed set of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. No entry adds an error inject
or a top-port drive. A check is blocked only when it needs a PROPOSED item or a
value that the specification does not state (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>).

[cols="3,2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Probe |Status |What it observes
|Register frontdoor over `s_axi` (CPU-LSU splice)
|approved (`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, CPU-LSU AXI)
|Every SPI and DMA register read and write with the response code, and the content of SEP SRAM at `0x1000_0000`.
|SPI pad ports `spi_sck_o`, `spi_cs_n_o`, `spi_mosi_o` and the responder input `spi_miso_i`
|approved (`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, SPI host pads; `tb_top.sv`)
|The sck edges, the chip select and `sd[0]`. Without `+sep_spi_four_lane`, `spi_miso_i` is lane 1 of the response and lanes 0, 2 and 3 read 0. With no responder the input stays at its testbench default.
|SPI lane taps `spi_sd_o[3:0]`, `spi_sd_oe_o[3:0]`, `spi_cs_oe_o`, `spi_sck_oe_o` and `spi_sd_rsp_o[3:0]`; the SEP top-port drive `spi_sd_i[3:0]`
|approved (`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, SPI host pads; `tb_top.sv`)
|The four data lanes the host drives, their output enables, the chip-select and clock output enables, and the four lanes the DUT receives. With `+sep_spi_four_lane` all four response lanes follow `spi_sd_i`; a leaf that drives `spi_sd_i` fails at start without the plusarg. The responder is `OcahSpiFlash` (bound to four lanes in `quad_io` mode) or the leaf-side lane responder `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_lane_responder.py`, which drives the seeded RX bytes of each frame on `spi_sd_i` and drives nothing else.
|`sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`
|approved (`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, IRQ aggregate; `tb_top.sv`)
|PIC source n at index n-1: sources 9, 10 and 11 (DMA) and 14 (SPI).
|`spi_tx_qd_probe_o`, `spi_lsio_trigger_probe_o`, `dma_busy_probe_o`
|approved (`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, SPI-to-DMA pacing probes)
|The TX queue depth, the SPI trigger at the DMA input and `STATUS.BUSY` of the DMA, on each clock.
|`dbg_sep_reset_n_o`
|approved (`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, SEP reset probes)
|The release of `sep_reset_n` after `rst_ni`.
|`dma_axi_req_probe_o`
|observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list
|The DMA master AR, AW, W and B channel fields after the alias remap: address, AxLEN, write strobes and handshakes. Read-only.
|`dma_status_probe_o`
|observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list
|The level of `STATUS.DONE`, `CHUNK_DONE`, `ERROR`, `ABORTED` and `SHA2_DIGEST_VALID` of the DMA, on each clock. Read-only.
|===

The lane map, the output enables and their rules come from `smu-sep.adoc` ("SEP SPI Host Pin
Assignment"). No check reads a pad struct member other than through an approved port or tap.

* Control window. Each "never" check names a window in which a healthy design
drives the signal, and the leaf logs the value of that window.
* Reset release. After every `rst_ni` pulse the leaf waits, with a bound, for the
`sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (`reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset
Controller) before the first register access.
* One fault per injection, from a clean state. SPI clean state: write-one-to-clear
on ERROR_STATUS and INTR_STATE.ERROR, CSID=0, then SW_RST with both FIFOs empty
before release. DMA clean state: write one to STATUS.ERROR, STATUS.ABORTED,
STATUS.DONE and STATUS.CHUNK_DONE. A write of one to STATUS.ERROR clears
ERROR_CODE (`dma.hjson`, ERROR_CODE). INTR_STATE is read-only
(`secure_dma.adoc`, INTR_STATE). A write of 1 to the matching STATUS bit releases a
status interrupt ("Since interrupts are implemented as status bits, they are cleared
by writing a '1' to the corresponding bit in the STATUS register", OpenTitan DMA
`programmers_guide.md:39`; `dma.adoc:131-132`). The specification states no write that
releases a bit forced by INTR_TEST (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>),
so a trial that grades INTR_STATE or an interrupt line starts from a cold reset.
* DMA codes. OPCODE 0 is COPY, and 1, 2 and 3 select SHA-256, SHA-384 and
SHA-512. TRANSFER_WIDTH 0, 1 and 2 select 1, 2 and 4 bytes per transaction, and 2
is the reset value (`sep-programming.adoc`, DMA; `dma.hjson`, OPCODE,
TRANSFER_WIDTH). `secure_dma.adoc` has no table for these encodings. Where
`secure_dma.adoc` or `secure_dma.rdl` states the access type of a register, it
wins over `dma.hjson`; `dma.hjson` gives the text that they leave out.
A plain copy uses OPCODE 0 and width 2 unless the entry walks the widths.
The tests use 4-aligned addresses and sizes that are multiples of 4. An
address is aligned to the transfer width (`secure_dma.adoc`, SRC_ADDR_LO,
DST_ADDR_LO).
Source and destination footprints, guard words included, are disjoint and lie
inside BASE to LIMIT.
* Sentinel. Every byte of the sentinel word of a memory-to-memory transfer is
one byte value s that no source byte of that transfer takes: drawn source data
excludes s, and for a published hash vector s is a value that the vector does
not hold. So every byte that the transfer writes differs from the sentinel.
Drawn source words are unique within a transfer. The handshake entry draws its
sentinel from its reference run (<<sep_spi_dma_handshake_rand_test,entry 4.15>>).
* DMA transaction size. The specification states single-beat DMA transactions
(`fabric.adoc`, Top Level AXI4) and the width of each transaction
(`secure_dma.adoc`, TRANSFER_WIDTH). It does not state the AxSIZE that the
TL-UL to AXI4 path gives a narrow transaction, and it does not state the address bits [1:0] of a 1-byte or 2-byte transaction. At width encodings 0 and 1 the address checks grade AxADDR[31:2] and the WSTRB byte lanes, and log AxADDR[1:0]. The beat checks (AxLEN 0, beat
count, write byte count from WSTRB, read address step) read
the observation tap `dma_axi_req_probe_o`. AxSIZE is not
graded. The destination image is graded through the frontdoor.
* DMA chunks. A transfer of several chunks without hardware handshake stops after
each non-final chunk and continues only after the next CONTROL.GO ("A start is a
write of `CONTROL.GO` while `STATUS.BUSY` is clear: the first chunk of a transfer,
with `INITIAL_TRANSFER` set, or a later chunk, with `GO` alone.", `dma.adoc:79-81`).
After a chunk end with DONE=0 and the engine idle, the leaf writes GO again with
INITIAL_TRANSFER=0. The number of GO writes is stimulus and is not graded; the stops
and the destination progress at each later GO are graded by CHK-DMA-GO-CHUNKS
(<<sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test,entry 4.12>>). Every wait for DONE has a bound,
and a timeout fails with `FAIL-DMA-TIMEOUT`.
* FIFO depth. The SEP SPI host is the unmodified OpenTitan SPI Host
(`sep-programming.adoc:784`), built with TxDepth 72, RxDepth 64 and CmdDepth 4
(`spi_host.hjson:93-107`). The leaf finds the depths at run time from STATUS to size
stimulus and grades them against these values (CHK-SPI-DEPTH-TX, CHK-SPI-DEPTH-RX and
CHK-SPI-DEPTH-CMD in <<sep_spi_fifo_stall_watermark_rand_test,entry 4.9>>).
* Time. Every time relation is in core clocks, sck half-cycles or ratios. No
check uses an absolute frequency.
* Lane data. Lane n is `sd[n]` with output enable `sd_oe[n]`, and the pad input of lane n returns as `sd[n]`; in standard mode DATA[0] is the host output and DATA[1] the host input; the host drives a data lane only while chip select is asserted (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, "SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment"). Each byte moves most significant bit first; in x2 and x4 the least significant bit of each pair or nibble is on `sd[0]`; with `ByteOrder = 1` the first byte on the wire is TXDATA[7:0] and the first byte received lands in RXDATA[7:0]; at the end of a TX segment the unused bytes of its last TXDATA word are dropped, and each RX segment starts word-aligned in RXDATA (OpenTitan SPI_HOST theory of operation, "Input and Output Byte Ordering", "Command Length and Alignment in DATA"). A leaf that grades lane data gives each TX
segment of a chain its own TXDATA words. The byte order inside a word is an open
question (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>, SPI byte order inside a word);
the goldens use `ByteOrder = 1`, and a `STATUS.BYTEORDER` readback is never the golden.
<<sep-spi-lane-coverage,Entry 4.20>> states what is graded at each width.
* Disable vector. The vector stays at the harness value. SYS_DIS bit 0 is never
randomized, because it filters inbound traffic (`lifecycle_controller.adoc`,
Disable Vector Format).
* Tool and run mode. A `no_cpu` leaf uses target `lsu_stub_all_live`, joins the
testlist groups `cpu_stub` and `all`, and lives in `spi.toml` (SPI, the
SPI-to-DMA handshake included) or `system.toml` (DMA). Tool "both" means Verilator and VCS. Tool "VCS" means a
`tools = ["vcs"]` pin, because the leaf reads state after reset. A leg that
reads reset state on a four-state simulator is a VCS-only leg inside a
both-tools leaf. A leaf that asserts `rst_ni` runs with real fuse sense (no
`+skip_fuse_sense`).

[[sep_spi_pad_speed_dir_matrix_test]]
===== 4.7 `sep_spi_pad_speed_dir_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+sep_spi_four_lane`, no `rst_ni` pulse, 4 seeds; tool: both (Verilator and VCS); testlist groups `cpu_stub` and `all`, file `spi.toml`, one testlist entry. The seed draws the long dummy leg with a weight of 1 in 4. The leg takes 2,097,152 core clocks, so a seed that draws it runs longer.

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_pad_speed_dir_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` for the register frontdoor over `s_axi` (CPU-LSU splice, approved), with the response code of every access. A test-side sampler reads the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` on each core clock (approved). It counts one sck cycle at each leading edge, an edge that leaves the idle level, while chip select is low. The lane responder `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_lane_responder.py` drives the seeded RX bytes of each graded frame on `spi_sd_i[3:0]` (approved top-port drive), which the DUT receives with `+sep_spi_four_lane`. `OcahSpiMonitor` (`ocah_spi_vip`, attached through `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_wire_monitor.py` with the output-enable taps) records the lanes the DUT drives, their output enables and the lanes the DUT receives at each sample edge, and `SepSpiScoreboard` (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`, no flash model) decodes them. No checker needs a proposed probe. No inject. No check is blocked.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed (RAND-REP). Dimensions: the order of the 16 cells; the LEN class of each legal cell and of each dummy segment, `len0` (LEN 0), `sub_word` (LEN 1), `word_multiple` (LEN 3, 15, 31, or 0x3FF for a dummy segment) or `non_multiple` (LEN 4, 16, 32, or a value up to 63 with LEN+1 not a multiple of 4), weighted toward `len0` and `word_multiple`, and the value inside the class; the CLKDIV class of each cell, `zero` (0), `small` (1 to 3) or `mid` (4 to 7), and the value inside the class; the chain shapes (2 to 6 segments, the speed and direction of each segment); the long dummy leg (weight 1 in 4); the TX bytes and the RX bytes of every data segment; the speed of the one-width chain, the address width (x2 or x4), dummy length and data direction of the lane chain. One constraint holds: each of DIRECTION 1 and 3 has at least one cell in `word_multiple`, so that every seed grades a whole-word Rx count in both directions (CHK-SPI-RXCNT). Every seed walks all 16 cells, one short register-level segment each. `reseed = 4` samples other orders, classes, values inside a class, chains, long dummy draws and data. The LEN class, CLKDIV class and CSAAT=1 cells depend on the seed, so `sep_spi_seg_rand_cg` closes on a merge of regressions (<<sep-fcov-rand,SEP_FCOV>>). No single regression is claimed to fill them.

*Objective*: The SPI host command contract holds (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/spi_controller/regs/gen/adoc/spi_controller.adoc`, COMMAND and ERROR_STATUS registers; `hw/sys/sep/doc/spi.adoc`, Features; later cites in this entry use the file names only). SPEED 0, 1 and 2 move LEN+1 bytes at one bit per data line per sck cycle on 1, 2 and 4 data lines, so a data segment takes (LEN+1)*8/lanes sck cycles and a dummy segment (DIRECTION 0) takes LEN+1 sck cycles. The host runs at single transfer rate and has no x8 lane group, so a DDR or x8 host gives another count. CSAAT joins segments under one chip-select assertion, and each segment of a chain has its own speed, direction and length. SPEED 3, and DIRECTION 3 at SPEED 1 or 2, raise ERROR_STATUS.CMDINVAL. Lane n is `sd[n]` with output enable `sd_oe[n]`, and the pad input of lane n returns as `sd[n]`; in standard mode DATA[0] is the host output and DATA[1] the host input; the host drives a data lane only while chip select is asserted (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, "SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment"). Each byte moves most significant bit first; in x2 and x4 the least significant bit of each pair or nibble is on `sd[0]`; with `ByteOrder = 1` the first byte on the wire is TXDATA[7:0] and the first byte received lands in RXDATA[7:0]; at the end of a TX segment the unused bytes of its last TXDATA word are dropped, and each RX segment starts word-aligned in RXDATA (OpenTitan SPI_HOST theory of operation, "Input and Output Byte Ordering", "Command Length and Alignment in DATA"). Data lane output enables per segment: an x1 TX or x1 bidirectional segment drives lane 0 (0b0001; `smu-sep.adoc`: DATA[0] is the host output); an x2 TX segment drives lanes 1:0 (0b0011) and an x4 TX segment lanes 3:0 (0b1111); an x2 or x4 RX segment drives no lane (0b0000; derived from `smu-sep.adoc`: the pad input is enabled only while the host does not drive the pad). No source states the output enables of an x1 RX-only segment or of a dummy segment: OpenTitan "SPI Protocol Basics" says that in standard mode SD[0] is always an output, and OpenTitan "Command Interface" says that in a dummy segment neither side transmits, but neither text names the output enable. The leaf logs these two segment classes as `OBS-SPI-OE-X1RX` and `OBS-SPI-OE-DUMMY` and grades them not (<<sep-spi-lane-coverage,entry 4.20>>, Open items).

*Test Coverage*: The 10 legal SPEED x DIRECTION cells as single segments with CSAAT=0, at LEN classes 0, 1, 3, 4, 15, 16, 31, 32 and a value up to 63, and LEN 0x3FF for dummy segments. The Rx word count at every speed and in every LEN class, a partial last word included (CHK-SPI-RXCNT). A dummy segment with TXQD and RXQD both at least 1. Chains of 2 to 6 legal segments in which speed and direction change while chip select stays low: on every seed one chain whose data segments share one width, one whose data segments change width, and a lane chain (an x1 command byte, a 3-byte address at x2 or x4, a dummy segment and x4 data) in one chip-select window. The data of every legal cell, of the FIFO-leg dummy segment and of every chain segment, at x1, x2 and x4 in both directions, graded byte for byte against the seeded stimulus. The data lane output-enable pattern of every segment of those frames (x1 TX, x1 RX, x1 bidirectional, x2 TX, x2 RX, x4 TX, x4 RX, dummy). A long dummy segment (LEN 0xFFFFF at CLKDIV 0) on a seed that draws it. The 6 illegal cells (SPEED 3 with DIRECTION 0 to 3, and DIRECTION 3 at SPEED 1 and 2). The open tests `sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test` and `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` drive SPEED 0 for data and grade SPEED 3 with DIRECTION 2 at the ERROR_STATUS level. That cell runs here as a control: it logs `CTL-SPI-ILLEGAL LOG`, prints no CHK-SPI-ILLEGAL line and fills no bin. This entry adds the cycle counts at SPEED 1 and 2, dummy segments, chains and the five other illegal cells. INTR_STATE.ERROR for a real CMDINVAL is graded in <<sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test_leg,entry 4.10>>. Functional coverage: `sep_spi_command_segment_cg` (`cp_speed_dir_legal`, `cp_chain_change`, `cp_fifo_effect`), `sep_spi_seg_rand_cg` (graded on a merge of regressions: `cp_len_x_kind`, `cp_csaat_x_dir`, `cp_clkdiv_x_speed`), `sep_spi_illegal_access_cg` (`cp_illegal_speed_dir`), `sep_spi_fifo_status_cg` (`cp_cmd_accept`), `sep_spi_pad_rand_cg` (graded on a merge of regressions: `cp_mode_x_dir`, `cp_clkdiv_class`), `sep_spi_lane_data_cg` (cells `pad_speed_dir_matrix` x x1, x2, x4 x TX, RX) and `sep_spi_lane_abort_chain_cg` (`cp_chain_width`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw the cell order, the LEN class and the CLKDIV class of each cell and the value inside each class, the chain shapes, the long dummy choice, the TX words and the responder stream from the seed. Log the plan first (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`), with the class of every cell, the CSAAT=1 directions of the chains and `long_dummy=<0 or 1>`. Every wait in the steps below is a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check that the step feeds. A segment is done when the sampler has shown chip select low and then high (for a chain with CSAAT=1, high after the last segment) and a later STATUS read shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0. Every wait for a segment below is this bounded wait, so a STATUS read taken before the command starts does not end it. Before every COMMAND write the test polls STATUS until READY=1 (bounded). The SW_RST helper writes CONTROL.SW_RST=1 with the other fields kept, polls STATUS until TXEMPTY=1 and RXEMPTY=1, then writes CONTROL.SW_RST=0. The SPI clean state writes 1 to every bit of ERROR_STATUS and to INTR_STATE.ERROR, writes CSID=0, and runs the SW_RST helper.
. Start from the harness reset state. Write CONTROL with SPIEN=1, OUTPUT_EN=1, SW_RST=0 and both watermark fields at their reset values. Write CSID=0. Leave ERROR_ENABLE at its reset value 0x1F. Read ERROR_STATUS and INTR_STATE; both read 0.
. Single-segment leg: repeat steps 3 to 7 for each legal cell in the drawn order. Write CONFIGOPTS with CPOL=0, CPHA=0, FULLCYC=0, CSNLEAD, CSNTRAIL and CSNIDLE at 0 and the drawn CLKDIV, while ACTIVE=0 and chip select is high.
. Poll STATUS until READY=1. Write COMMAND with the cell's SPEED and DIRECTION, the drawn LEN and CSAAT=0. For DIRECTION 0 and 2, read STATUS before the COMMAND and require RXEMPTY=1 (control for CHK-SPI-RXCNT).
. Service the segment until it is done. Write the TXDATA words that the segment needs (LEN+1 bytes rounded up to words), each after a STATUS read that shows TXFULL=0. For DIRECTION 1 and 3, read one RXDATA word after each STATUS read that shows RXFULL=1, and count the words read in the segment.
. After the segment is done, read STATUS for RXQD. For DIRECTION 1 and 3, the total Rx word count is the words read in the segment plus RXQD. Read RXDATA until STATUS shows RXEMPTY=1, each read after a STATUS read that shows RXEMPTY=0; the words read equal RXQD. For DIRECTION 0 and 2, RXQD reads 0. The Rx word count is ceil((LEN+1)/4) for every LEN, a partial last word included, and CHK-SPI-RXCNT grades it.
. Compare the sck cycle count that the sampler took for the segment with the expected count. Read ERROR_STATUS; it reads 0. Grade the frame as in step 13. Run the SW_RST helper.
. Dummy and FIFO leg. Run the SW_RST helper. Write one TXDATA word with no COMMAND. Run a 4-byte Rx segment (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 1, CSAAT=0) and do not read RXDATA. After the segment is done, read STATUS; TXQD and RXQD are each at least 1 (control; else CTRL-MISSING). Log both values. Run a dummy segment (DIRECTION 0, a LEN of the drawn class of this segment, a drawn SPEED from 0 to 2, CSAAT=0). After the segment is done, read TXQD and RXQD again. Read RXDATA until RXEMPTY=1 and run the SW_RST helper.
. Chain leg. Control for the sampler: write two legal dummy commands (DIRECTION 0, a drawn LEN, CSAAT=0 on both) back to back, each after a STATUS read that shows READY=1, and wait until the sampler has shown chip select low and then high twice and a STATUS read shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0. Require that the sampler saw chip select rise between the two low windows, else CTRL-MISSING. Then, for each drawn chain, write CONFIGOPTS (drawn CLKDIV) while ACTIVE=0. Build the chain from 2 to 6 legal segments with at least one change of speed or of direction between neighbours. Each seed runs three chains: one whose data segments share one drawn speed, one whose data segments change speed, and the lane chain (SPEED 0 TX of 1 byte, a TX of 3 bytes at SPEED 1 or 2, a dummy segment of 1 to 7 cycles, and SPEED 2 data, TX or RX, of LEN+1 a multiple of 4). Data segments of the first two chains have LEN+1 a multiple of 4, so the word count does not depend on how a partial word packs across segments. Each TX segment has its own TXDATA words, the last word padded, because the host drops the unused bytes of a word at the end of a TX segment. Write each COMMAND after a STATUS read that shows READY=1, with CSAAT=1 on every segment except the last, which has CSAAT=0. The depth of the command queue is not claimed, so interleave the data with the COMMAND writes: between the COMMAND writes and while waiting for READY=1, write the TXDATA words of the Tx segments already written, in order, each after a STATUS read that shows TXFULL=0, and read RXDATA as in step 5 for the Rx segments already written. After the last COMMAND, write the remaining TXDATA words and read RXDATA as in step 5 until the chain is done (the sampler shows chip select high after the last segment, and STATUS shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0). Read ERROR_STATUS; it reads 0. Grade the frame as in step 13. Run the SW_RST helper.
. Long dummy leg, on a seed that draws it. Write CONFIGOPTS with CLKDIV 0 while ACTIVE=0. After a STATUS read that shows READY=1, write COMMAND with DIRECTION 0, SPEED 0, LEN 0xFFFFF and CSAAT=0. Wait until the segment is done and take every chip-select-low window after the COMMAND write and the sck cycle count of all of them. The wire monitor and the lane responder are stopped for this leg and restart after it.
. Illegal cells, as error injection. For each of the 6 illegal cells in the drawn order, run the SPI clean state, write CONTROL.SPIEN=0 (other fields kept) so that the core never runs the illegal command, and poll STATUS until READY=1. Control: write a legal dummy COMMAND (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 0, LEN 0); ERROR_STATUS reads 0, else CTRL-MISSING, so an error below does not come from SPIEN=0. Poll STATUS until READY=1. Write COMMAND with the cell's SPEED and DIRECTION, a drawn LEN and CSAAT=0, and push no TX data. Point: COMMAND write over the register bus. Kind: reserved SPEED encoding, or bidirectional at dual or quad. Expected response: ERROR_STATUS.CMDINVAL=1 and no other ERROR_STATUS bit (`spi_controller.adoc`, ERROR_STATUS). Poll ERROR_STATUS until CMDINVAL reads 1, then require the value 0x08. The cell SPEED 3 with DIRECTION 2 is a control: it logs `CTL-SPI-ILLEGAL LOG seed=<n> speed=3 dir=2 err_status=0x<v>`, a value other than 0x08 fails the leaf, and it prints no CHK-SPI-ILLEGAL line. The SW_RST of the next SPI clean state empties the command queue before SPIEN returns to 1.
. After each illegal cell, run the SPI clean state and poll STATUS until READY=1. Write one legal control command (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 0, a drawn LEN, CSAAT=0) and wait until the segment is done. Read ERROR_STATUS; it reads 0.
. Frame grade. Before the first COMMAND of a graded frame, take the scoreboard mark and give the lane responder the segment layout and the RX bytes of the frame. After the frame, register the segments with `expect_frame` and the RXDATA bytes the frame delivered. Slice the monitor beats of the frame per segment (data: (LEN+1)*8/lanes sample edges; dummy: LEN+1) and compare the data lane output enables of every beat with the pattern of that segment; for an x1 RX-only or a dummy segment, log the output enables seen (`OBS-SPI-OE-X1RX`, `OBS-SPI-OE-DUMMY`) and grade nothing. Print one CHK-SPI-LANE-DATA line per data segment and direction after the frame passed CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME, then write one `spi_lane_fcov` data record per width and direction graded (and one chain record per chain).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SPI-CELL
|Per legal cell, the sck cycle count on the pad while chip select is low equals (LEN+1)*8/lanes for DIRECTION 1, 2 and 3 and LEN+1 for DIRECTION 0, with lanes 1, 2 and 4 at SPEED 0, 1 and 2, and ERROR_STATUS reads 0 after the segment (`spi.adoc`, Features; `spi_controller.adoc`, COMMAND). A host with a DDR mode, an x8 lane group or the wrong lane count gives another count and fails. A legal cell that sets any ERROR_STATUS bit fails. Anchor: the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` (approved) and an ERROR_STATUS readback. Control for "a legal cell never sets CMDINVAL": the illegal cells of the same seed set it (CHK-SPI-ILLEGAL). Evidence: one line per legal cell, `CHK-SPI-CELL PASS seed=<n> speed=<s> dir=<d> len=<LEN> csaat=<c> clkdiv=<d> sck_cycles=<got> expect=<exp> err_status=0x00`.
|CHK-SPI-RXCNT
|For DIRECTION 1 and 3, the Rx word count equals ceil((LEN+1)/4) at every speed: "For RX (or Bidirectional) segments, if the last few bytes received do not fill an entire DATA word, the partial word will be zero-padded and inserted into the RX FIFO once the segment is completed." (OpenTitan SPI_HOST `theory_of_operation.md:485`, tenstorrent/tt-opentitan@d1ce369cb). The count comes from three readings that must agree: the RXDATA words read (in the segment and in the drain), RXQD with ACTIVE=0 at the segment end, and the words read in the segment plus that RXQD. The position of the pad byte in the last word depends on ByteOrder and is not graded. A host that returns more or fewer words fails. Anchor: STATUS and RXDATA readback over the frontdoor (approved). Control: a Tx-only or dummy segment that starts with RXEMPTY=1 leaves RXQD at 0. A seed with no DIRECTION 1 cell or no DIRECTION 3 cell logs CTRL-MISSING and fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-RXCNT PASS seed=<n> speed=<s> dir=<d> bytes=<b> words=<w> rxqd=<q> in_segment=<k> drained=<k> expect=<ceil((LEN+1)/4)> partial_word=<0 or 1> control_nonrx_rxqd=0`.
|CHK-SPI-DUMMY
|A dummy segment leaves TXQD and RXQD unchanged, because it moves no data (`spi_controller.adoc`, COMMAND.DIRECTION), and runs LEN+1 sck cycles. A host that pops a TX word or pushes an RX word for a dummy segment, or that runs another cycle count, fails. Anchor: STATUS readback with ACTIVE=0 before and after the segment. Control: both counters are at least 1 before the segment, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-DUMMY PASS seed=<n> txqd_before=<a> txqd_after=<a> rxqd_before=<b> rxqd_after=<b> speed=<s> len=<LEN> len_class=<k> sck_cycles=<got> expect=<LEN+1>`.
|CHK-SPI-CHAIN
|With CSAAT=1 on every segment but the last, chip select stays low for the whole chain, the sck cycle count of the chain equals the sum of the segment counts, and chip select is high when the chain is done (`spi_controller.adoc`, COMMAND.CSAAT). Each seed grades a chain whose data segments share one width and a chain whose data segments change width, else CTRL-MISSING, plus the lane chain; the data of every chain segment is graded at its own width by CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME. A host that raises chip select between segments shows a mid-chain rise and fails. Anchor: the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` (approved). Control for "never rises mid-chain": in the same seed the sampler sees chip select rise between the two back-to-back CSAAT=0 transactions of the control step and at the chain end, so the sampler is shown to see a rise; the chain has at least 2 segments and at least one change of speed or direction, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-CHAIN PASS seed=<n> segs=<k> sck_cycles_in_chain=<got> expect=<sum> cs_dropped_mid=0 cs_high_after=1 control_cs_rise_between_csaat0=1`.
|CHK-SPI-ILLEGAL
|Each of the five graded illegal cells (every illegal cell except SPEED 3 with DIRECTION 2, which is a control here) sets ERROR_STATUS.CMDINVAL and no other bit, and the control command after the SPI clean state reads ERROR_STATUS 0 (`spi_controller.adoc`, ERROR_STATUS; `COMMAND.SPEED`, `COMMAND.DIRECTION`). A host that accepts a reserved cell, or that stays in error after the clean state, fails. Anchor: ERROR_STATUS readback (approved frontdoor). Control for "a legal cell never sets CMDINVAL": the legal cells of the same seed read ERROR_STATUS 0. INTR_STATE.ERROR is graded in entry 4.10. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-ILLEGAL PASS seed=<n> speed=<s> dir=<d> err_status=0x08 control_err_status=0x00`.
|CHK-SPI-LONGDUMMY
|The long dummy segment (LEN 0xFFFFF, DIRECTION 0) gives LEN+1 = 1,048,576 sck cycles in exactly one chip-select-low window, with `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` on at every clock of the window. Every window after the COMMAND write counts, so a host that truncates the 20-bit LEN field gives a lower count and fails, a host that splits the segment into two windows fails, and a host that drops the chip-select or sck output enable in the window fails. Anchor: the pad ports (approved). The check runs only on a seed that draws the long dummy leg; on any other seed it logs `OBS-SPI-LONGDUMMY seed=<n> long_dummy=0 graded=0` and does not count in k. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-LONGDUMMY PASS seed=<n> cycles=1048576 expect=1048576 windows=1 expect_windows=1`.
|CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME
