|Each graded frame (every legal cell, the dummy segment of the FIFO leg and every chain) has exactly the sample edges of its segments, and the SPI data scoreboard (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`, `expect_frame`) grades it segment by segment at the width of each segment: the wire decode of a TX segment equals its seeded bytes; the wire decode of an RX segment equals the seeded bytes the lane responder drove; and the bytes read from RXDATA, unpacked with `ByteOrder = 1`, equal them. A bidirectional segment is graded as TX and as RX on the same sample edges. x1 TX decodes `sd[0]`, x1 RX decodes `sd[1]`, and x2 and x4 decode lanes 1:0 and 3:0 with the least significant bit of each pair or nibble on `sd[0]` (`smu-sep.adoc`, "SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment"; OpenTitan SPI_HOST theory of operation, "Input and Output Byte Ordering"). A host that swaps lanes, reverses the bit order, sends the bytes of a word in the other order, drops or adds a byte, or samples the wrong lane fails. Anchor: the lane taps `spi_sd_o` and `spi_sd_rsp_o` (the lanes the DUT receives) through `OcahSpiMonitor`, and RXDATA readback over the frontdoor (approved). A frame with no expectation, or an expectation with no frame, fails. Evidence: one `SPI-SB FRAME` line per frame and `CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> bytes=<n>`.
|CHK-SPI-LANE-DATA
|One line per data segment and direction of a frame that passed CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME, with its width and byte count: the TX line compares the monitor decode, and the RX line the RXDATA bytes, with the seeded bytes. Control: x1, x2 and x4 are each graded in both directions in the seed, else the leaf fails (`CTL-SPI-LANES LOG seed=<n> data_graded=<cells> oe_rows=<rows>`). Evidence: `CHK-SPI-LANE-DATA PASS seed=<n> ... width=x<w> dir=<tx or rx> bytes=<b>`.
|CHK-SPI-OE-SEG
|In each graded frame, `spi_sd_oe_o` at every sample edge of a segment equals the pattern of that segment on the lanes the segment uses: lanes 1:0 = 0b01 for x1 TX and x1 bidirectional, lanes 1:0 = 0b11 for x2 TX and 0b00 for x2 RX, and lanes 3:0 = 0b1111 for x4 TX and 0b0000 for x4 RX (Objective). No source states lanes 3:2 at x1 or x2; the leaf logs them (`OBS-SPI-OE-UNUSED seed=<n> <frame> seg=<i> row=<row> lanes_3_2=<values>`) and does not grade them. An x1 RX-only or a dummy segment has no stated pattern: the leaf logs its output enables (`OBS-SPI-OE-X1RX LOG`, `OBS-SPI-OE-DUMMY LOG`) and marks the segment `/obs` in the evidence line. The monitor's `oe_lanes` check accepts any of the four legal patterns, so it cannot catch a wrong width; this check can. An X or Z on an output enable fails. A host that drives lane 1 in an x1 TX or bidirectional segment, keeps a used lane driven in an x2 or x4 RX segment, or leaves a used lane undriven in a TX segment fails. Anchor: the output-enable tap `spi_sd_oe_o` through `OcahSpiMonitor` (approved). Control: every graded pattern row (x1 TX, x1 bidirectional, x2 TX, x2 RX, x4 TX, x4 RX) is graded in the seed, else the leaf fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-OE-SEG PASS seed=<n> <frame> beats=<k> segments=<i>:<row>:<seen>/exp<pattern>/lanes<used>,...`, with `/obs` in place of `/exp<pattern>` for an x1 RX-only or a dummy segment.
|CHK-SPI-WIRE
|The wire and output-enable checks of `OcahSpiMonitor` (`oe_idle`, `oe_lanes`, `oe_ctrl`, the edge and X/Z rules on every enabled lane) over every frame of the leaf in mode 0, as in <<sep_spi_pad_timing_cfg_rand_test,entry 4.8>>. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-WIRE PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> per_mode=00:<frames> wire_errors=0`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The position of the zero pad bytes in the last RXDATA word of an RX segment is not graded, because it depends on ByteOrder: the frame grade compares the bytes of the segment only, and CHK-SPI-RXCNT grades the word count. The long dummy leg grades no data and no output enable. CONFIGOPTS stays at CPOL=0, CPHA=0 and FULLCYC=0; the pad timing of CONFIGOPTS is graded in <<sep_spi_pad_timing_cfg_rand_test,entry 4.8>>. INTR_STATE.ERROR for a real CMDINVAL is graded in entry 4.10. The open tests `sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test` and `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` own the cell SPEED 3 with DIRECTION 2 at the ERROR_STATUS level; here it is a control that logs `CTL-SPI-ILLEGAL LOG` and is not graded.

'''''

[[sep_spi_pad_timing_cfg_rand_test]]
===== 4.8 `sep_spi_pad_timing_cfg_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+sep_spi_four_lane`, no `rst_ni` pulse, 3 seeds; tool: both (Verilator and VCS); testlist groups `cpu_stub` and `all`, file `spi.toml`. Every run grades two mode cells.

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_pad_timing_cfg_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` for the register frontdoor over `s_axi` (CPU-LSU splice, approved). A test-side sampler reads the pad ports `spi_sck_o`, `spi_cs_n_o` and `spi_mosi_o` on each core clock (approved). `spi_mosi_o` is data line `sd[0]`. The lane responder `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_lane_responder.py` drives the seeded RX bytes of each Rx segment on `spi_sd_i[3:0]` in the mode of the cell (approved top-port drive; `+sep_spi_four_lane`). The shared SPI monitor `OcahSpiMonitor` (`ocah_spi_vip`, attached through `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_wire_monitor.py`) reads `spi_cs_n_o`, `spi_sck_o`, the lane taps and the output-enable taps in the CPOL and CPHA of each mode cell, and `SepSpiScoreboard` (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`, one per mode cell, no flash model) grades the data of each frame; the leaf sets the monitor mode before the CONFIGOPTS writes of the mode cell. No proposed probe. No inject. No check is blocked.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed (RAND-REP). Every seed grades two mode cells (CPOL x CPHA x FULLCYC). The seed draws the first mode cell from the 8. The second mode cell has the other CPOL value, the other CPHA value and a drawn FULLCYC, so every seed grades both sck idle levels and both CPHA values. Dimensions: the two mode cells and their order, and per mode cell the lane width (x1, x2 or x4), CLKDIV a (0 to 15), CSNLEAD, CSNTRAIL and CSNIDLE (0 to 15 each), CLKDIV b for the ratio leg (0 to 15, not equal to a), the TX bytes and the RX length (1 to 8 bytes) and bytes of each pair. The first two TX bytes of each segment hold lane 0 at 1, 0, 1, 0 on successive clocks, so the data-edge check has its changes by construction. The CLKDIV and CSN draws are weighted toward 0 and 15. The control run of every mode cell writes CSNIDLE=0. `reseed = 3` samples other mode cells, orders and values. The plan shape (two mode cells, each with a control run, a graded run and a ratio leg) is the same on every seed. The 8 mode cells, the CLKDIV and CSN classes and the CPHA=1 edge depend on the seed and close on a merge of regressions (<<sep-fcov-rand,SEP_FCOV>>); with two of the 8 mode cells per seed and 3 seeds, about 8 x ln 8 / 6, so about three regressions, fill the 8 mode cells.

*Objective*: The pad effect of CONFIGOPTS holds (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/spi_controller/regs/gen/adoc/spi_controller.adoc`, CONFIGOPTS register; later cites in this entry use the file name only). sck idles at the CPOL level. The sck period is 2*(CLKDIV+1) core clocks. Chip select falls (CSNLEAD+1) half sck cycles before the first sck edge, rises (CSNTRAIL+1) half sck cycles after the last sck edge, and stays high at least (CSNIDLE+1) half sck cycles between commands, where a half sck cycle is CLKDIV+1 core clocks. Tx data changes on the trailing sck edge when CPHA=0 and on the leading edge when CPHA=1. The core clock frequency is not stated, so the test grades a period ratio between two CLKDIV values and no absolute frequency (`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`). Every segment of a mode cell runs at the drawn width, and its data follows the lane contract of the Rules of this section (Lane data).

*Test Coverage*: CPOL x CPHA x FULLCYC (two mode cells per seed, one at each CPOL value and each CPHA value; the 8 mode cells close on a merge of regressions; FULLCYC is written in the mode cells; its Rx sampling point is graded at CPHA=0 and at CPHA=1 in the FULLCYC leg at x1, x2 and x4), CLKDIV 0 to 15, CSNLEAD and CSNTRAIL 0 to 15, CSNIDLE 1 to 15 in the graded run (step 4 raises it above the control gap, so CSNIDLE 0 is not graded), the sck idle level, the chip-select and clock output enables with CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN = 1 (CHK-SPI-CTRL-OE), the sck period, the chip-select lead, trail and idle gap, the Tx data edge at CLKDIV 1 or more, the period ratio of two CLKDIV values, the Tx and Rx data of every frame of each mode cell (control pairs, graded pair, ratio leg) at the drawn width, and the SPI wire checks of the shared monitor in both mode cells. The shift edge of the cell CPHA=1 with FULLCYC=1 is logged with `OBS-SPI-CPHA` and not graded: `spi_host.hjson:378-379` ("data is shifted and sampled with the trailing edge"), `theory_of_operation.md:42` (at CPHA = 1 data is "always asserted on the leading edge of `SCK`") and `theory_of_operation.md:298` (FULLCYC "has no effect on any of the signals transmitted") state different edges. The open test `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` grades CONFIGOPTS read-back only; the pad effect is graded here. Functional coverage: `sep_spi_pad_interface_cg` (`cp_pad_timing_ok`) and `sep_spi_pad_rand_cg` (`cp_idle_level`; graded on a merge of regressions: `cp_mode8`, `cp_mode_x_dir`, `cp_clkdiv_class`, `cp_csn_class`, `cp_cpha_edge`) and `sep_spi_lane_data_cg` (cells `pad_timing_cfg` x the two drawn widths x TX, RX; the three widths close on a merge of regressions).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw the two mode cells and their order and, per mode cell, the lane width, CLKDIV a, CSNLEAD, CSNTRAIL, CSNIDLE, CLKDIV b and the data from the seed. Every COMMAND of a mode cell uses the SPEED of its width (0, 1 or 2 for x1, x2 or x4) in place of SPEED 0 below, each TX segment has its own TXDATA words, and the lane responder drives the RX bytes of each Rx segment. After each frame the leaf registers it with the scoreboard of the mode cell (`expect_frame`, with the RXDATA bytes it read). Log the plan first (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`). Steps 3 to 7 run once for each of the two mode cells in the drawn order, with the draws of that mode cell, and each checker prints one PASS line per mode cell with `mode=<cpol><cpha><fullcyc>` in it. Every wait in the steps below is a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check that the step feeds. A segment is done when the sampler has shown chip select low and then high (for a chain with CSAAT=1, high after the last segment) and a later STATUS read shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0. Every wait for a segment below is this bounded wait, so a STATUS read taken before the command starts does not end it. Before every COMMAND write the test polls STATUS until READY=1 (bounded). For the pair of transactions of steps 3 and 5, the pair is done when the sampler has shown chip select low and then high twice and a STATUS read shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0.
. Start from the harness reset state. Sample `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` on one rising `clk_i` edge before the first CONTROL write; both read 0 (control of CHK-SPI-CTRL-OE, else CTRL-MISSING). Write CONTROL with SPIEN=1, OUTPUT_EN=1 and SW_RST=0. Write CSID=0. Read ERROR_STATUS; it reads 0.
. Control run. With ACTIVE=0, write CONFIGOPTS with the drawn mode cell, CLKDIV a, the drawn CSNLEAD and CSNTRAIL, and CSNIDLE=0. Never write CONFIGOPTS while a transaction runs. Write the TXDATA words of a Tx segment of 16 bytes of seeded data. After a STATUS read that shows READY=1, write COMMAND for that Tx segment (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 2, CSAAT=0). Then poll STATUS until a read shows READY=1 and ACTIVE=1, so that the first segment is still active, and write COMMAND for an Rx segment of 1 to 8 bytes (DIRECTION 1, CSAAT=0) right after that read. When a read shows READY=1 and ACTIVE=0 before the Rx COMMAND is written, repeat the control run with a Tx segment twice as long (at most 3 doublings, a test parameter, while TXFULL=0 allows the words); when ACTIVE=0 still, log CTRL-MISSING and fail. Wait until the pair is done. Read RXDATA until RXEMPTY=1, each read after a STATUS read that shows RXEMPTY=0. Take g0, the number of core clocks that chip select stays high between the two transactions.
. Raise CSNIDLE. When (CSNIDLE+1)*(a+1) is not above g0, set CSNIDLE to the smallest value in 0 to 15 whose minimum (CSNIDLE+1)*(a+1) is above g0. When even the value 15 does not give a minimum above g0, log CTRL-MISSING and fail.
. Graded run. With ACTIVE=0, write CONFIGOPTS with the drawn mode cell, CLKDIV a, CSNLEAD, CSNTRAIL and the CSNIDLE of step 4. Run the same pair of transactions as in step 3, with the same segment lengths and the same write order, so that the Rx COMMAND is queued while the Tx segment is active. From the sampler, take: every sck period inside the two chip-select-low windows; the lead and the trail of each transaction; the chip-select-high gap between the two transactions; the sck level in every chip-select-high window that starts after the first transaction ends (the gap and the interval after the second transaction); the number of sck toggles in each chip-select-low window; and the Tx data changes on `spi_mosi_o`.
. Ratio leg. With ACTIVE=0, write CONFIGOPTS with the mode cell of step 5, CLKDIV a, and CSNLEAD, CSNTRAIL and CSNIDLE as in step 5. Write the TXDATA word first, seeded as in step 3, so that step 7 has at least two data changes after the first bit when it grades this run; then after a STATUS read that shows READY=1 write COMMAND for one Tx segment of 4 bytes (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 2, CSAAT=0), wait until the segment is done, and take the sck period as period_a. With ACTIVE=0, write CONFIGOPTS with CLKDIV b and the other fields unchanged. Run the same Tx segment in the same order, wait until the segment is done, and take the period as period_b.
. Grade the Tx data edge on the run whose CLKDIV is 1 or more: the graded run of step 5 when a is at least 1, else the CLKDIV b run of step 6. Take the data changes on `spi_mosi_o` after the first bit. The first bit is exempt, because it is set up before the first sck edge. A change is "in the stated half" when it lies at or after the stated sck edge and before the next sck edge: the trailing edge for CPHA=0 and the leading edge for CPHA=1. In the cell CPHA=1 with FULLCYC=1, do not grade; log the cell with `OBS-SPI-CPHA` (the OpenTitan texts for its shift edge conflict). When the graded run has fewer than two data changes after the first bit, log CTRL-MISSING and fail.
. FULLCYC leg, after both mode cells. Stop the wire monitor: it samples at the sample edge, so it would decode the late beats below one beat late, and RXDATA is the graded side. For each width x1, x2 and x4 in a seeded order, draw CPOL and CLKDIV (1 to 15) and 1 to 8 Rx bytes whose first beat is 0..01 on the lanes of the width. The lane responder runs late (`env/sep_spi_lane_responder.py`, `set_late`): it puts each beat one core clock after its sample edge and holds it to one core clock after the next sample edge. Run the leg for every width at CPHA=0, then for every width at CPHA=1, each cell with its own CPOL, CLKDIV and bytes; the late responder runs at both CPHA values. In a seeded order, read one Rx segment of the bytes with CONFIGOPTS FULLCYC=1 (graded window open) and one with FULLCYC=0 (control) at the cell's CPHA, each after ACTIVE=0, and drain RXDATA after each; the drain gives ceil(n/4) words. Grade with CHK-SPI-FULLCYC. Read ERROR_STATUS; it reads 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SPI-IDLE-LEVEL
|sck is at the CPOL level while chip select is high, in every chip-select-high window of the graded run that starts after the first transaction ends (`spi_controller.adoc`, CONFIGOPTS.CPOL). A host that idles at the other level fails. Anchor: the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` (approved). Control: the chip-select-low window of the same transaction shows sck toggles, so the level is read in a design that drives sck. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-IDLE-LEVEL PASS seed=<n> cpol=<c> level=<l> windows=<w> control_toggles=<t>`.
|CHK-SPI-CTRL-OE
|With CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN = 1, `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` read 1 on every sampled clock of the two chip-select-low windows of the graded pair and of every chip-select-high window after the first of them, the gap and the windows that follow (`spi_host.hjson:222-224`, CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN: "Enable the SPI host output buffers for the sck, csb, and sd lines."; `smu-sep.adoc:274-276` narrows only the data lanes). A host that drops the chip-select or clock output enable in a frame, in the gap or while chip select is high fails. Anchor: the output-enable taps `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` (approved), sampled on each core clock in the read-only phase. Control: one sample before the first CONTROL write that sets OUTPUT_EN reads both 0 (`smu-sep.adoc:275-276`: the host "drives no pad until software sets its `CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN`"), and the chip-select-low and chip-select-high windows have samples, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-CTRL-OE PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> width=x<w> expect_cs_oe=1 expect_sck_oe=1 cs_low_clks=<k> cs_high_clks=<k> high_windows=<k> cs_oe_bad_low=0 sck_oe_bad_low=0 cs_oe_bad_high=0 sck_oe_bad_high=0 pre_enable_cs_oe=0 pre_enable_sck_oe=0`.
|CHK-SPI-PERIOD
|Every sck period in the graded run equals 2*(CLKDIV+1) core clocks exactly (`spi_controller.adoc`, CONFIGOPTS.CLKDIV). The sampler reads every core clock, so no tolerance applies. A host with another divider or a half-rate sck fails. Anchor: the pad ports (approved). Evidence: `CHK-SPI-PERIOD PASS seed=<n> clkdiv=<d> periods=<k> period_clk=<got> expect=<2*(d+1)>`.
|CHK-SPI-RATIO
|The two runs of the ratio leg give period_a*(b+1) equal to period_b*(a+1) exactly, with a not equal to b. A host whose period does not scale with CLKDIV fails. No absolute frequency enters the check. Anchor: the pad ports (approved). Evidence: `CHK-SPI-RATIO PASS seed=<n> a=<a> b=<b> period_a=<pa> period_b=<pb> lhs=<pa x (b+1)> rhs=<pb x (a+1)>`.
|CHK-SPI-CSN
|In the graded run, the lead of each transaction is at least (CSNLEAD+1)*(CLKDIV+1) core clocks, the trail at least (CSNTRAIL+1)*(CLKDIV+1), and the chip-select-high gap at least (CSNIDLE+1)*(CLKDIV+1) (`spi_controller.adoc`, CONFIGOPTS.CSNLEAD, CSNTRAIL, CSNIDLE; OpenTitan spi_host theory of operation, chip-select timing: "A<->B minimum (CSNLEAD+1)", "C<->D minimum (CSNTRAIL+1)", "D<->E minimum (CSNIDLE+1)", "These settings are all minimum bounds, and delays in the FSM implementation may create more margin"; the spi_host theory of operation states these as minimum bounds that FSM delays may exceed). The lead and trail checks are lower bounds only: a host whose natural lead or trail already meets the drawn minimum (the trail at CPHA=1, for example) passes whether or not it applies the field, because the specification states no upper bound to grade against. Control for the minimum: the control run gives the natural gap g0 with CSNIDLE=0, and the graded CSNIDLE has a minimum above g0, so a host that ignores CSNIDLE shows a gap of g0 and fails. In both runs the Rx COMMAND is written while the Tx segment is active (the READY=1 read shows ACTIVE=1), so the gap is the one the host produces between queued commands and does not include bus latency. Anchor: the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` (approved). Evidence: `CHK-SPI-CSN PASS seed=<n> clkdiv=<d> lead=<got>/<min> trail=<got>/<min> idle_gap=<got> min=<exp> control_gap=<g0>`. The excess of each lead, trail and gap over its minimum is allowed margin and is logged: `OBS-SPI-CSN-EXACT seed=<n> mode=<m> clkdiv=<d> half=<h> lead_excess=<a>,<b> trail_excess=<a>,<b> idle_gap=<g> csnidle_plus_one=<c> width=x<w>`. The chip-select and clock output enables in the frames, the gap and the idle windows are graded by CHK-SPI-CTRL-OE.
|CHK-SPI-CPHA
|At CLKDIV 1 or more, each Tx data change after the first bit lies in the half sck period that starts at the stated edge, and none lies in the half period that starts at the other edge (`spi_controller.adoc`, CONFIGOPTS.CPHA). A host with the opposite phase fails. Anchor: the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_mosi_o` (approved). Control: the run has at least two data changes after the first bit, else CTRL-MISSING, and `spi_mosi_o` is never X or Z while chip select is low, else the check fails (an unknown sample hides a change). The cell CPHA=1 with FULLCYC=1 does not run this check; the leaf logs `OBS-SPI-CPHA seed=<n> cpha=1 fullcyc=1 graded=0 ambiguous=1` and the check does not count in k, because `spi_host.hjson:378-379` ("data is shifted and sampled with the trailing edge"), `theory_of_operation.md:42` ("data is always asserted on the leading edge of `SCK`") and `theory_of_operation.md:298` (FULLCYC "has no effect on any of the signals transmitted") state different shift edges. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-CPHA PASS seed=<n> cpha=<p> clkdiv=<d> edge=<trailing or leading> in_stated_half=<k> in_other_half=0 ... mosi_xz=0`.
|CHK-SPI-FULLCYC
|With CONFIGOPTS.FULLCYC=1 the host samples the Rx lanes a full sck cycle after its shift edge, at CPHA=0 and at CPHA=1 (`spi_controller.adoc`, CONFIGOPTS.FULLCYC: "Rather than sampling the SD bus a half cycle after shifting out data, the data is sampled a full cycle after shifting data out ... if CPHA = 0, data is shifted out on the trailing edge, and sampled a full cycle later"; `spi_host.hjson:373-380`: "If CPHA = 1, data is shifted and sampled with the trailing edge, also separated by a full cycle"; `theory_of_operation.md:42`: at CPHA = 1 data is "sampled on the trailing edge of `SCK`"), so from the late responder it reads the seeded bytes, byte for byte, at x1, x2 and x4 at both CPHA values. At CPHA=1 the late change of beat k lies one core clock after trailing edge k; a sample at the next leading edge (`theory_of_operation.md:312-317`) and a sample at the next trailing edge both lie after that change and before the change of beat k+1 (CLKDIV 1 or more), so the graded bytes do not depend on which of the two points the host uses. A host that ignores FULLCYC and samples a half cycle after the shift edge reads every beat one beat late and fails. Anchor: RXDATA readback (approved) and the response lanes `spi_sd_i` (approved top-port drive). Control: the FULLCYC=0 read of the same bytes from the same late responder equals the bytes shifted by one beat (first beat 0, `late_sample_edge_bytes`), which differ from the seeded bytes by construction (at CPHA=1 beat 0 reads the lane level 0 that holds before the first late change); else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-FULLCYC PASS seed=<n> width=x<w> cpol=<c> cpha=<0 or 1> clkdiv=<d> rx_bytes=<n> expected=<hex> fullcyc1=<hex> fullcyc0=<hex> late_expected=<hex>`, one per width and CPHA value.
|CHK-SPI-WIRE
|On every frame of the leaf, in the CPOL and CPHA of its mode cell (`hw/common/dv/vip/ocah_spi_vip/README.md`, OcahSpiMonitor): SCK is at the CPOL level at each chip-select edge and moves only to that level while chip select is high; while chip select is low, MOSI does not change on a sample edge, nor after a sample edge and before the next shift edge; while chip select is low, chip select, SCK and MOSI resolve to 0 or 1. The monitor also grades the monitor-only taps `spi_sd_o`, `spi_sd_oe_o`, `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` against the output-enable rules of `doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc` ("SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment": lane n drives `sd[n]` with output enable `sd_oe[n]`, chip select and clock have `cs_oe` and `sck_oe`, and the host drives a data lane only while chip select is asserted): no data lane output enable rises, or is on at an SCK edge, while chip select is high (`oe_idle`); at each sample edge the data lane output enables are none, lane 0, lanes 1:0 or lanes 3:0 (`oe_lanes`); and the chip-select and clock output enables are on at each sample edge (`oe_ctrl`). The MOSI edge and X/Z rules apply to every data lane whose output enable is on. A host that drives a data lane while chip select is high, enables another lane pattern, or turns off the chip-select or clock output enable in a frame fails. A host with the wrong idle level or the opposite data phase fails. Anchor: the pad ports `spi_cs_n_o`, `spi_sck_o` and `spi_mosi_o` and the monitor-only taps `spi_sd_o`, `spi_sd_oe_o`, `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` (approved, `hw/sys/sep/dv/docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, SPI host pads). Control: the CPOL and CPHA of each of the two mode cells has at least one graded frame, else the check fails. Evidence: one line per leaf, `CHK-SPI-WIRE PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> per_mode=<cpol><cpha>:<frames>,<cpol><cpha>:<frames> wire_errors=0`.
|CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME
|Every Tx and Rx frame of each mode cell (control pairs, graded pair, ratio leg) at the width of the cell, through one scoreboard per cell (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`, `expect_frame`): the Tx bytes queued equal the wire decode; the Rx bytes the responder drove equal the wire decode and the RXDATA bytes the DUT delivered (Rules of this section, Lane data). The check runs in every CPOL and CPHA, so a host that shifts or samples data on the wrong edge at x2 or x4 fails too. A frame with no expectation, or an expectation with no frame, fails. Anchor: the lane taps through `OcahSpiMonitor` and RXDATA readback (approved). Evidence: one `SPI-SB FRAME` line per frame with its mode, width and leg, and `CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> bytes=<n>` per mode cell.
|CHK-SPI-LANE-DATA
|One line per mode cell with `width=` and the Tx and Rx frame and byte counts, after CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME of the cell passed. Control: the cell graded at least one Tx frame and one Rx frame, else the leaf fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-LANE-DATA PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> width=x<w> tx_frames=<k> tx_bytes=<b> rx_frames=<k> rx_bytes=<b>`.
|CHK-SPI-CELL-FRAMES
|Per mode cell, the monitor frames of the cell are exactly the frames the cell registered with its scoreboard: every monitor frame index of the cell has one expectation, every expectation has a monitor frame in the cell, and the monitor frame count in the CPOL and CPHA of the cell equals the number of registered frames. A host that emits an extra chip-select frame, also after the last expected frame of the cell, fails. Anchor: `OcahSpiMonitor` frame list and per-mode tally (approved). Control: the cell registered at least one frame, else the check fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-CELL-FRAMES PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> width=x<w> first=<i> frames=<k> mode_tally=<k> registered=<k> unexpected=[] unseen=[]` per mode cell.
|CHK-SPI-RX-WORDS
|After each pair of steps 3 and 5, the RXDATA reads until RXEMPTY=1 give exactly ceil(n/4) words for the Rx segment of n bytes, and no word when the Rx COMMAND was not written. A host that delivers a surplus or a missing RXDATA word fails; the surplus word is not cut away before the compare. Anchor: STATUS.RXEMPTY and RXDATA readback (approved). Evidence: `CHK-SPI-RX-WORDS PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> width=x<w> <tag> rx_bytes=<n> words=<k> expect=<k>` per pair.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The CPHA edge at CLKDIV 0, the shift edge of the cell CPHA=1 with FULLCYC=1 (`OBS-SPI-CPHA`; except by CHK-SPI-WIRE), and any absolute sck frequency are not graded. Between a CONFIGOPTS write and the next command, only CHK-SPI-WIRE grades the sck level: sck moves only to the CPOL level and is at it when chip select falls. Each mode cell runs at one drawn width, so one run grades two of the width x mode cells; the sck cycle counts of each speed are graded in <<sep_spi_pad_speed_dir_matrix_test,entry 4.7>>. The CSNLEAD and CSNTRAIL checks are minimum bounds; their excess is logged with OBS-SPI-CSN-EXACT. The FULLCYC leg grades RXDATA only; the wire monitor is stopped for it. The open test `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` owns the CONFIGOPTS read-back.

'''''

[[sep_spi_fifo_stall_watermark_rand_test]]
===== 4.9 `sep_spi_fifo_stall_watermark_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+sep_spi_four_lane`, no `rst_ni` pulse, 3 seeds; tool: both (Verilator and VCS); testlist groups `cpu_stub` and `all`, file `spi.toml`

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_fifo_stall_watermark_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` for the register frontdoor over `s_axi` (CPU-LSU splice, approved). A test-side sampler reads the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` on each core clock (approved). The lane responder `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_lane_responder.py` drives seeded bytes on `spi_sd_i[3:0]` in every Rx segment (approved top-port drive; `+sep_spi_four_lane`). `OcahSpiMonitor` (`ocah_spi_vip`, attached through `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_wire_monitor.py` with the output-enable taps) decodes every frame, and `SepSpiScoreboard` (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`, one per width, no flash model) grades it. The monitor runs in sessions: a session stops before each SW_RST abort and a new one starts after it, so the monitor never sees a frame cut on purpose. The abort check reads the taps `spi_sd_oe_o`, `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` (approved). No checker needs a proposed probe. No inject. No check is blocked.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed (RAND-REP). Dimensions: the order of the three lane widths x1, x2 and x4 (each runs on every seed), the TX arrival gap, the Rx read gap and burst length, the payload words, the mid TX watermark value (2 up to the TX depth minus 1), the TX control watermark (1, the mid value or the TX depth), the mid RX watermark value (2 up to the RX depth minus 2), the order of the RX watermark values and CLKDIV (1 to 3); per width the Tx and Rx lengths of the data frames, the sck point of the SW_RST abort, the lengths of the clean frame after it, the watermark values and word counts of the event leg, and the data bytes. Every seed runs the per-width legs at x1, x2 and x4, the TX watermarks 0 and the TX depth plus 1 (when that is at most 255) with the drawn control watermark, and the RX watermarks 0, 1 and the mid value. The graded TX cells are TX_WATERMARK 0 and the TX depth plus 1. The classes "depth" and "depth plus 1" resolve against the depth that the leaf finds in the run, which CHK-SPI-DEPTH-TX and CHK-SPI-DEPTH-RX grade. `reseed = 3` samples other speeds, gaps, bursts, payloads, control watermarks, mid values and CLKDIV values. The plan of legs is the same on every seed. Every seed runs each width, so the stall x SPEED cells fill on every run.

*Objective*: The FIFO status contract holds (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/spi_controller/regs/gen/adoc/spi_controller.adoc`, CONTROL and STATUS registers; later cites in this entry use the file name only). TXSTALL and RXSTALL assert, and sck pauses, while an active segment waits for TX data or for RX space. STATUS.TXWM is set while TXQD is below TX_WATERMARK. STATUS.RXWM is set while RXQD exceeds RX_WATERMARK. Each TXDATA write and each RXDATA read moves one 32-bit word. The SEP SPI host is the unmodified OpenTitan IP (`sep-programming.adoc:784`) with TxDepth 72, RxDepth 64 and CmdDepth 4 (`spi_host.hjson:93-107`). With no COMMAND, STATUS.TXFULL reads 1 after 73 TXDATA writes ("the fifo is observed as having an effective depth of N+1 where N is the parameterized depth", OpenTitan SPI_HOST `theory_of_operation.md:684-685`, tenstorrent/tt-opentitan@d1ce369cb) and 0 after 72. After an idle Rx segment of 64 words with no RXDATA read, STATUS.RXFULL reads 1 and RXQD reads 64; after 63 words RXFULL reads 0 and RXQD reads 63. CMDQD counts the accepted commands and stays at 4 or below. TXFULL, TXEMPTY, RXFULL and RXEMPTY follow the occupancy, and a FIFO is never full and empty together. INTR_STATE.SPI_EVENT is the OR of the levels of the sources EVENT_ENABLE selects (`hw/sys/sep/doc/spi.adoc`, Features). CONTROL.SW_RST clears the internal state and not the registers (CONTROL.SW_RST); after it the host drives no data lane and chip select is high (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, "SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment": a data lane is driven only while chip select is asserted), and with CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN = 1 `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` stay 1 (SW_RST "Clears the internal state (not registers)", `spi_host.hjson:213`; OUTPUT_EN "Enable the SPI host output buffers for the sck, csb, and sd lines.", `spi_host.hjson:222`). The data of every frame follows the lane contract of the Rules of this section (Lane data).

*Test Coverage*: TXSTALL, RXSTALL, the RX watermark, CMDQD, TXFULL while active and RXFULL while idle at x1, x2 and x4 on every seed; the Tx and Rx data of every frame of each width, byte for byte; INTR_STATE.SPI_EVENT for the TXWM and RXWM events at each width, with the event enabled and masked; a SW_RST abort of a Tx segment at each width (x1, x2 and x4) on every seed, then one clean frame at that width; TX watermark cells at watermark 0 and above the depth; RX watermark cells on both sides of each of the thresholds 0, 1 and a mid value; each RXDATA read; CMDQD with a held head segment; TXFULL while a segment is active; RXFULL below and at the RX depth while the host is idle; the TX depth (73 words), the RX depth (64 words) and the CMDQD bound of 4 (CHK-SPI-DEPTH-TX, CHK-SPI-DEPTH-RX, CHK-SPI-DEPTH-CMD); the four flags TXFULL, TXEMPTY, RXFULL and RXEMPTY. The open test `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` grades TXWM and TXQD at TX_WATERMARK 2 to 8, and the open test `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test` grades STATUS.TXWM against TXQD at TX_WATERMARK 4. The TX cells at watermark 1, a mid value and the TX depth are controls here: the leaf runs one of them, drawn per seed, logs it with `OBS-SPI-WM` and grades nothing. Entry 4.7 grades the Rx word count and RXQD (CHK-SPI-RXCNT), so TXQD and RXQD at idle are logged and not graded here, except RXQD at RXFULL (CHK-SPI-DEPTH-RX). This entry adds watermark 0, a watermark above the depth, the RX axis, the stall flags, the RXQD step per read and RXFULL. The overflow and underflow kinds are graded in <<sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test_leg,entry 4.10>>. Functional coverage: `sep_spi_fifo_status_cg` (`cp_flag`, `cp_stall`, `cp_ready_x_active`, `cp_cmd_accept`, `cp_tx_write_space`, `cp_rx_read`, `cp_depth_counter`), `sep_spi_fifo_rand_cg` (`cp_txwm`, `cp_rxwm`, `cp_stall_x_speed`), `sep_spi_pad_rand_cg` (graded on a merge of regressions: `cp_clkdiv_class`), `sep_spi_lane_data_cg` (cells `fifo_stall_watermark` x x1, x2, x4 x TX, RX) and `sep_spi_lane_abort_chain_cg` (`x_abort_width`, cells `sw_rst` x x1, x2, x4).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw the SPEED of the stall legs, the TX arrival gap, the Rx read gap and burst, the payload words, CLKDIV, the two mid watermark values, the TX control watermark and the RX watermark order from the seed. Log the plan first (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`, one `PLAN seed=<n> width=x<w> ...` line per width). The run order is: step 3; then, for each width in the drawn order, steps 4 to 7, 10 to 15, 17 to 19 and 16; then steps 8 and 9. "The drawn speed" in a per-width step is the SPEED of that width (0, 1 or 2). Step 5 finds the RX depth at the first width only. The head segment of step 12, the segment of step 14 and the extra segment of step 15 also run at the drawn speed. Each TX segment has its own TXDATA words of seeded data, and the lane responder drives the seeded bytes of every Rx segment. Every frame of a width is registered with the scoreboard of that width (`expect_frame`, with the RXDATA bytes read). Every wait in the steps below is a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check that the step feeds. The stall window is 8 sck periods, a test parameter. A segment is done when the sampler has shown chip select low and then high (for a chain with CSAAT=1, high after the last segment) and a later STATUS read shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0. Every wait for a segment below is this bounded wait, so a STATUS read taken before the command starts does not end it. Before every COMMAND write the test polls STATUS until READY=1 (bounded). Run the SPI clean state before every leg. The SW_RST helper writes CONTROL.SW_RST=1 with the other fields kept, polls STATUS until TXEMPTY=1 and RXEMPTY=1, then writes CONTROL.SW_RST=0. The SPI clean state writes 1 to every bit of ERROR_STATUS and to INTR_STATE.ERROR, writes CSID=0, and runs the SW_RST helper.
. Start from the harness reset state. Sample `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` on one rising `clk_i` edge before the first CONTROL write; both read 0 (control of CHK-SPI-SWRST-ABORT, else CTRL-MISSING). Write CONTROL with SPIEN=1, OUTPUT_EN=1, SW_RST=0, TX_WATERMARK=0 and RX_WATERMARK=0x7F. Write CONFIGOPTS with CPOL=0, CPHA=0, FULLCYC=0, CSNLEAD, CSNTRAIL and CSNIDLE at 0 and the drawn CLKDIV. Write CSID=0. Read STATUS; log that TXEMPTY and RXEMPTY are set.
. TX depth. With no COMMAND, write TXDATA words one at a time, each after a STATUS read that shows TXFULL=0 and ACTIVE=0. Stop at the first STATUS read that shows TXFULL=1, and never write once more. The number of words written is the TX depth, graded by CHK-SPI-DEPTH-TX: 73, with TXFULL=0 in the read after the 72nd write (control). Run the SW_RST helper.
. RX stall. Poll STATUS until READY=1, then write COMMAND for an Rx segment of 1032 bytes (the drawn speed, DIRECTION 1, CSAAT=0), which is more words than the 8-bit RXQD counts. Read no RXDATA. Count the sck edges from the COMMAND write until a STATUS read shows RXFULL=1 and RXSTALL=1; this count is the control and must be above 0, else CTRL-MISSING. Open the stall window at that read and count the sck edges in it. Read STATUS at the start and at the end of the window; both reads show RXFULL=1 and RXSTALL=1 and the same RXQD. Log that RXQD as s. Because RXQD may be an underestimate while ACTIVE=1, s is a lower bound of the RX depth and only the start of the search in step 5. When the two RXQD values differ, log CTRL-MISSING and fail.
. Drain the RX FIFO in bursts of the drawn length with the drawn gap, each RXDATA read after a STATUS read that shows RXEMPTY=0, until the segment is done and RXEMPTY=1. The drain reads 258 words in total, the words of the 1032 bytes. Then find the RX depth with ACTIVE=0: for r from the larger of 1 and s minus 1 upward, run the SPI clean state, run an Rx segment of 4*r bytes (the drawn speed, DIRECTION 1, CSAAT=0) with no RXDATA read, wait until the segment is done, and read STATUS. The first r whose read shows RXFULL=1 is the RX depth R, graded by CHK-SPI-DEPTH-RX: R = 64 and RXQD = 64 in that read, and the read at r = 63 shows RXFULL=0 and RXQD = 63 (control). Every smaller r shows RXFULL=0; log RXQD with `OBS-SPI-QD`. When r reaches 255, the largest the 8-bit RXQD holds, with no RXFULL=1, log CTRL-MISSING and fail. Read RXDATA until RXEMPTY=1 and run the SW_RST helper.
. TX stall. Draw m (the segment length in words, 2 up to the TX depth) and m1 (1 up to m minus 1). After a STATUS read that shows READY=1, write COMMAND for a Tx segment of 4*m bytes (the drawn speed, DIRECTION 2, CSAAT=0). Write m1 TXDATA words. Wait until the sampler shows 4*m1*8/lanes sck cycles. Poll STATUS until TXSTALL=1. Open the stall window at that read and count the sck edges in it; read STATUS at the start and at the end of the window, and both show TXSTALL=1. Hold for the drawn gap after the window. Write the remaining m minus m1 words. Poll STATUS until TXSTALL=0; that read is taken while the sampler still shows chip select low. Wait until the segment is done and take the sck cycle count of the segment.
. TX stall control. Run the SPI clean state. Write all m TXDATA words, then after a STATUS read that shows READY=1 write the same COMMAND. Read STATUS repeatedly until the segment is done. Every read that the sampler shows taken while chip select is low shows TXSTALL=0; at least one read is taken while chip select is low, else CTRL-MISSING. Take the sck cycle count.
. TX watermark. Take three values of Wt, resolved against the TX depth: 0, the TX depth plus 1 (skipped when it is above 255) and the drawn control value (1, the drawn mid value or the TX depth). For each Wt, write CONTROL.TX_WATERMARK=Wt with the other fields kept. With ACTIVE=0 and no COMMAND, write TXDATA words (each after a STATUS read that shows TXFULL=0) to the occupancies q in increasing order from the set of 0, 1, Wt minus 1, Wt and the TX depth that lie in 0 to the TX depth. At each q, poll STATUS until TXQD=q with ACTIVE=0; that read gives TXWM. The reads of Wt 0 and of the TX depth plus 1 are graded; the reads of the control value are logged with `OBS-SPI-WM`. Run the SW_RST helper after each Wt.
. TX watermark control. Run the SPI clean state. Write CONTROL.TX_WATERMARK=1 and poll STATUS until TXWM=1, with TXQD=0 in that read.
. RX watermark. For each Wr of 0, 1 and the drawn mid value, in the drawn order, write CONTROL.RX_WATERMARK=Wr with the other fields kept. For each r in Wr minus 1 (when Wr is at least 1) and Wr plus 1, run the SPI clean state, then after a STATUS read that shows READY=1 run an Rx segment of 4*r bytes (DIRECTION 1, CSAAT=0) with no RXDATA read, skipping the segment when r is 0. Wait until the segment is done, then poll STATUS until RXQD=r; that read gives RXWM. Never drive r equal to Wr. After the Wr plus 1 run of each Wr, run step 11 before the next Wr.
. RX drain and counters. After the Wr plus 1 run of each Wr, read RXDATA one word at a time until RXEMPTY=1, each read after a STATUS read that shows RXEMPTY=0. After each read, poll STATUS until RXQD is one lower; that read gives RXWM. For Wr of 1 or more, the read at RXQD equal to Wr minus 1 shows RXWM=0 (CHK-SPI-WM, crossing out). RXWM at RXQD equal to Wr is logged and not graded. Run the SW_RST helper.
. Command queue. After a STATUS read that shows READY=1, write one TXDATA word, then COMMAND for a Tx segment of 8 bytes (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 2, CSAAT=0). The core sends the queued word and stalls inside the segment. Poll STATUS until ACTIVE=1 and TXSTALL=1; the head segment is held. Read CMDQD as c0. Write n further COMMANDs (DIRECTION 0, LEN 0, CSAAT=0), n drawn from 1 to 3, each after a STATUS read that shows READY=1; stop when READY=0. After each accepted COMMAND, poll STATUS until CMDQD differs from its previous value; the new value is the previous value plus 1. Log CMDQD and READY. Every STATUS read of this step shows CMDQD at 4 or below (CHK-SPI-DEPTH-CMD). At least one COMMAND must be accepted, else CTRL-MISSING.
. Release the queue. Write one TXDATA word. Wait until the sampler has shown, after that write, one chip-select rise for the held segment and one chip-select-low window per accepted COMMAND, and STATUS shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0. Run the SW_RST helper.
. TX full while active. Write CONFIGOPTS with CLKDIV 3, 7 or 15 for x1, x2 or x4 (each width then takes 256 core clocks per TX word, so the TXDATA writes outpace the segment) and the other fields as in step 2. Let D be the TX depth and K a test parameter equal to D. After a STATUS read that shows READY=1, write COMMAND for a Tx segment of 4*(D+K) bytes (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 2, CSAAT=0). Write TXDATA words, each after a STATUS read that shows TXFULL=0, until a STATUS read shows TXFULL=1 with ACTIVE=1; log its TXQD. When the segment is done, or all D+K words are written, without such a read, log CTRL-MISSING and fail. Write the remaining words, each after TXFULL=0. Wait until the segment is done and take the sck cycle count.
. RX full while idle. Run the SPI clean state. Run an Rx segment of exactly 4*R bytes (the drawn speed, DIRECTION 1, CSAAT=0), where R is the RX depth found in step 5, with no RXDATA read. Wait until the segment is done. Read STATUS; RXFULL is graded and RXQD is logged with `OBS-SPI-QD`. Capacity check: after a STATUS read that shows READY=1, write COMMAND for one more Rx segment of 4 bytes (the drawn speed, DIRECTION 1, CSAAT=0) with no RXDATA read. Wait until the sampler shows chip select high after that segment, or until the stall window passes with no sck edge, and read STATUS: RXQD is not above R, because a full FIFO takes no word. Then read RXDATA until the segment is done and RXEMPTY=1, each read after a STATUS read that shows RXEMPTY=0; log the number of words read with `OBS-SPI-RX-EXTRA` and grade nothing from it; a returned extra word is compared with the data of its segment. Run the SW_RST helper.
. Apply the flag-pair rule to every STATUS read of the width: TXFULL and TXEMPTY are never both set, and RXFULL and RXEMPTY are never both set. Require that each of the four flags is set in at least one read of the width.
. Data frames. Run one Tx segment and one Rx segment of seeded length at the width, each with its own scoreboard mark, and read the RX words.
. Event interrupts. TX side: write TX_WATERMARK k and n >= k TXDATA words, so that STATUS shows TXWM=0; read INTR_STATE; run a Tx segment of the n words; read STATUS (TXWM=1) and INTR_STATE. RX side: write RX_WATERMARK k >= 1 (RX_WATERMARK 0 gives RXWM=1 with an empty RX FIFO); read STATUS (RXWM=0) and INTR_STATE; run an Rx segment of n > k words with no RXDATA read; read STATUS (RXWM=1) and INTR_STATE. Each side runs with EVENT_ENABLE.TXWM (RXWM) set and again with EVENT_ENABLE 0 (masked control).
. SW_RST abort. End the monitor session. Load the TX words of a Tx segment at the width and write its COMMAND. After the drawn number of sck cycles read `spi_cs_n_o` and `spi_sd_oe_o` (the last pad sample before the write), then write CONTROL with SW_RST=1 and the other fields kept. Poll STATUS until TXEMPTY=1 and RXEMPTY=1, then write SW_RST=0. Read `spi_sd_oe_o`, `spi_cs_n_o`, `spi_cs_oe_o`, `spi_sck_oe_o` and STATUS. Start a new monitor session and run one clean frame at the width (Tx then Rx, CSAAT on the Tx segment).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SPI-TXSTALL
|In the stall window TXSTALL=1 and no sck edge appears. After the remaining words are written, TXSTALL reads 0 and the segment ends with 4*m*8/lanes sck cycles (`spi_controller.adoc`, STATUS.TXSTALL). A host that keeps sck running without data, or that keeps TXSTALL set after the data arrives, fails. Anchor: STATUS readback and the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` (approved). Control: with all words written before the COMMAND, TXSTALL reads 0 in every STATUS read taken while the sampler shows chip select low, in the same seed, and at least one such read exists. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-TXSTALL PASS seed=<n> speed=<s> gap_clk=<g> txstall=1 sck_edges_in_stall=0 txstall_after_push=0 cycles=<got>/<exp> control_gap0_txstall=0`.
|CHK-SPI-RXSTALL
|With RXFULL=1 and RXSTALL=1, no sck edge appears in the stall window, and after the drain the segment ends with all 258 words delivered and 258*32/lanes sck cycles (`spi_controller.adoc`, STATUS.RXFULL, STATUS.RXSTALL). A host that keeps sck running with a full RX FIFO, or that does not resume after the reads, fails. Anchor: STATUS readback and the pad ports (approved). Control: the same segment shows sck toggles before RXFULL, so the pause has a cause. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-RXSTALL PASS seed=<n> speed=<s> rxfull=1 rxstall=1 sck_edges_in_stall=0 ctrl_toggles_before_rxfull=<t> drained_words=<w> ... cycles=<c>/<c>`.
|CHK-SPI-WM
|At TX_WATERMARK 0, TXWM reads 0 at every occupancy q. At TX_WATERMARK equal to the TX depth plus 1, TXWM reads 1 at every occupancy q up to the full FIFO. For each RX_WATERMARK 0, 1 and the mid value, RXWM equals (RXQD > RX_WATERMARK) at RXQD one below and one above it (`spi_controller.adoc`, STATUS.TXWM, STATUS.RXWM). A host with the opposite polarity, with TXWM set at watermark 0, or with TXWM clear at a full FIFO below the watermark, fails. Anchor: the STATUS read that shows the expected occupancy with ACTIVE=0 (approved frontdoor). In the drain of step 11, for RX_WATERMARK 1 and the mid value, RXWM reads 0 at RXQD equal to RX_WATERMARK minus 1, so RXWM clears when the reads take RXQD below the watermark. RXWM at RXQD equal to RX_WATERMARK is not graded, because `CONTROL.RX_WATERMARK` ("reaches") and `STATUS.RXWM` ("exceeds") differ there. The TX cells at TX_WATERMARK 1, a mid value and the TX depth are controls: the open tests grade TXWM against TXQD at TX_WATERMARK 2 to 8 (`sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`, `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test`), so the leaf logs them with `OBS-SPI-WM` and prints no `CHK-SPI-WM PASS` for them. Control for "TXWM never set at watermark 0": watermark 1 with TXQD 0 gives TXWM=1 in the same seed, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-WM PASS seed=<n> side=<tx or rx> wm=<w> q=<q> flag=<f> expect=<e>` per graded occupancy, `CHK-SPI-WM PASS seed=<n> side=rx crossing=out wm=<w> q=<w-1> flag=0` per drain of a watermark of 1 or more, `OBS-SPI-WM seed=<n> side=tx wm=<w> q=<q> flag=<f>` per control occupancy, and `CTRL-SPI-WM seed=<n> side=tx wm=1 q=0 flag=1` for the polarity control.
|CHK-SPI-QD
|With ACTIVE=0, each RXDATA read lowers RXQD by 1 (`spi_controller.adoc`, STATUS.RXQD). TXFULL is set in a STATUS read with ACTIVE=1 during a Tx segment of 4*(D+K) bytes, and that segment ends with 4*(D+K)*8 sck cycles, so a full FIFO loses no word. R is the first idle segment length whose read shows RXFULL=1, so RXFULL below R is the search itself and is logged, not graded. After an Rx segment of exactly R, RXFULL is set and a further 4-byte Rx segment does not raise RXQD above R, because a full FIFO takes no word. The number of words that the drain then returns is logged with `OBS-SPI-RX-EXTRA` and not graded: no source states a count past RXFULL (`theory_of_operation.md:780` names "the RX FIFO plus any intervening buffers" and gives no count). A host that sets RXFULL before the FIFO is full shows RXQD of R+1 after the further segment and fails. R comes from idle reads only; the RXQD of the stall window is a lower bound and is not graded. TXFULL and TXEMPTY, and RXFULL and RXEMPTY, are never both set. A host that keeps RXQD after a read, that loses a word at a full TX FIFO, or that sets both flags of a pair, fails. Anchor: STATUS readback (approved frontdoor). TXQD and RXQD at idle are logged and not graded, except RXQD at RXFULL (CHK-SPI-DEPTH-RX): the open test `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` grades TXQD against the words written (CHK-WATERMARK), and entry 4.7 grades the received word count (CHK-SPI-RXCNT). Control for the flag pairs: each of the four flags is seen set in the same seed, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-QD PASS seed=<n> idle=1 rxqd_step_ok=1 txfull_active=1 txfull_cycles=<got>/<exp> rxfull_at_R=1 rxqd_after_extra=<q> max=<R> words_after_extra_obs=<w> pairs_both_set=0 seen_txfull=1 seen_txempty=1 seen_rxfull=1 seen_rxempty=1` and `OBS-SPI-QD seed=<n> txqd=<t> rxqd=<r>` per idle read.
|CHK-SPI-CMDQD
|CMDQD rises by 1 per accepted COMMAND while the head segment is held, and after the held segment is released every queued command runs on the pad (one chip-select-low window per accepted COMMAND after the held segment) and the queue empties (CMDQD reads 0 with ACTIVE low) (`spi_controller.adoc`, STATUS.CMDQD). A host that does not count a queued command, or that drops it, fails: a dropped command gives CMDQD 0 with fewer chip-select-low windows. Anchor: STATUS readback (approved frontdoor) and the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` (approved). Control: the head segment is held (ACTIVE=1 and TXSTALL=1) when the first COMMAND is written, and at least one COMMAND is accepted, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-CMDQD PASS seed=<n> queued=<n> cmdqd=<c> ready=<r> cs_windows=<got>/<1+n> drained=1`.
|CHK-SPI-DEPTH-TX
|With no COMMAND (ACTIVE=0), STATUS.TXFULL reads 1 after 73 TXDATA writes (`theory_of_operation.md:684-685`: "the fifo is observed as having an effective depth of N+1 where N is the parameterized depth"; N = TxDepth = 72, `spi_host.hjson:93-97`; `spi_host.hjson:263-264`: TXFULL "indicates that the transmit data fifo is full"). A host with another depth, or one that sets TXFULL early, fails. Anchor: STATUS readback with ACTIVE=0 (approved frontdoor). Control: TXFULL reads 0 after 72 writes, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-DEPTH-TX PASS seed=<n> written=73 expect=73 txfull=1 active=0 control_txfull_after_72=0`.
|CHK-SPI-DEPTH-RX
|An idle Rx segment of 64 words with no RXDATA read leaves STATUS.RXFULL = 1 ("When high, indicates that the receive fifo is full.", `spi_host.hjson:288`) and RXQD = 64 ("how many unread 32-bit words are currently in the RX FIFO", `spi_host.hjson:324-325`), with RxDepth = 64 (`spi_host.hjson:98-102`). A host with another depth fails. Anchor: STATUS readback with ACTIVE=0 (approved frontdoor). Control: the 63-word segment of the same search reads RXFULL = 0 and RXQD = 63, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-DEPTH-RX PASS seed=<n> width=x<w> words=64 expect_words=64 rxfull=1 rxqd=64 expect_rxqd=64 active=0 control_rxfull_at_63=0 control_rxqd_at_63=63`.
|CHK-SPI-DEPTH-CMD
|Every STATUS read of the command queue leg shows CMDQD at 4 or below ("how many unread 32-bit words are currently in the command segment queue", `spi_host.hjson:318-319`; CmdDepth = 4, `spi_host.hjson:103-107`). The check is an upper bound: no source ties READY = 0 to a full queue (`spi_host.hjson:250-252` says only "ready to receive commands") or says whether the held head segment counts in CMDQD, so CMDQD reaching 4 and the head count c0 are logged and not graded. A host with a deeper queue fails. Anchor: STATUS readback (approved frontdoor). Control: the CHK-SPI-CMDQD controls (a held head segment and at least one accepted COMMAND, CMDQD +1 per accepted COMMAND). Evidence: `CHK-SPI-DEPTH-CMD PASS seed=<n> width=x<w> status_reads=<k> max_cmdqd=<m> bound=4 control_accepted=<n> control_cmdqd_held=<h> control_c0=<c>`.
|CHK-SPI-EVENT-WM
|INTR_STATE.SPI_EVENT is a Status-type interrupt: the OR of the levels of the sources that EVENT_ENABLE selects (`hw/sys/sep/doc/spi.adoc`, Features; `spi_controller.adoc`, INTR_STATE, EVENT_ENABLE). After a segment moves a watermark flag to 1, SPI_EVENT reads 1 with EVENT_ENABLE.TXWM (RXWM) set and 0 with EVENT_ENABLE 0. A host whose event ignores the enable, or that does not raise it for the watermark source, fails. Anchor: STATUS and INTR_STATE readback over the frontdoor (approved). Controls: before the segment STATUS shows the flag 0 and SPI_EVENT reads 0; after it STATUS shows the flag 1. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-EVENT-WM PASS seed=<n> width=x<w> side=<tx or rx> wm=<k> words=<n> event_enable=<e> flag_before=0 spi_event_before=0 flag_after=1 spi_event_after=<e> expect=<e> intr_state=0x<v>`.
|CHK-SPI-SWRST-ABORT
|After CONTROL.SW_RST cuts a Tx segment at x1, x2 or x4, `spi_sd_oe_o` reads 0 and chip select is high (`smu-sep.adoc`, "SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment": a data lane is driven only while chip select is asserted), and TXQD = RXQD = CMDQD = 0 (`spi_controller.adoc`, CONTROL.SW_RST). A host that keeps a data lane driven or chip select low after the abort, or keeps a TX word, an RX word or a command, fails. With CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN = 1, `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` read 1 after the SW_RST release: SW_RST "Clears the internal state (not registers)" (`spi_host.hjson:213`), so OUTPUT_EN stays set, and OUTPUT_EN is "Enable the SPI host output buffers for the sck, csb, and sd lines." (`spi_host.hjson:222`). A host that turns off the chip-select or clock output enable after the abort fails. Controls: the last pad sample before the SW_RST write shows chip select low and `spi_sd_oe_o` equal to the lane mask of the Tx segment (0001 at x1, 0011 at x2, 1111 at x4), so a data lane is driven when the abort lands; the window has fewer sck cycles than the segment; and one STATUS read after the abort point shows ACTIVE=1, TXQD, RXQD and CMDQD each at least 1 (a finished Rx segment leaves two RXDATA words unread, and a second COMMAND waits behind the Tx segment), and one pad sample before the first CONTROL write that sets OUTPUT_EN reads `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` both 0 (`smu-sep.adoc:275-276`: the host "drives no pad until software sets its `CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN`"), else CTRL-MISSING. The clean frame after the abort is graded by CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-SWRST-ABORT PASS seed=<n> width=x<w> dir=tx words=<k> t=<t> cycles=<got>/<total> cs_low_at_sw_rst=1 pre_cs_n=0 pre_sd_oe=<0001|0011|1111> expect_pre_sd_oe=<same> sd_oe=0 expect_sd_oe=0 cs_n=1 pre_active=1 pre_txqd=<a> pre_rxqd=2 pre_cmdqd=1 txqd=0 rxqd=0 cmdqd=0 output_en=1 cs_oe=1 sck_oe=1 expect_cs_oe=1 expect_sck_oe=1 control_pre_enable_cs_oe=0 control_pre_enable_sck_oe=0`.
|CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME
|Every frame of each width (the legs above, the data frames and the clean frame after the abort), through one scoreboard per width (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`, `expect_frame`): the Tx bytes queued equal the wire decode; the Rx bytes the responder drove equal the wire decode and the RXDATA bytes the DUT delivered (Rules of this section, Lane data). A frame with no expectation, or an expectation with no frame, fails. Evidence: one `SPI-SB FRAME` line per frame and `CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME PASS seed=<n> frames=<n> bytes=<n>` per width.
|CHK-SPI-LANE-DATA
|One line per width with `width=` and the Tx and Rx frame and byte counts, after CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME of the width passed. Control: at least one Tx and one Rx frame, else the leaf fails.
|CHK-SPI-SESSION-FRAMES
|Per monitor session: every frame the monitor saw carries one scoreboard expectation, and the session has at least one frame (the difference of the monitor tally across the session) and one graded frame. A frame cut by an abort while the monitor runs has no expectation and fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-SESSION-FRAMES PASS seed=<n> session=<k> width=x<w> first=<i> frames=<n> tally=<n> graded=<n> unexpected=[]`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The TX and RX depths are found at run time and graded against 73 TX words and 64 RX words (CHK-SPI-DEPTH-TX, CHK-SPI-DEPTH-RX); the words that the RX path returns past RXFULL, CMDQD reaching 4 and the head-segment count of CMDQD are logged and not graded. RXWM at RXQD equal to RX_WATERMARK is not graded. A watermark of 0x7F against the reset value of RX_WATERMARK is not graded, because the RX depth is not stated. TXQD and RXQD are not compared while ACTIVE=1. The values read from RXDATA are graded by CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME. `spi_cs_oe_o` and `spi_sck_oe_o` after a SW_RST abort are graded by CHK-SPI-SWRST-ABORT. The open tests `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` and `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test` own their TX watermark cells; the TX cells at watermark 1, a mid value and the TX depth are controls here and are not graded. TXQD and RXQD at idle are logged and not graded. Overflow and underflow are graded in entry 4.10.

'''''

[[sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test_leg]]
===== 4.10 `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` (extension)

*Phase*: 3 (extends an open test)

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`) with `+skip_fuse_sense`, as the open test, 3 seeds, the seed count of the open test entry; tool: both (Verilator and VCS); the entry of the open test in `spi.toml`, with no testlist entry added

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` for the register frontdoor over `s_axi` (CPU-LSU splice, approved), including 8-byte writes with a chosen strobe and the response code of every access. A test-side sampler reads the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` on each core clock (approved). `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` index 13 is PIC source 14 (approved). No checker needs a proposed probe. No inject. No check is blocked.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. RANDCFG: INTR_ENABLE of the L1 state reads, the source-state order and the masked kind. RAND-REP: the rest. Dimensions: one EVENT_ENABLE value (a subset of TXEMPTY, TXWM, RXFULL and RXWM) at each true state, INTR_ENABLE of the L1 state reads (0 to 3), the order of the four INTR_ENABLE values of the line walk, the kind order, the command kind of the accumulation and of the write of 0 (CSIDINVAL, CMDINVAL or CMDBUSY), the accumulation depth (2 to 3), a 6-bit clear mask (at least one injected bit inside the mask and at least one outside), the pair order, the strobe order, the data words (implemented bits only, unique per word, different from the register content), the out-of-range CSID value v (2 to 0xFFFFFFFE, uniform), the SW_RST point inside a segment, the extra TX words (1 to 4) and the queued command count n (1 to 3). `reseed = 3` samples other orders, enables, masks, command kinds, points and data. Every seed runs each source level change with its EVENT_ENABLE bit at 0 and at 1, the 16 (INTR_ENABLE, INTR_STATE) cells of the line walk (register accesses only), the five error kinds, CSIDINVAL at v (graded) and at CSID=1 (logged only), and all 12 strobe cells. The write of 0 runs over the drawn command kind with UNDERFLOW and OVERFLOW, so the `w0_on_set` cells of the command kinds in `cp_clear_form` depend on the seed and close on a merge of regressions (<<sep-fcov-rand,SEP_FCOV>>).

*Objective*: This entry extends the open test `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`. The open test grades the reset values and the read-write walk, INTR_STATE.ERROR set by INTR_TEST and cleared by write one, SPI_EVENT set by the INTR_TEST force, PIC source 14 against INTR_ENABLE for both sources, each ERROR_STATUS kind set by its own trigger and cleared by write one, TXWM at two TX_WATERMARK values, one SPIEN hold window with OUTPUT_EN=1, the SW_RST FIFO check, and a zero-strobe half on the CONTROL register. Every cell of the open test stays as a control, and the open test keeps the grading of those cells. The four legs below add only the new cells.

* Leg L1, event sources and interrupt line (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/spi_controller/regs/gen/adoc/spi_controller.adoc`, INTR_STATE, INTR_ENABLE, EVENT_ENABLE and STATUS registers; `hw/sys/sep/doc/spi.adoc`, Features; `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map; later cites in this entry use the file names only). INTR_STATE.SPI_EVENT follows the OR of the levels of the sources that EVENT_ENABLE selects. The level of IDLE is STATUS.ACTIVE low. The level of READY, TXEMPTY, RXFULL, TXWM and RXWM is the matching STATUS bit. INTR_ENABLE gates only the line into PIC source 14.
* Leg L2, error latch (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/spi_controller/regs/gen/adoc/spi_controller.adoc`, ERROR_ENABLE, ERROR_STATUS, INTR_STATE and STATUS.READY; `hw/sys/sep/doc/spi.adoc`, Features). Each injected condition with its ERROR_ENABLE bit set sets the latched INTR_STATE.ERROR. A clear of ERROR_STATUS leaves INTR_STATE.ERROR set. A kind injected with its ERROR_ENABLE bit clear sets its own ERROR_STATUS bit and leaves INTR_STATE.ERROR at 0: "The ERROR_STATUS register will continue to report all violations even if a particular class of error event has been disabled." (`theory_of_operation.md:606`, tenstorrent/tt-opentitan@d1ce369cb); "Clearing the bit corresponding bit in the ERROR_ENABLE register in the suppresses interrupts for that class of error event" (`theory_of_operation.md:605`); INTR_STATE.ERROR is "Latched event. An enabled error event or an `INTR_TEST` write sets it." (`spi.adoc:79`). Accumulated kinds OR into ERROR_STATUS, and a write of 1 clears only the written bits. A COMMAND written while READY=0 sets CMDBUSY also when no segment is active.
* Leg L3, 8-byte writes (`hw/sys/sep/doc/spi.adoc`, Features; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/spi_controller/regs/gen/adoc/spi_controller.adoc`, ERROR_STATUS). An 8-byte AXI write whose strobes select one 4-byte half lands that half, leaves the other half unchanged and returns OKAY. A half with all strobes clear issues no TL-UL request and queues no TXDATA word. A forwarded half returns an error (`spi.adoc`, Features), so BRESP OKAY and an unchanged TXQD grade that the bridge does not forward the half.
* Leg L4, SW_RST and SPIEN (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/spi_controller/regs/gen/adoc/spi_controller.adoc`, CONTROL and STATUS). CONTROL.SW_RST reads back 1 until firmware writes 0, and clears the FIFOs and the core state but not the registers. CONTROL.SPIEN=0 holds queued commands off the pad, and SPIEN=1 runs them in order.

*Test Coverage*: This entry extends `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`. Only the new cells below are graded here. The open test keeps every cell listed in the Objective. L1: SPI_EVENT from real sources (TXEMPTY, TXWM, RXWM, RXFULL, IDLE, READY) for EVENT_ENABLE 0xF and a drawn EVENT_ENABLE value at each true state, each source level change with its EVENT_ENABLE bit at 0 and at 1, INTR_ENABLE 0 to 3 with an enabled twin, the line walk of INTR_ENABLE 0 to 3 against the INTR_STATE cells none, error, event and both (11 graded cells; the five cells that the open test grades with the same stimulus are controls), the mask step and the level legs for IDLE and READY. L2: the five kinds CSIDINVAL, CMDINVAL, UNDERFLOW, OVERFLOW and CMDBUSY singly (the INTR_STATE.ERROR latch cells; the own-bit and clear cells stay with the open test) and accumulated (at most one command kind, injected first), with 6-bit clear masks that have a hit bit and a kept bit, a write of 0 with the drawn command kind set together with UNDERFLOW and OVERFLOW, CMDBUSY with ACTIVE=1 and with ACTIVE=0, and one kind injected with its ERROR_ENABLE bit clear (own ERROR_STATUS bit set, INTR_STATE.ERROR 0; CHK-SPI-ERR-MASK). L2 also grades the own-bit value of CSIDINVAL at a drawn CSID from 2 to 0xFFFFFFFE and logs it at CSID=1 with `OBS-SPI-CSID1`, where two statements of the OpenTitan theory of operation differ (`theory_of_operation.md:205` and `:597`; <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>); the open test drives CSID=0xFFFFFFFF. L3: four register pairs (0x00, 0x18, 0x28 and 0x30) with strobes 0x0F, 0xF0 and 0xFF (12 cells), including the TXDATA half. L4: SW_RST at a drawn point of a segment with non-reset register values, and SPIEN=0 with 1 to 3 queued commands followed by SPIEN=1. Functional coverage: `sep_spi_interrupt_cg` (`cp_event_aggregation`, `cp_intr_test`), `sep_spi_misc_rand_cg` (`cp_event_src_x_en_x_cond`, `cp_intr_en_x_state`, `cp_swrst_midseg`; graded on a merge of regressions: `cp_clear_form`), `sep_spi_error_status_cg` (`cp_error_raised_cleared`, `cp_intr_error_latch`, `cp_underflow_cell`, `cp_overflow_cell`, `cp_zero_strobe_cell`, `cp_csid_out_of_range`), `sep_spi_illegal_access_cg` (`cp_cmdbusy_cell`, `cp_spien0_cmd_write`), `sep_periph_reset_state_cg` (`cp_sw_rst_domain`, `cp_sw_rst_hold`), `sep_spi_pad_interface_cg` (`cp_spien_hold_run`) and `sep_spi_fifo_status_cg` (`cp_ready_x_active`, `cp_depth_counter`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw INTR_ENABLE (I), the EVENT_ENABLE value of each true state, the line walk order, the state order, the kind order, the command kind of the accumulation, the accumulation depth d, the clear mask, the masked kind, the pair order, the strobe order, the data words, the out-of-range CSID v, the SW_RST point t, the extra TX words and n from the seed. Every wait in the steps below is a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check that the step feeds. A segment is done when the sampler has shown chip select low and then high (for a chain with CSAAT=1, high after the last segment) and a later STATUS read shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0. Every wait for a segment below is this bounded wait, so a STATUS read taken before the command starts does not end it. Before every COMMAND write the test polls STATUS until READY=1 (bounded), except the last COMMAND of each CMDBUSY injection, which is written while READY=0. A "state read" samples STATUS, INTR_STATE (SPI_EVENT and ERROR) and PIC source 14 with INTR_ENABLE=I, then writes INTR_ENABLE=3 and samples PIC source 14 again (the enabled twin), then restores INTR_ENABLE=I. The SW_RST helper writes CONTROL.SW_RST=1 with the other fields kept, polls STATUS until TXEMPTY=1 and RXEMPTY=1, then writes CONTROL.SW_RST=0. The SPI clean state writes 1 to every bit of ERROR_STATUS and to INTR_STATE.ERROR, writes CSID=0, and runs the SW_RST helper.
. Start from the harness reset state. Write CONTROL with SPIEN=1, OUTPUT_EN=1 and SW_RST=0, CONFIGOPTS with CLKDIV 1 and the other fields 0, and CSID=0. ERROR_ENABLE keeps its reset value 0x1F. Read INTR_STATE.ERROR; it reads 0 (control for the latch).
. L1, quiet states. Steps 3 and 4 run in two passes. Pass A writes EVENT_ENABLE=0xF (TXEMPTY, TXWM, RXFULL and RXWM) before each set-up and pass B writes EVENT_ENABLE=0; each pass writes its value again before every SPI clean state that ends a state. The RXFULL-only sequence of step 6 also runs a second time with EVENT_ENABLE=0 in place of RXFULL. So each level change of TXEMPTY, TXWM, RXFULL and RXWM in a set-up or a clean state happens once with the bit of the source at 1 and once at 0. In pass B and in the second RXFULL run every state read expects SPI_EVENT=0. Run the SPI clean state. Write the EVENT_ENABLE value of the pass and INTR_ENABLE=I. State F: write TX_WATERMARK=1 and RX_WATERMARK=2, write 2 TXDATA words, and poll STATUS until TXQD=2 with ACTIVE=0. STATUS shows TXEMPTY=0, TXWM=0, RXFULL=0 and RXWM=0. Take a state read. Expected SPI_EVENT=0.
. L1, true states in the drawn order. Each state starts from the SPI clean state. State T1 (TXEMPTY): TX_WATERMARK=0 and TXQD=0. State T2 (TXWM): TX_WATERMARK=2 and 1 TXDATA word. State T3 (RXWM): TX_WATERMARK=0, 1 TXDATA word, RX_WATERMARK=2 and an Rx segment of 12 bytes, which leaves RXQD=3. State T4 (TXEMPTY and TXWM together): TX_WATERMARK=2 and TXQD=0. For T3, wait until the Rx segment is done and a STATUS read shows RXQD=3 with ACTIVE=0. Read STATUS and require the named levels, else CTRL-MISSING. In pass A, take a state read with EVENT_ENABLE=0xF, then write the drawn value of the state to EVENT_ENABLE and take a second state read; in pass B take one state read. Expected SPI_EVENT equals the OR, over the bits that EVENT_ENABLE holds at the read, of the named levels, so a read with no selected source true expects 0.
. L1, TXEMPTY-only sequence. Run the SPI clean state. Write EVENT_ENABLE=TXEMPTY only and TX_WATERMARK=0. State read: SPI_EVENT=1. Mask step: write EVENT_ENABLE=0 while STATUS shows TXEMPTY=1, take a state read (SPI_EVENT=0), restore EVENT_ENABLE=TXEMPTY. Write 1 TXDATA word; state read (SPI_EVENT=0). Write COMMAND for a Tx segment of 4 bytes (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 2, CSAAT=0), wait until the segment is done, and take a state read (SPI_EVENT=1).
. L1, RXFULL-only sequence. Run the SPI clean state. Write EVENT_ENABLE=RXFULL only. State read: SPI_EVENT=0. Write COMMAND for an Rx segment of 1032 bytes (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 1, CSAAT=0) and read no RXDATA. Poll STATUS until RXFULL=1 and RXSTALL=1, and take a state read (SPI_EVENT=1). Read RXDATA until the segment is done and RXEMPTY=1, each read after a STATUS read that shows RXEMPTY=0. State read: SPI_EVENT=0.
. L1, IDLE level leg. Run the SPI clean state. Write EVENT_ENABLE=IDLE only. State read: ACTIVE=0 and SPI_EVENT=1. Stall a Tx head segment: write one TXDATA word, then COMMAND for a Tx segment of 8 bytes (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 2, CSAAT=0). The core sends the queued word and stalls inside the segment. Poll STATUS until ACTIVE=1 and TXSTALL=1, and take a state read (SPI_EVENT=0). Write one more TXDATA word, wait until the segment is done, and take a state read (SPI_EVENT=1).
. L1, READY level leg. Run the SPI clean state. Write EVENT_ENABLE=READY only. State read: READY=1 and SPI_EVENT=1. Stall a Tx head segment as in step 7 (one TXDATA word, then COMMAND for an 8-byte Tx segment), poll STATUS until ACTIVE=1 and TXSTALL=1, then write dummy COMMANDs (DIRECTION 0, LEN 0, CSAAT=0), each after a STATUS read that shows READY=1, until a STATUS read shows READY=0. When READY=1 after 16 accepted writes, log CTRL-MISSING and fail. In the READY-low window take a state read with EVENT_ENABLE=READY (SPI_EVENT=0), write EVENT_ENABLE=0 and take a state read (SPI_EVENT=0), then restore EVENT_ENABLE=READY. Write one TXDATA word, wait until ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0, and take a state read (READY=1 and SPI_EVENT=1). Then the line walk: for each INTR_ENABLE value 0 to 3 in the drawn order, run the SPI clean state, write EVENT_ENABLE=0 and the INTR_ENABLE value, and read the line in four states in this order: none (INTR_STATE.ERROR and SPI_EVENT clear), error (after a write of INTR_TEST.ERROR=1), both (after a write of EVENT_ENABLE=TXEMPTY with TXQD=0), event (after a write of 1 to INTR_STATE.ERROR), and none again after a write of EVENT_ENABLE=0. Each line read samples INTR_STATE and PIC source 14 (CHK-SPI-LINE). The five cells that the open test grades with the same stimulus, (INTR_ENABLE 0, 1 and 2, none) and (INTR_ENABLE 0 and 1, error), are controls: each logs `CTL-SPI-LINE LOG seed=<n> intr_en=<v> state=<s> pic14=<got> expect=<exp>`, a mismatch fails the leaf, and no CHK-SPI-LINE line is printed for it.
. L2, single kinds (Phase A). For each of the five kinds in the drawn order, run the SPI clean state, write INTR_ENABLE=0 and EVENT_ENABLE=0, read ERROR_STATUS and INTR_STATE.ERROR (both 0), then inject the kind as error injection, with every COMMAND write after a STATUS read that shows READY=1 (the last COMMAND of the CMDBUSY kind is the one write that skips it): point CSID register write then COMMAND, kind out-of-range CSID, CSID=v, then a dummy COMMAND, then CSID=0, expected ERROR_STATUS.CSIDINVAL=1; point COMMAND write with CONTROL.SPIEN=0, kind SPEED=3, expected CMDINVAL=1, then the SW_RST helper, a poll until CMDQD=0 and the write of the earlier CONTROL value, so that the core never runs a reserved speed; point RXDATA read with RXEMPTY=1 and ACTIVE=0, kind read of an empty RX FIFO (the value is not graded), expected UNDERFLOW=1; point TXDATA write with TXFULL=1, kind write to a full TX FIFO (write TXDATA after each STATUS read that shows TXFULL=0 until one shows TXFULL=1, then once more), expected OVERFLOW=1; point COMMAND write while READY=0 with ACTIVE=1, kind a COMMAND written while the head segment is held in a TXSTALL and the queue is full (stall a Tx head segment as in step 7, poll STATUS until ACTIVE=1 and TXSTALL=1, write dummy COMMANDs while STATUS shows READY=1 until it shows READY=0, then write one more COMMAND), expected CMDBUSY=1. Each expected response also includes INTR_STATE.ERROR=1. The CSIDINVAL kind runs twice: with the drawn out-of-range value v (graded) and with CSID=1 (logged only). Before the first CSIDINVAL injection, with CSID=0, write a dummy COMMAND, wait until the segment is done and read ERROR_STATUS (0). `sep_io` has one chip select (`spi.adoc`, Features: `NUM_CS = 1`) and the core asserts `cio_csb_o[CSID]` for a command (`spi_host.hjson`, CSID), so 0 is the only valid CSID. At NumCS=1 the OpenTitan theory of operation says "However in this case the CSID value is ignored." (`theory_of_operation.md:205`) and "Submitting a command segment to an invalid CSID (one larger or equal to `NumCS`) will trigger a ERROR_STATUS.CSIDINVAL event." (`theory_of_operation.md:597`), so CSID=1 has no single stated result (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). Every value from 2 to 0xFFFFFFFF is larger than NumCS and selects no chip-select line, so the graded leg takes `theory_of_operation.md:597`. The open test drives 0xFFFFFFFF.
. For each kind of step 9, poll ERROR_STATUS until the kind's own bit reads 1 and log that read (CMDBUSY 0x01, OVERFLOW 0x02, UNDERFLOW 0x04, CMDINVAL 0x08, CSIDINVAL 0x10). For the CSIDINVAL kind at v, CHK-SPI-CSID-RANGE grades that it equals 0x10 alone; at CSID=1 the leaf logs the read as `OBS-SPI-CSID1 LOG seed=<n> csid=0x1 err_status=0x<g> intr_error=<e> active=<a> cmdqd=<q> reason=<text>` and grades nothing, because `theory_of_operation.md:205` and `:597` give CSID=1 no single result; for the other kinds the open test grades that it equals that bit alone. Read INTR_STATE.ERROR (1). Write 1 to that ERROR_STATUS bit; ERROR_STATUS reads 0 (a control, graded by the open test) and INTR_STATE.ERROR still reads 1. Write 1 to INTR_STATE.ERROR; it reads 0. After the CMDBUSY kind, write one TXDATA word and wait until ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0. Then run the SPI clean state.
. L2, accumulation (Phase B). An error bit must be cleared before any further command is issued (`spi_host.hjson`, ERROR_STATUS), so each accumulation holds at most one of the command kinds CSIDINVAL, CMDINVAL and CMDBUSY and injects it first. The later kinds are UNDERFLOW and OVERFLOW, which issue no command. Run the SPI clean state. Inject d kinds as in step 9, without clearing ERROR_STATUS: the drawn command kind first, then the first d - 1 of UNDERFLOW and OVERFLOW in the drawn order. Run the SW_RST helper after each injection (it clears the internal state and not the registers; the READY=1 poll precedes each COMMAND write that follows it), so that no kind starts from the queue or FIFO state of an earlier kind. The CSIDINVAL injection of this phase uses the drawn value v. Write CSID=0 after it. After the last injection, read ERROR_STATUS as old; it equals the OR of the bits of the injected kinds. Write the drawn 6-bit mask to ERROR_STATUS and read it again; it equals old and not mask. Require `hit` (mask and old) and `kept` (old and not mask) both non-zero, else CTRL-MISSING. Run the SPI clean state. Then inject the drawn command kind, UNDERFLOW and OVERFLOW in this order and in the same way, write 0 to ERROR_STATUS and read it again; it equals the OR of the three bits, because a write of 0 clears no bit. Run the SPI clean state.
. L2, masked kind (Phase C). Write ERROR_ENABLE=0x1F with the bit of the masked kind clear. Before the injection read ERROR_STATUS and INTR_STATE.ERROR (both 0, control) and require that the same kind with its ERROR_ENABLE bit set gave INTR_STATE.ERROR=1 in step 9 (the unmasked twin), else CTRL-MISSING. Inject that kind as in step 9, poll ERROR_STATUS until the own bit of the kind reads 1, and read INTR_STATE.ERROR (0); CHK-SPI-ERR-MASK grades both. Write ERROR_ENABLE=0x1F and run the SPI clean state.
. L2, CMDBUSY with ACTIVE low. Run the SPI clean state. Write CONTROL.SPIEN=0 with ERROR_ENABLE=0x1F. Write dummy COMMANDs (DIRECTION 0, LEN 0, CSAAT=0), each after a STATUS read that shows READY=1, and read ERROR_STATUS after each; CMDBUSY stays 0 (control). Stop at the first STATUS read that shows READY=0 and ACTIVE=0. When READY=1 after 16 accepted writes, log CTRL-MISSING and fail (CMDQD is 4 bits wide). Point: COMMAND write while READY=0 with ACTIVE=0. Kind: one more COMMAND. Expected response: ERROR_STATUS.CMDBUSY=1. After that read, write 1 to ERROR_STATUS.CMDBUSY, so that no error bit is set when the queued commands run. Write CONTROL.SPIEN=1, wait until the sampler has shown one chip-select-low window per accepted command and STATUS shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0, and run the SPI clean state.
. L3, strobe cells. For each of the four pairs in the drawn order and each strobe 0x0F, 0xF0 and 0xFF in the drawn order, run the SPI clean state. Write CONTROL (SPIEN=1, SW_RST=0), CONFIGOPTS=0, CSID=0, ERROR_ENABLE=0x1F, INTR_ENABLE=0 and EVENT_ENABLE=0 with 4-byte writes. Apply the condition of the pair table below, so that every write-one-to-clear half holds a set bit that the data clears and every read-write half holds a value that differs from the data. The data of a write-one-to-clear half carries every implemented bit. For the pair at 0x30 the condition is error injection as the CMDINVAL kind of step 9: point COMMAND write with CONTROL.SPIEN=0, kind reserved SPEED encoding (SPEED=3, DIRECTION 0, LEN 0, CSAAT=0), expected response ERROR_STATUS.CMDINVAL=1, then the SW_RST helper, a poll until CMDQD=0 and the write of the earlier CONTROL value; poll ERROR_STATUS until CMDINVAL reads 1 and require the value 0x08 before the strobed write.
.
