For each strobe cell, read both registers of the pair (TXDATA is not read), issue one 8-byte write at the aligned pair offset with the drawn data words and the strobe, and read both registers again. Point: 8-byte write on the register bus. Kind: strobe 0x00 on the unselected half of a 0x0F or 0xF0 beat. Expected response: OKAY with no write on that half. A beat with all eight strobes clear is not driven, because the AXI master emits no such beat. When ERROR_STATUS is not one of the pair, read it and log ACCESSINVAL.
. For the pair at 0x28, with ACTIVE=0: after strobe 0x0F or 0xFF, poll STATUS until TXQD=1; after strobe 0xF0, read STATUS (TXQD=0 and TXEMPTY=1). Then after a STATUS read that shows READY=1 write COMMAND for a Tx segment of 4 bytes (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 2, CSAAT=0), write one TXDATA word after strobe 0xF0 only, and wait until the segment is done. Read STATUS; TXEMPTY=1 and TXQD=0, so the empty half queues no spurious word. Run the SW_RST helper after the cell.
. L4, SW_RST. Run the SPI clean state. Write drawn non-reset values, each different from its reset value, to CONFIGOPTS, ERROR_ENABLE, EVENT_ENABLE and CONTROL (SPIEN=1, OUTPUT_EN=1, a non-zero TX_WATERMARK and an RX_WATERMARK other than 0x7F). Read each register and log it. Write COMMAND for a Tx segment (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 2, CSAAT=0) and write the words that it consumes plus the drawn 1 to 4 extra TXDATA words, each after a STATUS read that shows TXFULL=0. Write CONTROL.SW_RST=1 (other fields kept) when the sampler shows t sck cycles of the segment, with t drawn from 1 to half of the segment's sck cycles.
. Read STATUS immediately before the SW_RST write and log ACTIVE, TXQD and RXQD. When ACTIVE=0 and TXQD+RXQD=0, log CTRL-MISSING and fail. After the write, read CONTROL; SW_RST reads 1. Poll STATUS until TXEMPTY=1 and RXEMPTY=1. Read CONTROL again; the other CONTROL fields hold the non-reset values (read before the release write, which rewrites every CONTROL field). Write CONTROL.SW_RST=0 with the other fields kept. Read STATUS: ACTIVE, TXQD and RXQD read 0. Read CONFIGOPTS, ERROR_ENABLE and EVENT_ENABLE; they hold the non-reset values. Poll STATUS until READY=1, write one clean command (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 0, a drawn LEN, CSAAT=0), wait until the segment is done, and take its sck cycle count.
. L4, SPIEN. Run the SPI clean state. Draw n distinct LEN values. With SPIEN=1 and ACTIVE=0, run one dummy segment (DIRECTION 0, the largest drawn LEN, CSAAT=0) and take its chip-select-low time T. Write CONTROL.SPIEN=0. Write n dummy COMMANDs with the drawn LEN values (DIRECTION 0, CSAAT=0), each after a STATUS read that shows READY=1, and stop when READY=0. Read STATUS; log CMDQD and the number of accepted commands, which is at least 1, else CTRL-MISSING.
. Sample the pad ports for 4*T core clocks after the last accepted COMMAND, a window that is a test parameter. Write CONTROL.SPIEN=1. Wait until the sampler has shown one chip-select-low window per accepted command and STATUS shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0. Take the chip-select-low windows and their sck cycle counts in order.

[cols="1,3,5",options="header"]
|===
|Offset |Pair (leg L3) |Condition before each cell
|0x00 |INTR_STATE, INTR_ENABLE |INTR_STATE.ERROR set through INTR_TEST.
|0x18 |CONFIGOPTS, CSID |None.
|0x28 |TXDATA, ERROR_ENABLE |ACTIVE=0 and both FIFOs empty. The TXDATA half is graded by TXQD.
|0x30 |ERROR_STATUS, EVENT_ENABLE |ERROR_STATUS.CMDINVAL set by the COMMAND injection of this step (SPEED=3).
|===

The offsets come from `spi_controller.adoc`, and TXDATA at 0x28 and RXDATA at 0x24 from `spi_controller.rdl`. The pairs at 0x08 (INTR_TEST, ALERT_TEST) and 0x20 (COMMAND, RXDATA) are not used, because a write has an effect that the register description leaves open or launches a command. The pair at 0x10 (CONTROL, STATUS) is not used, because the open test grades its CONTROL half and the response to a write to the read-only STATUS is not stated. STATUS is not compared with a literal (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SPI-EVENT
|At each state read, SPI_EVENT equals the OR of the levels of the selected sources, also with INTR_ENABLE=0, equals 0 after the mask step, and follows the IDLE and READY levels in their legs (`spi_controller.adoc`, INTR_STATE.SPI_EVENT, EVENT_ENABLE; `spi.adoc`, Features). A source that is not selected never sets SPI_EVENT, and a stuck SPI_EVENT fails. Anchor: INTR_STATE and STATUS readback (approved frontdoor), with the source levels named by the stimulus. Control for "SPI_EVENT stays 0": the same seed holds a state with a selected source true and reads SPI_EVENT=1 (the TXEMPTY-only sequence), and the READY-low state with EVENT_ENABLE.READY=0 is read next to a READY-low state with EVENT_ENABLE.READY=1. A read pair taken while STATUS shows another level than the one named fails with CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-EVENT PASS seed=<n> ev_en=0x<e> src=<ids> level=<bits> event=<got> expect=<exp> mask_step=<0 or 1>`.
|CHK-SPI-LINE
|PIC source 14 equals (SPI_EVENT and INTR_ENABLE.SPI_EVENT) or (ERROR and INTR_ENABLE.ERROR) (`interrupts.adoc`, SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map; `spi.adoc`, Features). A line stuck low, stuck high, or not gated by INTR_ENABLE fails. Anchor: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` index 13 (approved). Control: the twin window with INTR_ENABLE=3 shows source 14 = 1 for a state with SPI_EVENT=1, so a stuck-low line fails also when the drawn INTR_ENABLE is 0. The line walk reads all 16 (INTR_ENABLE, INTR_STATE) cells on every seed and grades the 11 cells that the open test does not grade; the other five print `CTL-SPI-LINE LOG` (step 8). Evidence: `CHK-SPI-LINE PASS seed=<n> intr_en=<v> state=<s> pic14=<got> expect=<exp> twin_pic14=<t>` per state read, and `CHK-SPI-LINE PASS seed=<n> walk=1 intr_en=<v> state=<none\|error\|both\|event> pic14=<got> expect=<exp>` per graded line read of the walk.
|CHK-SPI-ERR-KIND
|Each real kind, injected with its ERROR_ENABLE bit set, sets INTR_STATE.ERROR, INTR_STATE.ERROR stays 1 after the ERROR_STATUS write-one-to-clear, and it clears only by its own write-one-to-clear (`spi_controller.adoc`, ERROR_STATUS, INTR_STATE; `spi.adoc`, Features). A host that does not latch the error, that clears the latch with the ERROR_STATUS write, or that keeps it after the INTR_STATE write fails. Anchor: INTR_STATE readback (approved frontdoor). Control for the latch: INTR_STATE.ERROR reads 0 before the first injection, and reads 1 immediately before the ERROR_STATUS clear, so the held read is a held latch. The own-bit rule and the ERROR_STATUS clear of each kind are logged as controls and graded by the open test. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-ERR-KIND PASS seed=<n> kind=<k> intr_error=1 after_status_w1c=1 after_intr_w1c=0 control_intr_error_before=0` and the logged `err_status=0x<v>`.
|CHK-SPI-CSID-RANGE
|A COMMAND with the drawn value v (2 to 0xFFFFFFFE) sets ERROR_STATUS to 0x10 (CSIDINVAL alone) and INTR_STATE.ERROR to 1 (`spi.adoc`, Features: `NUM_CS = 1`; `spi_host.hjson`, CSID, ERROR_ENABLE.CSIDINVAL and ERROR_STATUS.CSIDINVAL: v exceeds NumCS and selects no chip-select line). A host that accepts a mid-range CSID, or that sets another error bit, fails. CSID=1 is not graded: `theory_of_operation.md:205` ("the CSID value is ignored") and `theory_of_operation.md:597` ("one larger or equal to `NumCS`") give it no single result (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), and the leaf logs it as `OBS-SPI-CSID1 LOG`. Anchor: ERROR_STATUS and INTR_STATE readback (approved frontdoor); the expected value comes from the injected kind. Control: a COMMAND with CSID=0 after the SPI clean state leaves ERROR_STATUS at 0, so a host that flags every CSID fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-CSID-RANGE PASS seed=<n> csid=0x<v> err_status=0x<g> expect=0x10 intr_error=1 control_csid0_err_status=0x00`.
|CHK-SPI-ERR-MASK
|The accumulated ERROR_STATUS equals the OR of the bits of the injected kinds, and after the drawn clear mask it equals (old and not mask) (`spi_controller.adoc`, ERROR_STATUS). A clear that does nothing, or one that clears every bit, fails. Anchor: ERROR_STATUS readback (approved frontdoor). Control: `hit` (mask and old) and `kept` (old and not mask) are both non-zero, else CTRL-MISSING. With the drawn command kind, UNDERFLOW and OVERFLOW set, a write of 0 leaves ERROR_STATUS at the OR of the bits of the three injected kinds; the expected value comes from the injected kinds, not from a read-back. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-ERR-MASK PASS seed=<n> kinds=<list> old=0x<o> mask=0x<m> hit=0x<h> kept=0x<k> got=0x<g> expect=0x<e>` and `CHK-SPI-ERR-MASK PASS seed=<n> zero_write=1 cmd_kind=<k> got=0x<g> expect=0x<OR of the three kind bits>`. A kind injected with its ERROR_ENABLE bit clear sets its own ERROR_STATUS bit and leaves INTR_STATE.ERROR at 0 (`theory_of_operation.md:605` and `:606`; `spi.adoc:79`). A host that suppresses the status bit with the enable, or that raises the interrupt, fails. Control: the bit and INTR_STATE.ERROR read 0 before the injection, and the same kind with its ERROR_ENABLE bit set gave INTR_STATE.ERROR=1 (CHK-SPI-ERR-KIND), else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-ERR-MASK PASS seed=<n> masked=1 kind=<k> error_enable=0x<e> err_status=0x<g> own_bit=0x<b> intr_error=0 expect_intr_error=0 control_err_status_before=0x00 control_intr_error_before=0 control_twin_intr_error=1`.
|CHK-SPI-CMDBUSY0
|A COMMAND written while READY=0 and ACTIVE=0 sets CMDBUSY (`spi_controller.adoc`, ERROR_STATUS.CMDBUSY). A host that sets CMDBUSY only while a segment is active fails. Anchor: ERROR_STATUS and STATUS readback (approved frontdoor). Control: the COMMAND writes while READY=1 left CMDBUSY clear. When READY stays 1 after 16 accepted writes, the leaf fails with CTRL-MISSING (CMDQD is 4 bits wide, `spi_controller.adoc`, STATUS.CMDQD). Evidence: `CHK-SPI-CMDBUSY0 PASS seed=<n> writes=<w> error_enable=0x1F ready=0 active=0 cmdbusy=1 control_cmdbusy=0`.
|CHK-SPI-STRB
|BRESP is OKAY, the strobed half follows its register type (read-write bits equal the data, write-one-to-clear bits equal old and not data), and the other half keeps its old value (`spi.adoc`, Features). A host that writes both halves, or that returns an error for the empty half, fails. Anchor: BRESP and register readback (approved frontdoor), compared on the implemented writable bits. The TXDATA half is graded in CHK-SPI-STRB-TX. ERROR_STATUS.ACCESSINVAL is logged and not graded, because no stimulus sets it and a read of 0 has no control. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-STRB PASS seed=<n> off=0x<o> strb=0x<s> resp=OKAY lo=0x<lo> hi=0x<hi> expect_lo=0x<e0> expect_hi=0x<e1>`, and `OBS-SPI-STRB-ACCESSINVAL seed=<n> off=0x<o> strb=0x<s> accessinval=<v>`.
|CHK-SPI-STRB-TX
|Strobe 0x0F on the pair at 0x28 raises TXQD by 1. Strobe 0xF0 leaves TXQD unchanged and TXEMPTY set, so a half with no strobes queues no TXDATA word (`spi.adoc`, Features). After the Tx segment that follows the cell, TXEMPTY=1 and TXQD=0, so no spurious word is left in the FIFO. A host that forwards the empty half, or that drops the strobed half, fails. Anchor: STATUS readback with ACTIVE=0 (approved frontdoor). Evidence: `CHK-SPI-STRB-TX PASS seed=<n> strb=0x<s> txqd_before=<a> txqd_after=<b> txempty=<t> txempty_after_segment=1`.
|CHK-SPI-STRB-CTRL
|Control for "the other half is unchanged": strobe 0xFF on each pair changes both halves in the same seed, so an unchanged half in the other cells is a strobe effect and not a dead register. Anchor: register readback and STATUS readback (approved frontdoor). Evidence: `CHK-SPI-STRB-CTRL PASS seed=<n> off=0x<o> strb=0xFF both_changed=1`.
|CHK-SPI-SWRST
|CONTROL.SW_RST reads back 1, then both FIFOs are empty, and after the write of 0 ACTIVE, TXQD and RXQD read 0 while the registers keep the non-reset values (`spi_controller.adoc`, CONTROL.SW_RST). A host that clears the registers, or that keeps a word, fails. Anchor: CONTROL and STATUS readback and the pad ports (approved). Control for the work: before the SW_RST write ACTIVE=1 or TXQD+RXQD is at least 1, else CTRL-MISSING. Control for the registers: each value differs from its reset value. Control for the release: a clean command completes with the expected sck cycle count. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-SWRST PASS seed=<n> t=<t> active_before=<a> txqd_before=<b> rxqd_before=<c> hold_read=1 txempty=1 rxempty=1 active=0 txqd=0 rxqd=0 regs_kept=1 nonreset=<count> clean_cycles=<got>/<exp>`.
|CHK-SPI-SPIEN
|With SPIEN=0, CMDQD equals the accepted commands (at least 1, else CTRL-MISSING) and the pad ports show no chip select fall and no sck edge for 4*T core clocks. After SPIEN=1 the n commands run in order with the expected sck cycle counts (`spi_controller.adoc`, CONTROL.SPIEN). A host that ignores SPIEN, or that reorders the queue, fails. Anchor: STATUS readback and the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` (approved). Control for the hold: a dummy segment with the largest drawn LEN runs with SPIEN=1 in time T, which is below the window, and the same commands run after SPIEN=1. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-SPIEN PASS seed=<n> n=<n> cmdqd=<c> t_seg=<t> window_clk=<w> pad_activity=0 released_in_order=1 cycles=<list>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0. The open checks of the test stay in the pass set.

*Scope limit*: This entry extends `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` and grades only the four legs above; the open test keeps every cell in its Objective, including the own-bit rule and the write-one-to-clear of each ERROR_STATUS kind, which are controls here. Not claimed: the release of a forced SPI_EVENT (`OBS-SPI-EVENT-RELEASE`: no source states INTR_STATE.SPI_EVENT after INTR_TEST is written back to 0), the read value of reserved bits, the read response of TXDATA, partial strobes inside a half, a beat with no strobes, the ACCESSINVAL value (no stimulus sets it), the pairs at 0x08, 0x10 and 0x20, the pad state after a mid-segment SW_RST, the value of READY at the SW_RST release (the control command only waits for it), the STATUS reset value, the depth of the command queue, the pad state during a command with an out-of-range CSID, and the CSIDINVAL response at CSID=1 (`OBS-SPI-CSID1`; <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).

'''''

[[sep_spi_cold_reset_values_rand_test]]
===== 4.11 `sep_spi_cold_reset_values_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`) with real fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`), 3 seeds; tool: VCS (`tools = ["vcs"]`, because the leaf reads state after reset); testlist groups `cpu_stub` and `all`, file `spi.toml`

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_cold_reset_values_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` for the register frontdoor over `s_axi` (CPU-LSU splice, approved). A test-side sampler reads the pad ports `spi_sck_o` and `spi_cs_n_o` on each core clock (approved). `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` shows the release of `sep_reset_n` after `rst_ni` (approved). The testbench drives `rst_ni`. A per-clock sampler reads the output-enable taps `spi_cs_oe_o`, `spi_sck_oe_o` and `spi_sd_oe_o` (approved). `OcahSpiMonitor` (`ocah_spi_vip`, through `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_wire_monitor.py`) and `SepSpiScoreboard` (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`, no flash model) run only from the first CONTROL write after the release to the end of the clean commands, so they never see the frame the reset cuts. No proposed probe. No inject. No check is blocked.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed (RAND-REP). Dimensions: the order of the three lane widths of the mid-segment trials, the `rst_ni` point inside a segment (in sck cycles after the COMMAND), the armed non-reset value of each register (each different from its reset value), the Tx segment length m of each mid-segment trial (1 to 4 words), the extra TX words (1 to 4), and the bytes of the clean Tx command after each mid-segment reset. `reseed = 3` samples other points, values and word counts. Every trial runs on every seed: the idle trial and one mid-segment trial at each of x1, x2 and x4.

*Objective*: `rst_ni` returns every readable SPI register except STATUS to its reset value with the host idle, and returns INTR_STATE, INTR_ENABLE, CONTROL, CONFIGOPTS, ERROR_ENABLE and EVENT_ENABLE to their reset values in the middle of a segment at x1, x2 and x4 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/spi_controller/regs/gen/adoc/spi_controller.adoc`, INTR_ENABLE, CONTROL, CONFIGOPTS, CSID, ERROR_ENABLE, ERROR_STATUS, EVENT_ENABLE and the reset column of each register; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller; later cites in this entry use the file names only). The reset values are 0 for INTR_STATE, INTR_ENABLE, CONFIGOPTS, CSID, ERROR_STATUS and EVENT_ENABLE, 0x7F for CONTROL.RX_WATERMARK with every other CONTROL field 0, and 0x1F for ERROR_ENABLE. No SPI pad is driven from the cold reset until software sets CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN, which resets to 0 (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, "SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment"; `spi_controller.adoc`, CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN). The clean Tx command after a reset follows the lane contract of the Rules of this section (Lane data).

*Test Coverage*: Cold reset with non-reset values armed in INTR_STATE (ERROR set), INTR_ENABLE, CONTROL, CONFIGOPTS, CSID, ERROR_ENABLE, ERROR_STATUS and EVENT_ENABLE, once with the host idle (all eight armed) and once at each of x1, x2 and x4 with `rst_ni` asserted inside a Tx segment of that width with TXQD at least 1 (INTR_STATE, INTR_ENABLE, CONTROL, CONFIGOPTS, ERROR_ENABLE and EVENT_ENABLE armed). The output enables in each mid-segment reset window, and the data of a clean Tx command at the same width after the reset. The open test `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` walks the reset value of every readable SPI register, the open test `sep_reset_mid_transfer_recovery_test` grades a reset in the middle of a CONFIGOPTS access, and <<sep_cpu_reset_retention_test,entry 5.15>> grades CONFIGOPTS after an `rst_ni` pulse. This entry adds the armed non-reset values and the reset in the middle of a segment. CONFIGOPTS stays in the compare as a control. The SW_RST contract is graded in <<sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test_leg,entry 4.10>>. Functional coverage: `sep_spi_misc_rand_cg` (`cp_rst_entry_midseg`) and `sep_spi_lane_abort_chain_cg` (`x_abort_width`, cells `rst_ni` x x1, x2, x4).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw the armed non-reset values, the width order, and per mid-segment trial the `rst_ni` point, the Tx segment length m, the extra TX words and the clean command bytes from the seed. Steps 6 to 9 run once per width in the drawn order, with SPEED 0, 1 or 2 for x1, x2 or x4 in place of SPEED 0. Every wait in the steps below is a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check that the step feeds. A segment is done when the sampler has shown chip select low and then high (for a chain with CSAAT=1, high after the last segment) and a later STATUS read shows ACTIVE=0 and CMDQD=0. Every wait for a segment below is this bounded wait, so a STATUS read taken before the command starts does not end it. Before every COMMAND write the test polls STATUS until READY=1 (bounded).
. Wait for the release of `sep_reset_n` on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` after the harness bring-up. Read ERROR_STATUS and INTR_STATE; both read 0.
. Trial 1, idle. Write CONTROL with SPIEN=1 and OUTPUT_EN=1, and poll STATUS until READY=1. Write COMMAND with SPEED=3 (error injection, point: COMMAND write, kind: reserved SPEED encoding, expected response: ERROR_STATUS.CMDINVAL=1 and INTR_STATE.ERROR=1). Poll ERROR_STATUS until CMDINVAL reads 1 and INTR_STATE until ERROR reads 1. Then write the drawn non-reset values to INTR_ENABLE, CONTROL (SPIEN=1, OUTPUT_EN=1, a non-zero TX_WATERMARK, an RX_WATERMARK other than 0x7F, SW_RST=0), CONFIGOPTS, CSID, ERROR_ENABLE and EVENT_ENABLE. Read all eight registers; each reads a value different from its reset value (the control).
. Assert `rst_ni` and release it. Wait for the release of `sep_reset_n` on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`. Read the eight registers. Each equals its reset value. STATUS is not compared with a literal.
. Write CONTROL with SPIEN=1 and OUTPUT_EN=1, poll STATUS until READY=1, and run a clean command (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 0, a drawn LEN, CSAAT=0). Wait until the segment is done, take its sck cycle count, and read ERROR_STATUS; it reads 0.
. Trial 2, mid-segment. Write INTR_TEST.ERROR=1, so that INTR_STATE.ERROR reads 1. Write the drawn non-reset values to INTR_ENABLE, CONTROL (SPIEN=1, OUTPUT_EN=1, a non-zero TX_WATERMARK, an RX_WATERMARK other than 0x7F, SW_RST=0), CONFIGOPTS, ERROR_ENABLE and EVENT_ENABLE. CSID stays 0, because a segment needs a valid CSID and 0 is the only valid value (`spi.adoc`, Features: `NUM_CS = 1`). ERROR_STATUS stays 0, because the register description does not state that a segment runs while an error is latched. Read the registers; the six armed registers each read a value different from the reset value.
. Write the m words of a Tx segment (m drawn from 1 to 4) and the drawn 1 to 4 extra TXDATA words, each after a STATUS read that shows TXFULL=0, so that TXQD is at least 1 at every `rst_ni` point. No COMMAND drains the TX FIFO during these writes, so at most 8 words are queued, below the default TX depth of 72 words (`spi_host.hjson`, TxDepth). When a STATUS read shows TXFULL=1 before the last word, log CTRL-MISSING and fail. Poll STATUS until READY=1, then write COMMAND for the Tx segment (SPEED 0, DIRECTION 2, CSAAT=0). When the sampler shows t sck cycles of the segment, with t drawn from 1 to half of the segment's sck cycles, read `spi_cs_n_o`; chip select is low (control, else CTRL-MISSING).
. Error injection. Point: the testbench `rst_ni` input. Kind: `rst_ni` asserted inside a segment. Expected response: every listed register at its reset value, and no SPI pad driven. Assert `rst_ni` right after the chip-select read and release it. The output-enable sampler takes the last sample before `rst_ni` falls (control) and every sample from the first clock with `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` = 0 until the release and fuse sense are done, before the first register write (the reset window). Wait for the release of `sep_reset_n` on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`. Read the eight registers; each equals its reset value.
. Start the monitor and a scoreboard. Write CONTROL with SPIEN=1 and OUTPUT_EN=1, poll STATUS until READY=1, and run a clean command as in step 5. Then write the TXDATA words of the seeded clean bytes and run one Tx command of those bytes at the width of the trial. Register the dummy command and the Tx command with `expect_frame`, then stop the monitor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SPI-COLDRST
|After each `rst_ni` pulse, idle and in the middle of a segment, INTR_STATE, INTR_ENABLE, CONFIGOPTS, CSID, ERROR_STATUS and EVENT_ENABLE read 0, CONTROL reads 0x0000_007F, ERROR_ENABLE reads 0x1F, and a clean command then runs with the expected sck cycle count and ERROR_STATUS 0 (`spi_controller.adoc`, register reset values; `reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). A register that keeps its armed value, a reset that skips a register when the segment is active, or a host that stays busy after the reset fails. Anchor: register readback over the frontdoor after the release of `sep_reset_n` on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (approved), and the pad ports for the clean command. Control: before the reset each armed register held a value different from its reset value (`nonreset` counts them: 8 in the idle trial; 6 in the mid-segment trial, where CSID and ERROR_STATUS stay at 0). Control for the mid-segment trial: `spi_cs_n_o` is low when the sampler reads it before `rst_ni` asserts (`cs_low_at_reset=1`), else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-COLDRST PASS seed=<n> trial=<idle or midseg> cs_low_at_reset=<0 or 1> regs=<count> nonreset=<count> mismatch=0 clean_cmd=1 ... width=<x1, x2, x4, or - for idle> data_bytes=<b>`.
|CHK-SPI-OE-RESET
|Per mid-segment trial, every sample of the cold-reset window reads `spi_cs_oe_o`, `spi_sck_oe_o` and `spi_sd_oe_o` all 0, with no X or Z (`smu-sep.adoc`, "SEP SPI Host Pin Assignment": no pad is driven until CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN; `spi_controller.adoc`, CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN resets to 0). A host that keeps a pad driven through the reset, or drives an unknown output enable, fails. The leaf is VCS only, so an X fails here. Anchor: the output-enable taps (approved) and `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`. Controls: the last sample before `rst_ni` falls reads chip select low with `spi_cs_oe_o`, `spi_sck_oe_o` and `spi_sd_oe_o[0]` = 1, the SEP reset asserts in the hold, and the window has samples, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-OE-RESET PASS seed=<n> trial=midseg width=x<w> window_samples=<k> violations=0 xz=0 ... pre_reset_cs_n=0 pre_cs_oe=1 pre_sck_oe=1 pre_sd_oe=<v> sep_reset_n_at_hold_end=0`.
|CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME
|Per mid-segment trial, one scoreboard (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_spi_scoreboard.py`, `expect_frame`): the clean dummy command has LEN+1 sample edges, and the clean Tx command at the width of the trial puts its queued bytes on the lanes (Rules of this section, Lane data). A host that resumes after the reset at another width, loses or repeats a byte, or sends stale words of the cut segment fails. Evidence: `CHK-SPI-SB-FRAME PASS seed=<n> frames=2 bytes=<b>` per trial.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The STATUS reset value is not graded. The pad state inside the reset window is graded for the mid-segment trials only. The mid-segment trial arms six registers and keeps CSID and ERROR_STATUS at 0; both are armed in the idle trial. The open tests `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` and `sep_reset_mid_transfer_recovery_test` own the reset walk and the reset in the middle of a CONFIGOPTS access, and entry 5.15 owns CONFIGOPTS after an `rst_ni` pulse. The SW_RST contract is graded in entry 4.10.

'''''
[[sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test]]
===== 4.12 `sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 4 seeds, Verilator and VCS; testlist groups `cpu_stub` and `all`, file `system.toml`, one testlist entry. The test never asserts `rst_ni`. Leg A runs 16 transfers per run, one per mode cell, each of at most 1 KB.

*Source Module*: `sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip register frontdoor over `s_axi` (also for the SRAM source and destination images); a model of the register text that gives the expected destination image of each cell; an independent SHA-2 library (Python `hashlib`) that gives the digest golden. Approved probes: `dma_busy_probe_o`, and bit 9 of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (PIC source 10, DMA chunk done, `interrupts.adoc`) for the control of CHK-DMA-CHUNKDONE. `dma_axi_req_probe_o` (checkers CHK-DMA-BEAT, CHK-DMA-ADDR, CHK-DMA-DONE-ORDER, CHK-DMA-GO-CHUNKS) and `dma_status_probe_o` (checkers CHK-DMA-CHUNKRISE, CHK-DMA-DONE-ORDER) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. Dimensions and legal ranges: chunk size C (4 to 64 bytes, multiple of 4, for the mode cells; up to 4096 bytes for the geometry leg); chunk count n (1 to 16, with n = 1 at a weight of 1 in 3; n = 1 in a cell with INCREMENT=0 and WRAP=0 on a side; n of 2 or more for the pairs that `sep_dma_basic_test` grades, see Leg A; total at most 16 KB); the order of the 16 mode cells; the width encoding of each mode cell (0, 1 or 2; every seed draws each encoding at least once); the middle single-chunk total of Leg B (64, 256, 1024 or 4096 bytes); order of the configuration writes; source and destination SRAM offsets (footprints with guard words are disjoint and inside BASE to LIMIT); guards of the range-edge leg (4 bytes or more); the region order of each edge cell (source below destination, or the reverse); order of the algorithms (SHA-256, SHA-384, SHA-512); two message lengths per algorithm (one of the ten block-boundary lengths 52, 56, 60, 64, 108, 112, 116, 124, 128 and 132 bytes at a weight of 3 in 4, else a multiple of 4 from 4 to 256 bytes) and the DIGEST_SWAP value of each drawn message; split of a message of L bytes into k equal chunks of one transfer, k drawn from the values 2, 3 and 4 for which L / k is a multiple of 4, so that no chunk has size 0 and a split run always has a later chunk (a message with no such k, for example 4 bytes, has no split form); order of the two DIGEST_SWAP values of a published vector; the algorithm, the length (1024 to 4096 bytes, a multiple of 4) and the DIGEST_SWAP value of one long message; all data. Every drawn offset and guard is a multiple of 4, so both addresses are aligned to every width encoding and have the same alignment (`secure_dma.rdl`, SRC_ADDR_LO, DST_ADDR_LO, TRANSFER_WIDTH). Every drawn size is a multiple of 4, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE is a multiple of CHUNK_DATA_SIZE, and the message and split lengths of the hash trials are multiples of 4. Every seed runs the 16 mode cells, each width encoding, the totals 4 and 16384 bytes, the six edge cells, each algorithm with its published vector at both DIGEST_SWAP values, and the pairs of `sep_dma_basic_test` at n of 2 or more. `reseed = 4` samples four different widths, chunk counts, orders, middle totals, region orders, message lengths, splits, offsets and data sets. The mode x width and mode x chunk-count cells, the wrap restart per width, the middle totals, the region-edge positions and the block-boundary lengths depend on the seed and close on a merge of regressions (<<sep-fcov-rand,SEP_FCOV>>); the 48 mode x width cells take about three regressions of four seeds.

*Objective*: The DMA transfer contract holds in four legs. The open tests `sep_dma_basic_test` and `sep_dma_hash_test` grade the cells listed in each leg. Those cells are controls here.

* Leg A, address modes and widths (`secure_dma.adoc`, TRANSFER_WIDTH, SRC_CONFIG, DST_CONFIG; `dma.adoc`, overview; `fabric.adoc`, Top Level AXI4). INCREMENT=1 advances the address by the transfer width after each transaction. INCREMENT=0 keeps every access at one address. WRAP=1 returns the address to the start at each chunk end. WRAP=0 places the chunks at contiguous ascending addresses. Transactions of the programmed width are issued until TOTAL_DATA_SIZE bytes have moved, each as a single beat. `sep_dma_basic_test` walks the increment, fixed and wrap modes at 4 bytes, and the width encodings with INCREMENT only. This leg adds all 16 source-and-destination cells with three width encodings, destination wrap and single-chunk wrap. `sep_dma_basic_test` grades six pairs of a cell and a width encoding at one shape: INCREMENT=1 with WRAP=0 on both sides at the width encodings 0, 1 and 2 with one chunk; source INCREMENT=0 with WRAP=1 and destination INCREMENT=1 with WRAP=0, and source INCREMENT=1 with WRAP=0 and destination INCREMENT=0 with WRAP=1, each at the width encoding 2 with one chunk; source INCREMENT=1 with WRAP=1 and destination INCREMENT=1 with WRAP=0 at the width encoding 2 with two chunks. This entry runs those six pairs with n of 2 or more (n of 3 or more for the last pair), so no graded run repeats the open shape.
* Leg B, geometry and completion (`secure_dma.adoc`, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE, CHUNK_DATA_SIZE, CONTROL.GO, STATUS). A normal transfer sets DONE, clears GO itself, clears DONE when the next transfer starts, raises no CHUNK_DONE for a single chunk, and raises CHUNK_DONE for a multi-chunk memory-to-memory transfer. `sep_dma_basic_test` copies 16 to 32 bytes, polls DONE and grades that a multi-chunk transfer raises CHUNK_DONE. This leg adds sizes up to 16 KB, the single-chunk negative, DONE and GO auto-clear, and the stop after each non-final chunk of a multi-chunk transfer until the next GO (CHK-DMA-GO-CHUNKS). The zero-size error is graded in <<sep_dma_irq_error_lock_rand_test,entry 4.13>>.
* Leg C, range edges (`secure_dma.adoc`, ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_BASE, ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT, RANGE_VALID). With RANGE_VALID set, a transfer whose source and destination bytes all lie in BASE to LIMIT (LIMIT inclusive) completes. `sep_dma_basic_test` grades RANGE_VALID=0, BASE above LIMIT, one lock and one rejected write. This leg adds the exact edges in both region orders. The lock is graded in entry 4.13.
* Leg D, inline hashing (`dma.adoc`, overview and Completion Reporting; `secure_dma.adoc`, CONTROL, STATUS, SHA2_DIGEST_0, TRANSFER_WIDTH). With inline hashing selected, a transfer chain with INITIAL_TRANSFER on the first transfer gives SHA2_DIGEST_0 words equal to the SHA-2 digest of the bytes. SHA2_DIGEST_VALID clears at the initial transfer and sets when the digest is written. DIGEST_SWAP=1 gives each word in big-endian order without changing the word order. `sep_dma_hash_test` grades SHA-256 on random data, SHA-384 on one 56-byte vector, a two-chunk message and DIGEST_SWAP set against clear. This leg adds SHA-512, block boundaries and SHA2_DIGEST_VALID. The cells that `sep_dma_hash_test` grades are controls: SHA-256 at 56 bytes as one transfer with either DIGEST_SWAP value, SHA-256 at 56 bytes as one transfer of two 28-byte chunks with DIGEST_SWAP=1, and SHA-384 at 56 bytes as one transfer with DIGEST_SWAP=1. The leaf runs them, logs `OBS-DMA-HASH-CTRL`, prints no `CHK-DMA-HASH` or `CHK-DMA-HASH-CHAIN` PASS line for them and does not count them in k. The hash width rule is graded in entry 4.13.

*Test Coverage*: Leg A: the 16 cells of source {INCREMENT, WRAP} x destination {INCREMENT, WRAP} on every seed, each at a drawn width encoding (every encoding on every seed; the 48 mode and width cells close on a merge of regressions) and with a drawn chunk count, destination wrap, single-chunk wrap (same image as WRAP=0), multi-chunk wrap (restart at each chunk start), and INCREMENT=0 with WRAP=0 on a side with one chunk; the six pairs that `sep_dma_basic_test` grades run with n of 2 or more. Leg B: the single-chunk total 4 bytes and a drawn single-chunk total of 64, 256, 1024 or 4096 bytes, one 16384-byte total as four chunks of 4096 bytes, drawn multi-chunk transfers, DONE and GO auto-clear, no CHUNK_DONE for a single chunk, and in Leg A and Leg B one stop per non-final chunk of a multi-chunk transfer (CHK-DMA-GO-CHUNKS). Leg C: BASE and LIMIT exactly on the region edges (low edge, high edge, both edges, both edges with a guard at either end and at both ends), each in a drawn region order. Leg D: SHA-256, SHA-384 and SHA-512 at 4-byte transfers, drawn block-boundary lengths, the published 56-byte and 112-byte vectors, each message as one single-chunk transfer and, where an equal split exists, as one transfer of 2 to 4 equal chunks, both DIGEST_SWAP values, SHA2_DIGEST_VALID, with its clear graded on one long message of 1024 to 4096 bytes per seed; the three cells that `sep_dma_hash_test` grades are controls. The test builds on the open tests `sep_dma_basic_test` and `sep_dma_hash_test`. Only the cells above are graded here. The open owners keep the cells they grade. Functional coverage: `sep_dma_transfer_cg` (`cp_src_x_dst_mode`, `cp_alignment_baseline`, `cp_mode_hash_chunk`), `sep_dma_xfer_rand_cg` (`cp_mode_x_width`, `cp_mode_x_chunkcount`, `cp_wrap_independence`), `sep_dma_endpoint_cg` (`cp_beats_per_txn`, `cp_src_x_dst_endpoint`), `sep_dma_completion_cg` (`cp_go_lifecycle`, `cp_busy_phase`, `cp_done_set`, `cp_done_clear_path`, `cp_chunk_done_class`, `cp_chunk_done_clear`, `cp_chunk_decomp`), `sep_dma_copy_hash_cg` (`cp_hash_opcode`, `cp_chunk`), `sep_dma_hash_cg` (`cp_initial_transfer_ctx`, `cp_digest_valid_lifecycle`, `cp_digest_swap_x_word`, `cp_msg_len_class`), `sep_dma_range_config_cg` (`cp_range_valid_at_go`, `cp_base_limit_rel`, `cp_inrange_pos_x_side`, `cp_unlocked_write`) and `sep_dma_rand_cg` (`cp_len`). Graded on a merge of regressions, because the seed draws their cells: `cp_mode_x_width`, `cp_mode_x_chunkcount`, `cp_wrap_independence`, `cp_inrange_pos_x_side`, `cp_msg_len_class` and the `cp_len` bins `b64` to `b4096`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw every value from the seed and log it in `DRAW` lines. Build the model of the register text and the digest golden from the drawn data. The model never reads a value from the DUT.
. Write ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_BASE = 0x10000000 and ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT = 0x1003FFFF (the SEP SRAM, `memory_map.adoc`) and RANGE_VALID = 1. Write ADDR_SPACE_ID = 0x77 and both ADDR_HI registers = 0. Write 1 to STATUS.DONE and STATUS.CHUNK_DONE. Write INTR_ENABLE with DMA_CHUNK_DONE=1 and the other bits 0, and from this step sample bit 9 of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` at every clock (the control of CHK-DMA-CHUNKDONE). Never write RANGE_REGWEN. Never write a configuration register while STATUS.BUSY reads 1.
. Leg A. For each of the 16 mode cells, in a drawn order and at the drawn width encoding of the cell: fill the source footprint with unique words (index plus drawn bits). Fill the destination footprint and one guard word before and one after it with sentinel words (see Sentinel in the rules of this section). Keep the footprints disjoint and inside BASE to LIMIT.
. Leg A. Write SRC_ADDR_LO, DST_ADDR_LO, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE, CHUNK_DATA_SIZE, TRANSFER_WIDTH, SRC_CONFIG and DST_CONFIG in a drawn order. A side with INCREMENT=0 and WRAP=0 runs with one chunk (TOTAL_DATA_SIZE equals CHUNK_DATA_SIZE). Write CONTROL with OPCODE 0 (COPY), INITIAL_TRANSFER=1 and GO=1.
. Leg A. Poll STATUS until DONE=1 and BUSY=0, with a bounded wait that scales with the total of the cell. DONE and CHUNK_DONE are clear at the GO write (step 2 and step 7), so a DONE=1 read is the result of this transfer. A timeout fails with `FAIL-DMA-TIMEOUT`. When STATUS reads CHUNK_DONE=1, DONE=0 and BUSY=0, write CONTROL with OPCODE 0, GO=1 and INITIAL_TRANSFER=0, count the write in `go_writes`, and wait with a bound until `dma_busy_probe_o` has risen since that write before the next evaluation. This re-GO rule writes no STATUS bit, so CHUNK_DONE clears only by the hardware when the next chunk starts (CHK-DMA-CHUNKRISE). Log every STATUS value read.
. Leg A. After the poll ends, read STATUS and ERROR_CODE. Read the destination footprint and both guard words through the frontdoor. Compare them with the model (CHK-DMA-IMAGE).
. Leg A. Write 1 to STATUS.DONE and STATUS.CHUNK_DONE, so that the next run starts with both bits clear.
. Leg B. Run the single-chunk total 4 bytes, the drawn single-chunk total (64, 256, 1024 or 4096 bytes; CHUNK_DATA_SIZE equals TOTAL_DATA_SIZE) and the 16384-byte total as four chunks of 4096 bytes, back to back, with INCREMENT=1 and WRAP=0 on both sides and width encoding 2. Then run the drawn transfers (C up to 4096 bytes, total at most 16 KB). Before each transfer, fill the source with unique words and the destination and guards with sentinel words, and write the addresses, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE, CHUNK_DATA_SIZE, TRANSFER_WIDTH = 2, SRC_CONFIG and DST_CONFIG of the transfer. Never write STATUS.DONE between two transfers, so DONE from the previous transfer stays set.
. Leg B. Before the GO write of every transfer, write 1 to STATUS.CHUNK_DONE only. From the second transfer on, read STATUS once just before the GO write (DONE reads 1, the control of CHK-DMA-DONE-CLR). Write CONTROL with OPCODE 0, INITIAL_TRANSFER=1 and GO=1.
. Leg B. From the GO write, sample `dma_busy_probe_o` at every clock and poll STATUS with a bounded wait. The poll ends when the probe has risen since the GO write and STATUS shows DONE=1 and BUSY=0. A timeout fails with `FAIL-DMA-TIMEOUT`. When STATUS reads CHUNK_DONE=1, DONE=0 and BUSY=0, apply the re-GO rule of step 5. For a total of 1024 bytes or more, read CONTROL at least once while the probe reads 1 at the clock of the read (`control_go_busy`). Log every BUSY, DONE and CHUNK_DONE value read, the CONTROL.GO value, and the value of the probe at each clock from the GO write to the end of the poll.
. Leg B. After the poll ends, read STATUS, CONTROL.GO, ERROR_CODE, the destination and the guards (CHK-DMA-GEOM, CHK-DMA-DONE-CLR, CHK-DMA-CHUNKDONE).
. Leg C. Write 1 to STATUS.DONE and STATUS.CHUNK_DONE, because Leg B leaves DONE set.
. Leg C. For each edge cell (low edge: BASE exact and LIMIT wide; high edge: LIMIT exact and BASE wide; both edges exact; both edges exact with a guard word of 4 bytes or more just outside the range at the low end, at the high end and at both ends), in the drawn region order of the cell (source below destination, or the reverse): fill the regions and guards, write BASE equal to the lowest byte and LIMIT equal to the highest byte of the two regions as the cell requires, and write RANGE_VALID = 1. Write the addresses, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE and CHUNK_DATA_SIZE (equal), TRANSFER_WIDTH = 2, and SRC_CONFIG and DST_CONFIG with INCREMENT=1 and WRAP=0. Write CONTROL with OPCODE 0, INITIAL_TRANSFER=1 and GO=1, and poll STATUS until DONE=1 and BUSY=0 with a bounded wait. A timeout fails with `FAIL-DMA-TIMEOUT`.
. Leg C. Read STATUS, ERROR_CODE, the destination and the guards (CHK-DMA-RANGE-OK). Write 1 to STATUS.DONE and STATUS.CHUNK_DONE.
. Leg C. Control. Refill the destination and the guards with sentinel words. Write BASE = 0x10000000 and LIMIT = 0x1003FFFF (the range of step 2, wide over the regions) and run the same copy as in step 13. Poll STATUS until DONE=1 and BUSY=0 with a bounded wait, read STATUS, ERROR_CODE, the destination and the guards (`control_wide` of CHK-DMA-RANGE-OK), and write 1 to STATUS.DONE and STATUS.CHUNK_DONE. Never write RANGE_REGWEN in this leg.
. Leg D. For each algorithm in the drawn order, run three messages: the published vector (56 bytes for SHA-256, 112 bytes for SHA-384 and SHA-512) and the two drawn lengths. After the last algorithm, run the long message (the drawn algorithm, length and DIGEST_SWAP value) as one transfer only. Draw the message bytes of the drawn lengths and of the long message. Place the message in the source region and fill the destination region with sentinel words. Keep the regions disjoint and inside BASE to LIMIT.
. Leg D. For each form (the single-chunk form; for a message with a split form, the drawn split into k equal chunks) run the transfer, with each DIGEST_SWAP value in the drawn order for the published vector and with the drawn DIGEST_SWAP value for a drawn length. Write SRC_ADDR_LO, DST_ADDR_LO, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE = L, CHUNK_DATA_SIZE (L for the single-chunk form, L / k for the split form), TRANSFER_WIDTH = 2 (4 bytes), and SRC_CONFIG and DST_CONFIG with INCREMENT=1 and WRAP=0, so the chunks occupy contiguous ascending addresses (`secure_dma.adoc`, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE, SRC_CONFIG.WRAP, DST_CONFIG.WRAP).
. Leg D. From the second message of the seed on, read STATUS just before the GO write of the initial transfer and log SHA2_DIGEST_VALID (the control of CHK-DMA-HASH-VALID). Write CONTROL with OPCODE 1, 2 or 3 (SHA-256, SHA-384, SHA-512, `dma.hjson`, OPCODE), the DIGEST_SWAP value, INITIAL_TRANSFER=1 and GO=1.
. Leg D. After the initial GO write poll STATUS with a bounded wait until DONE=1 and BUSY=0 (single-chunk form) or until BUSY=0 with CHUNK_DONE=1 or DONE=1 (split form), and log every value read with BUSY=1. A timeout fails with `FAIL-DMA-TIMEOUT`. At DONE=1 write 1 to STATUS.DONE and STATUS.CHUNK_DONE.
. Leg D. For each later chunk of the split form: when STATUS reads CHUNK_DONE=1, DONE=0 and BUSY=0, write 1 to STATUS.CHUNK_DONE, then write CONTROL with the OPCODE and DIGEST_SWAP value of the initial GO write, INITIAL_TRANSFER=0 and GO=1, with no change to the addresses or sizes, and wait with a bound until `dma_busy_probe_o` has risen since that write before the next evaluation (the BUSY condition and the wait of the re-GO rule of step 5). Poll STATUS again as in the step before. At DONE=1 write 1 to STATUS.DONE and STATUS.CHUNK_DONE. More than k GO writes for one transfer fails with `FAIL-DMA-TIMEOUT`.
. Leg D. After the last transfer of the chain, poll STATUS until SHA2_DIGEST_VALID=1 with a bounded wait. A timeout fails CHK-DMA-HASH-VALID.
. Leg D. Read the used SHA2_DIGEST_0 words (8, 12 or 16 for SHA-256, SHA-384 or SHA-512) and the destination bytes. Compare the words with the golden for the DIGEST_SWAP value used (CHK-DMA-HASH). Compare the digest of the single-chunk form with the digest of the split form of the same message and swap value (CHK-DMA-HASH-CHAIN). A message with no split form (no k for which L / k is a multiple of 4, and the long message) runs no CHK-DMA-HASH-CHAIN and does not count it in k. For a control cell of Leg D the leaf logs `OBS-DMA-HASH-CTRL` with the compare result and grades nothing from this step.
. Beat and status checks. Sample `dma_axi_req_probe_o` and `dma_status_probe_o` at every clock from the first GO write of each transfer of Leg A and Leg B to the end of its poll. Grade CHK-DMA-BEAT and CHK-DMA-ADDR on the AR, AW and W beats of each Leg A transfer, CHK-DMA-CHUNKRISE on the CHUNK_DONE level of each transfer, CHK-DMA-DONE-ORDER on the DONE rise against the last B response of each transfer, and CHK-DMA-GO-CHUNKS on the STATUS reads and on the AW and W counts at each later GO write of each transfer of two or more chunks.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DMA-IMAGE
|After DONE, the destination image equals the image that the register text gives for the cell, the guard words are unchanged, and ERROR and ERROR_CODE read 0 (`secure_dma.adoc`, SRC_CONFIG, DST_CONFIG, TRANSFER_WIDTH). The model gives transaction k (k = 0 to T/w - 1, with width w, total T, chunk C) in chunk j = floor(k*w/C) at position p = k - j*C/w. The address of the transaction is SRC + p*w for INCREMENT=1 and WRAP=1, SRC + k*w for INCREMENT=1 and WRAP=0, and SRC for INCREMENT=0. The destination address follows the same rule with DST. The image is the source word of each transaction written in order. A wrong word address sequence, a wrong width, a missing wrap restart or a write outside the footprint gives another image or a changed guard. Anchored on frontdoor reads of the source and destination footprints. Control: every byte that the transfer writes differs from the sentinel byte s, so a missing write shows. The source words are unique, so a word read from a wrong word address shows wherever that word stays in the image. Source bytes are not unique, so at width encodings 0 and 1 a wrong byte address that reads an equal byte value does not show; the beat address check is CHK-DMA-ADDR. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-IMAGE PASS seed=<n> cell=<sI><sW><dI><dW> enc=<e> w=<w> chunk=<C> n=<n> bytes=<T> mem_mismatch=0 guard_ok=1 error=0 go_writes=<g>`, one line per cell and transfer. The six pairs of Leg A that `sep_dma_basic_test` grades are graded only at n of 2 or more (n of 3 or more for the last pair).
|CHK-DMA-BEAT
|Every DMA AR and AW has AxLEN 0, and the read and write beat counts each equal TOTAL divided by the width. Each write beat carries a WSTRB popcount equal to the width of the encoding. At width encoding 2 with INCREMENT=1, the read address step between two reads of one chunk equals 4 (`secure_dma.adoc`, SRC_CONFIG.INCREMENT: incremented by transfer_width after each read). The first read of a later chunk with WRAP=1 is not a step: the address returns to SRC_ADDR_LO there (`secure_dma.adoc`, SRC_CONFIG.WRAP), and CHK-DMA-ADDR grades that address. At width encodings 0 and 1, CHK-DMA-ADDR grades the read addresses on AxADDR[31:2]. A probe that sees no traffic gives a beat count of 0 and fails. Anchored on `dma_axi_req_probe_o`. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-BEAT PASS seed=<n> cell=<sI><sW><dI><dW> enc=<e> w=<w> bytes=<T> ar=<r> aw=<a> axlen_nonzero=0 wstrb_bad=0 rstep_bad=0`, one line per transfer.
|CHK-DMA-ADDR
|Every beat address equals the expectation that the model of CHK-DMA-IMAGE gives from the programmed registers and the width of the encoding (`secure_dma.adoc`, SRC_CONFIG, DST_CONFIG: the address steps by transfer_width, and with WRAP=1 it returns to the starting address when a chunk finishes). At width encoding 2 the check compares the full AxADDR. At width encodings 0 and 1 it compares AxADDR[31:2] with bits [31:2] of the model address, and for each write beat it compares the WSTRB byte lanes with bits [2:0] of the model address. AxADDR[1:0] of a 1-byte or 2-byte beat is logged as `OBS-DMA-ADDR-LOW LOG seed=<n> enc=<e> beats=<k> low_eq_model=<m>` and not graded, because the specification does not state the address low bits that the TL-UL to AXI4 path gives a narrow transaction (DMA transaction size rule). Anchored on `dma_axi_req_probe_o` against the model. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-ADDR PASS seed=<n> cell=<sI><sW><dI><dW> enc=<e> beats=<k> addr_mismatch=0 strb_lane_mismatch=0`, one line per transfer.
|CHK-DMA-GEOM
|After each transfer DONE=1, BUSY=0, GO=0 and ERROR=0, the destination equals the source and the guards are intact (`secure_dma.adoc`, CONTROL.GO, STATUS). A DUT that leaves GO set, leaves BUSY set, drops a tail or writes outside the footprint fails. Anchored on frontdoor reads of STATUS, CONTROL and the images. Control, on totals of 1024 bytes or more: a CONTROL read returns GO=1 while `dma_busy_probe_o` reads 1 at the clock of the read, so the transfer ran. The seed must show it on at least one transfer, else CTRL-MISSING. A total below 1024 bytes ends before a frontdoor read can land, so it is graded on the reads after DONE only. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-GEOM PASS seed=<n> total=<T> chunk=<C> n=<n> go_writes=<g> done=1 busy=0 go=0 error=0 mem_mismatch=0 control_go_busy=<1\|->`, with `-` for a total below 1024 bytes or a transfer where no read landed.
|CHK-DMA-DONE-CLR
|With no write of STATUS.DONE between the previous DONE and the GO write, at least one STATUS read with BUSY=1 shows DONE=0 before DONE rises again (`secure_dma.adoc`, STATUS.DONE: cleared automatically when a new transfer starts). A DUT that keeps the old DONE shows DONE=1 on every read of the transfer. Anchored on STATUS readback and `dma_busy_probe_o`. Control: DONE reads 1 on the read just before the GO write. The check runs on the Leg B totals of 1024 bytes or more (the 16384-byte total on every seed, and the drawn total when it is 1024 or 4096 bytes), whose transfer time lets the frontdoor read STATUS several times. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-DONE-CLR PASS seed=<n> total=<T> pre_go_done=1 busy_reads_done0=<k>`.
|CHK-DMA-CHUNKDONE
|A single-chunk transfer reads CHUNK_DONE=0 at every STATUS poll from the GO write to DONE, with CHUNK_DONE cleared by a write of 1 before the GO write so that a bit from an earlier transfer cannot show (`secure_dma.adoc`, STATUS.CHUNK_DONE: raised only for multi-chunk memory-to-memory transfers). A DUT that raises CHUNK_DONE for a single chunk shows 1. Anchored on STATUS readback. Control: the multi-chunk transfers of the seed, polled the same way, read CHUNK_DONE=1 at least once in total, or bit 9 of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (PIC source 10, with INTR_ENABLE.DMA_CHUNK_DONE=1) rises at least once during them, else CTRL-MISSING. The probe is part of the control because CHUNK_DONE clears when the next chunk starts, so a poll can miss a short pulse (`secure_dma.adoc`, STATUS.CHUNK_DONE, INTR_STATE.DMA_CHUNK_DONE). Evidence: `CHK-DMA-CHUNKDONE PASS seed=<n> n=<n> single=<0\|1> polls_with_chunk_done=<k> control_multi_polls=<m> control_chunk_irq_rises=<r>`.
|CHK-DMA-CHUNKRISE
|An n-chunk transfer shows n-1 to n rises of CHUNK_DONE, and a single-chunk transfer shows none (`secure_dma.adoc`, STATUS.CHUNK_DONE: only raised for multi-chunk memory-to-memory transfers). In an n-chunk transfer, each of the first n-1 CHUNK_DONE rises is followed by a later chunk start, so it is followed by a fall before the next CHUNK_DONE rise or the DONE rise, with no STATUS write between the rise and the fall (`secure_dma.adoc`, STATUS.CHUNK_DONE: cleared automatically by the hardware when starting the transfer of a new chunk). An n-th rise belongs to the last chunk, which no new chunk follows, so the check does not require its fall, and its order against the DONE rise is not graded. The window starts at the first GO write of the transfer and ends at the end of its poll, so the STATUS writes of steps 7 and 8 are outside it. A DUT that raises CHUNK_DONE for a single chunk, or that keeps CHUNK_DONE set when the next chunk starts, fails. Anchored on `dma_status_probe_o` and the frontdoor write log. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-CHUNKRISE PASS seed=<n> n=<n> rises=<r> hw_falls=<f> last_rise_exempt=<0\|1> status_writes_in_window=0`, with f at least the smaller of r and n-1, one line per transfer.
|CHK-DMA-GO-CHUNKS
|A transfer of n >= 2 chunks without hardware handshake stops after each non-final chunk and continues only after the next GO. Exactly n-1 STATUS reads show CHUNK_DONE=1, BUSY=0 and DONE=0, and at the start of each later GO write the AW and W counts equal the transactions of the chunks before it, so the destination takes no beat between the stop and that GO ("A start is a write of `CONTROL.GO` while `STATUS.BUSY` is clear: the first chunk of a transfer, with `INITIAL_TRANSFER` set, or a later chunk, with `GO` alone.", `dma.adoc:79-81`; "For a transfer in several chunks without hardware handshake, wait for `STATUS.CHUNK_DONE` after each chunk and start the next one by writing `CONTROL` with `GO=1` and `INITIAL_TRANSFER=0`.", `sep-programming.adoc:743-745`; CHUNK_DONE is "Only raised for multi-chunk memory-to-memory transfers.", `secure_dma.rdl:327-328`). A DUT that runs on without the next GO shows a beat before that GO write, and one that skips a stop shows fewer stop reads. The number of GO writes is stimulus and is not graded. Anchor: STATUS readback, the frontdoor write log and the AW and W counts of `dma_axi_req_probe_o` (approved observation tap). Control: the single-chunk transfers of Leg B show no such STATUS read, else CTRL-MISSING (`secure_dma.rdl:328`). Evidence: `CHK-DMA-GO-CHUNKS PASS seed=<n> n=<n> stop_reads=<k> expect_stops=<n-1> progress_bad=0 aw/w/expect_at_go=<j>:<aw>/<w>/<want>,... <tag>`, one line per transfer of two or more chunks.
|CHK-DMA-DONE-ORDER
|No DONE rise in the window comes before the last B response of the transfer (`secure_dma.adoc`, STATUS.DONE: configured DMA operation is complete). The window starts at the first GO write of the transfer and ends at the end of its poll. A DUT that raises DONE early shows the rise before the B response. Anchored on the DONE level of `dma_status_probe_o` and the B handshake of `dma_axi_req_probe_o`. Control on the same observables: in the window the probe shows a B handshake count equal to the AW count, and at least one DONE rise. A transfer with no B handshake, a B count other than the AW count, or no DONE rise in the window fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-DONE-ORDER PASS seed=<n> bytes=<T> aw=<a> b=<a> done_rises=<k> last_b_clk=<b> done_rise_clk=<d>`, one line per transfer.
|CHK-DMA-RANGE-OK
|A copy with BASE and LIMIT exactly on the edges, with or without guards, in the drawn region order, sets DONE, leaves ERROR clear and copies the data (`secure_dma.adoc`, ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT: inclusive). A DUT that rejects an edge byte, or treats LIMIT as exclusive, shows ERROR or a missing tail. Anchored on frontdoor reads of STATUS, ERROR_CODE and the destination. Control: the same regions with a wide range complete in the same seed. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-RANGE-OK PASS seed=<n> edge=<low\|high\|both\|guard> order=<src_low\|dst_low> base=0x<b> limit=0x<l> done=1 error=0 mem_mismatch=0 control_wide=1`.
|CHK-DMA-HASH
|The used SHA2_DIGEST_0 words with DIGEST_SWAP=1 equal the digest of an independent SHA-2 library as big-endian words (`secure_dma.adoc`, CONTROL.DIGEST_SWAP). The words with DIGEST_SWAP=0 equal their byte reversal at the same position. The copy bytes equal the source. A wrong digest, a wrong word order, a missing swap or a wrong algorithm for the opcode gives a mismatch. Anchored on frontdoor reads of the digest words against the library digest. The unused words are not read. The three control cells of Leg D print `OBS-DMA-HASH-CTRL` and no PASS line. Control: before each split-form run, a hash of the complemented message (graded window closed) leaves its own digest in SHA2_DIGEST and must match its library digest, which differs from the graded one (`CTL-DMA-HASH-SCRUB`), else CTRL-MISSING; so a split run that writes no digest reads the control digest. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-HASH PASS seed=<n> alg=<256\|384\|512> len=<L> split=<k> swap=<s> words=<8\|12\|16> mismatch=0`.
|CHK-DMA-HASH-VALID
|After the GO write of the initial transfer, at least one STATUS read with BUSY=1 shows SHA2_DIGEST_VALID=0, and the bit reads 1 within a bounded wait after the DONE of the last transfer of the chain (`secure_dma.adoc`, STATUS.SHA2_DIGEST_VALID: cleared on the initial transfer, set when the digest is written; `sep-programming.adoc`: read the digest only when the bit is set). The specification does not state that DONE implies the bit, so the leaf polls for it, and a timeout fails. A DUT that never clears the bit, or never sets it, fails. Anchored on STATUS readback. Control: the bit reads 1 on the read just before the GO write of the second message. The clear is graded on the long message (1024 bytes or more), whose transfer time lets a frontdoor STATUS read land with BUSY=1, the same bound as CHK-DMA-GEOM. A long message with no STATUS read at BUSY=1 logs CTRL-MISSING and fails. A shorter message ends before a frontdoor read can land, so it is graded for the set only. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-HASH-VALID PASS seed=<n> alg=<a> len=<L> busy_reads_valid0=<k> after_last=1 control_before_second=1`, with `busy_reads_valid0=-` for a message below 1024 bytes.
|CHK-DMA-HASH-CHAIN
|A single-chunk run and a split run of the same message and swap value give the same digest. The split run is one transfer with TOTAL_DATA_SIZE = L and k equal chunks, INITIAL_TRANSFER on its first GO write only (`secure_dma.adoc`, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE: the complete transfer operation may consist of multiple chunks; CONTROL.INITIAL_TRANSFER: the initial transfer initializes the SHA engine for one transfer that spans multiple single DMA transfers). The SHA-2 library digest of the whole message is the expected value of both runs (CHK-DMA-HASH). A DUT that restarts the hash at a later chunk, or that drops or repeats a chunk, gives another digest. The split run has 2 to 4 chunks, so it always has a later chunk. A message with no split form and the long message run no CHK-DMA-HASH-CHAIN. Anchored on frontdoor reads of the digest words of both runs. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-HASH-CHAIN PASS seed=<n> alg=<a> len=<L> split=<k> single_eq_split=1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry grades the cells of its four legs only. The open tests `sep_dma_basic_test` and `sep_dma_hash_test` keep the cells that they grade: AxSIZE of a DMA beat is not graded, a TRANSFER_WIDTH of 3 is graded by `sep_dma_basic_test`, and the software clear of STATUS.DONE is graded by `sep_dma_basic_test`. Not claimed: INCREMENT=0 with WRAP=0 and two or more chunks, the number of GO writes of a multi-chunk transfer (stimulus), the maximum TOTAL_DATA_SIZE and CHUNK_DATA_SIZE, a short final chunk or a chunk larger than the total, the content of the unused SHA2_DIGEST words, SHA2_DIGEST_VALID between the transfers of a chain, the order of the digest write and DONE, the order of the DONE clear and the BUSY set at the start of a transfer, a non-zero ADDR_HI (graded in entry 4.13), a region that straddles an edge, an alignment difference between the source and the destination address (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), and a second register master (`dma.adoc`, overview, shows one register slave). The zero-size error, the hash width rule and the range lock are graded in entry 4.13.

'''''

[[sep_dma_irq_error_lock_rand_test]]
===== 4.13 `sep_dma_irq_error_lock_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`) with real fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`), because the leaf asserts `rst_ni`. 4 seeds. Verilator and VCS, all four legs on both; testlist groups `cpu_stub` and `all`, file `system.toml`, one testlist entry. Leg L1 runs six short trials per run, each from a cold reset.

*Source Module*: `sep_dma_irq_error_lock_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip register frontdoor over `s_axi` (also for the SRAM images); a model of the interrupt gate (the line of source n is the matching event AND the matching INTR_ENABLE bit). Approved probes: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` indices 8, 9 and 10 (PIC sources 9, 10 and 11), `dma_busy_probe_o` and `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`. The probe `dma_busy_probe_o` orders the re-GO of a multi-chunk copy only. No added tap. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. Dimensions and legal ranges: for each L1 event two INTR_ENABLE values, one with the bit of the event clear (one of the four such values) and one with the bit of the event set (one of the four such values, 7 included), and the order of the trials; chunk size and chunk count of the multi-chunk copy; the outside case of leg L3 (one of four); regions of leg L3 (SRAM, 4-aligned, disjoint, total 16 to 1024 bytes) and their order (source below destination, or the reverse); guards (4 bytes or more); outside distance (4 bytes or more); order of the unlock attempts; for leg L4 the copy size (4-aligned, 16 to 1024 bytes) and one non-zero SRC_ADDR_HI and one non-zero DST_ADDR_HI value (1 to 0xFFFFFFFF); all data. Every drawn size, chunk size, offset, guard and outside distance is a multiple of 4, and TOTAL_DATA_SIZE is a multiple of CHUNK_DATA_SIZE, so no legal transfer breaks the alignment rule (`secure_dma.rdl`, SRC_ADDR_LO, DST_ADDR_LO, TRANSFER_WIDTH). A zero size and a narrow hash width are error injection only (step 7), and so is a non-zero ADDR_HI (step 15). `reseed = 4` samples four different enable values, trial orders, chunk shapes, outside cases, region placements, outside distances and attempt orders. Every seed runs each of the three L1 events with the bit of the event clear and with it set (6 trials); the trial with the bit set is the twin. The 24 (INTR_ENABLE, event) cells depend on the seed and close on a merge of regressions (<<sep-fcov-rand,SEP_FCOV>>), in about three regressions of four seeds.

*Objective*: Four contracts hold. Each trial starts from a cold reset. ERROR_CODE clears with a write of 1 to STATUS.ERROR (`dma.hjson`, ERROR_CODE). The same write releases INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR and the line of source 11 ("Since interrupts are implemented as status bits, they are cleared by writing a '1' to the corresponding bit in the STATUS register", OpenTitan DMA `programmers_guide.md:39`, tenstorrent/tt-opentitan@d1ce369cb; "Status completion and error bits are write-one-to-clear", `dma.adoc:131-132`). The release of a bit that INTR_TEST forced has no stated write (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The open interrupt tests grade INTR_TEST routes and the DMA_DONE interrupt, `sep_dma_basic_test` grades the opcode, address and ASID error legs and the width encoding 3, and `sep_dma_hash_test` grades the DMA_DONE interrupt through the PIC. Those cells are controls here.

* Leg L1, enable gate (`secure_dma.adoc`, INTR_STATE, INTR_ENABLE; `interrupts.adoc`, SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map). INTR_ENABLE gates each DMA interrupt line to PIC sources 9, 10 and 11 against the matching event: DMA_DONE (bit 0) for a completed copy, DMA_CHUNK_DONE (bit 1) for a multi-chunk copy and DMA_ERROR (bit 2) for an error. A single-chunk copy raises no chunk event: STATUS.CHUNK_DONE is "Only raised for multi-chunk memory-to-memory transfers" (`secure_dma.rdl:327-328`), and "Chunk completion applies to multi-chunk transfers without hardware handshake; it is not a per-trigger interrupt in handshake mode" (`dma.adoc:129-131`). Every STATUS read of the single-chunk copy shows CHUNK_DONE=0, INTR_STATE.DMA_CHUNK_DONE reads 0 at the end of the trial and the line of source 10 stays low, with INTR_ENABLE.DMA_CHUNK_DONE set or clear (CHK-DMA-IRQ-SINGLE).
* Leg L2, SIZE_ERROR (`secure_dma.adoc`, ERROR_CODE, STATUS, INTR_STATE; `dma.adoc`, overview). A TOTAL_DATA_SIZE of zero, a CHUNK_DATA_SIZE of zero, and hashing at a width of 1 or 2 bytes each set ERROR_CODE.SIZE_ERROR, STATUS.ERROR and INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR. A write of 1 to STATUS.ERROR then releases INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR and the line of source 11 (CHK-DMA-INTR-RELEASE).
* Leg L3, range lock (`doc/programmer/src/sep-programming.adoc`, zero fill procedure: write MuBi4 False `0x9` to RANGE_REGWEN to fix the window until reset; `hw/sys/sep/doc/dma.adoc`, Zero Fill; `secure_dma.adoc`, ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_BASE, ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT, RANGE_VALID, RANGE_REGWEN; `dma.hjson`, RANGE_VALID). After the write of 0x9, RANGE_REGWEN reads 0x9, no write to it re-opens the lock, and writes to BASE, LIMIT and RANGE_VALID do not change their values until reset. `sep_dma_basic_test` grades one lock and one rejected write. This leg adds the lock persistence over unlock attempts and after an error, and the lock of RANGE_VALID. The release of the lock by a cold reset and the reset values of the range registers are graded in <<sep_cpu_reset_retention_test,entry 5.15>>.
* Leg L4, non-zero ADDR_HI (`dma.adoc`, SEP secure DMA integration: the DMA master interface has 32-bit addresses, and ASID 0x7 is the only route). A copy with legal LO addresses, the SEP ASID and a non-zero SRC_ADDR_HI or DST_ADDR_HI moves no data to the 32-bit destination at DST_ADDR_LO, and it ends with DONE or ERROR in a bounded wait. No document defines the ADDR_HI rule for the SEP DMA route, so STATUS.ERROR and ERROR_CODE are logged and not graded.

*Test Coverage*: L1: a single-chunk copy, a multi-chunk copy and TOTAL_DATA_SIZE=0, each on every seed with a drawn INTR_ENABLE value that clears the bit of the event and a drawn value that sets it (the twin); the 24 cells close on a merge of regressions. The single-chunk copy grades STATUS.CHUNK_DONE, INTR_STATE.DMA_CHUNK_DONE and the line of source 10 at 0 (CHK-DMA-IRQ-SINGLE). L2: zero total, zero chunk, both zero, hashing at width encoding 0 and hashing at width encoding 1, each with INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR and the line of source 11 read after the write of 1 to STATUS.ERROR (CHK-DMA-INTR-RELEASE) and with a legal transfer after recovery, and hashing at width encoding 2 as the control. L3: lock from idle and after a size error, unlock attempts with RANGE_REGWEN values 0x6, 0xF, 0x9 and 0x0, writes to BASE, LIMIT and RANGE_VALID while locked, and one drawn outside-range trial (logged only). L4: one copy with a non-zero SRC_ADDR_HI and one with a non-zero DST_ADDR_HI, each with a recovery copy, and the same copy with both ADDR_HI registers 0 as the control. The test builds on the open tests named in the objective. Only the cells above are graded here. The open owners keep the cells they grade. Functional coverage: `sep_dma_rand_cg` (`cp_irq_x_enable`, graded on a merge of regressions), `sep_dma_completion_route_cg` (`x_route_mode`), `sep_dma_config_fault_cg` (`cp_size_zero_kind`, `cp_hash_width_fault`, and `cp_addr_hi_nonzero` over the two non-zero L4 copies), `sep_dma_error_terminal_cg` (`cp_error_x_aggregate`), `sep_dma_endpoint_cg` (`cp_src_x_dst_endpoint`), `sep_dma_range_config_cg` (`cp_range_valid_at_go`, `cp_unlocked_write`, `cp_regwen_value`) and `sep_dma_containment_cg` (`cp_locked_write_refused`, `cp_regwen_invalid_value`, `cp_lock_recovery`, `cp_lock_ctx`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw every value from the seed and log it in `DRAW` lines. Every trial below starts with a cold reset: assert `rst_ni`, release it, and wait with a bound for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` before the first register access.
. L1. For each event (single-chunk copy, multi-chunk copy, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE=0) and each of its two drawn INTR_ENABLE values, in the drawn order, run one trial. After the cold reset write BASE = 0x10000000, LIMIT = 0x1003FFFF (the SEP SRAM, `memory_map.adoc`) and RANGE_VALID = 1. Write ADDR_SPACE_ID = 0x77 and both ADDR_HI registers = 0. Fill the source with unique words and the destination with sentinel words. Write INTR_ENABLE.
. L1. Write the addresses, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE, CHUNK_DATA_SIZE (equal to TOTAL_DATA_SIZE for the single-chunk copy; a drawn chunk shape for the multi-chunk copy), TRANSFER_WIDTH = 2, INCREMENT=1 and WRAP=0 on both sides. For the zero-size event write TOTAL_DATA_SIZE = 0 with a legal drawn CHUNK_DATA_SIZE (error injection at the TOTAL_DATA_SIZE write; expected response: STATUS.ERROR=1, ERROR_CODE.SIZE_ERROR=1, INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR=1 and the line of source 11 asserted when enabled; `secure_dma.adoc`, ERROR_CODE, INTR_STATE). Write CONTROL with OPCODE 0, INITIAL_TRANSFER=1 and GO=1. A multi-chunk copy follows the re-GO rule: when STATUS reads CHUNK_DONE=1, DONE=0 and BUSY=0, the leaf writes 1 to STATUS.CHUNK_DONE, writes CONTROL with OPCODE 0, GO=1 and INITIAL_TRANSFER=0, and waits with a bound until `dma_busy_probe_o` has risen since that write before the next evaluation.
. L1. Sample `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` indices 8, 9 and 10 and `dma_busy_probe_o` at every clock from the GO write. Poll STATUS until DONE=1 (or until ERROR=1 for the zero-size event) with a bounded wait, then keep sampling for a further bounded wait. STATUS is clear after the cold reset, so a DONE=1 read is the result of this copy. A timeout on a copy fails with `FAIL-DMA-TIMEOUT`. Record whether each of the three lines asserted at any clock in the window. Read INTR_STATE and log it.
. L1. Compare the three recorded lines with the model for the event and the INTR_ENABLE value (CHK-DMA-IRQ). For the single-chunk copy grade CHK-DMA-IRQ-SINGLE: no STATUS read shows CHUNK_DONE, INTR_STATE.DMA_CHUNK_DONE reads 0 and the line of source 10 is low, with either INTR_ENABLE value. The control is the multi-chunk trial with DMA_CHUNK_DONE enabled, which shows source 10 high.
. L2. For each kind (TOTAL_DATA_SIZE=0, CHUNK_DATA_SIZE=0, both 0, hashing at width encoding 0, hashing at width encoding 1), run one trial after a cold reset. Program the range, ADDR_SPACE_ID and both ADDR_HI registers as in step 2 and the legal fields (TOTAL_DATA_SIZE and CHUNK_DATA_SIZE equal to a drawn size, TRANSFER_WIDTH = 2, INCREMENT=1 and WRAP=0 on both sides), and write INTR_ENABLE = 7.
. L2. Inject the error and start the transfer. For a size kind write TOTAL_DATA_SIZE = 0, CHUNK_DATA_SIZE = 0 or both 0 (point: the size registers; kind: size zero). For a hashing kind write TRANSFER_WIDTH with the encoding of the kind, 0 or 1 (point: TRANSFER_WIDTH with hashing selected; kind: width below 4 bytes). Write CONTROL with GO=1, with OPCODE 1 (SHA-256) and INITIAL_TRANSFER=1 for a hashing kind and OPCODE 0 for a size kind. Expected response (`secure_dma.adoc`, ERROR_CODE, INTR_STATE; `dma.adoc`, overview): STATUS.ERROR=1, ERROR_CODE.SIZE_ERROR=1, INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR=1 and the line of source 11 asserted.
. L2. Poll STATUS until ERROR=1 with a bounded wait. A timeout fails CHK-DMA-SIZE-ERR. Read ERROR_CODE and INTR_STATE. Sample the line of source 11. Do not grade DONE, BUSY or the beats after the error.
. L2. Recovery with no reset. Read INTR_STATE before the write; DMA_ERROR reads 1 (control of CHK-DMA-INTR-RELEASE, else CTRL-MISSING). Write 1 to STATUS.ERROR alone. Read STATUS, ERROR_CODE, INTR_STATE and the line of source 11; ERROR and ERROR_CODE read 0 (`dma.hjson`, ERROR_CODE), INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR reads 0 and source 11 is low (CHK-DMA-INTR-RELEASE). Run the DMA clean state. Write CONTROL with GO=0, because the GO state after an error is not stated. Keep the range registers and program a legal copy of drawn size: addresses, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE and CHUNK_DATA_SIZE equal, TRANSFER_WIDTH = 2, INCREMENT=1 and WRAP=0 on both sides. Write CONTROL with OPCODE 0, INITIAL_TRANSFER=1 and GO=1. Poll DONE with a bounded wait. Compare the destination with the source and read ERROR (the recovery of CHK-DMA-SIZE-ERR).
. L2. Control: for each hashing trial, after a cold reset run it again at TRANSFER_WIDTH = 2 (width encoding 2) with the same OPCODE and size. Poll DONE with a bounded wait. Read ERROR and ERROR_CODE.
. L3, outside trial. For the drawn case (source wholly below BASE, source wholly above LIMIT, destination wholly below BASE, or destination wholly above LIMIT), run one trial after a cold reset: draw regions as in the randomization, place the other region inside the range, and set BASE and LIMIT so that one region lies wholly outside the range by at least 4 bytes (error injection; point: the region against BASE and LIMIT; kind: region wholly outside BASE to LIMIT). Write RANGE_VALID = 1 and GO. Poll until DONE, ERROR or the bound. Log STATUS.ERROR and the ERROR_CODE bits (`OBS-DMA-RANGE-OUT`). Grade nothing. A timeout in this step is logged and does not fail.
. L3, lock from idle. After a cold reset write non-reset values to BASE and LIMIT and RANGE_VALID = 1, and read BASE, LIMIT, RANGE_VALID and RANGE_REGWEN (the control: BASE and LIMIT read the written values, RANGE_VALID reads 1, its reset value is 0, and RANGE_REGWEN reads 0x6). Write 0x9 (MuBi4 False, `sep-programming.adoc`, zero fill procedure) to RANGE_REGWEN. Write new values to BASE and LIMIT and 0 to RANGE_VALID, and read the four registers (CHK-DMA-LOCK, attempt `lock`). Every new value differs from the value written before the lock.
. L3, unlock attempts. Error injection: the point is a write to RANGE_REGWEN, BASE, LIMIT or RANGE_VALID while the lock is applied; the kind is a write to a locked register, with the values 0x6, 0xF, 0x9 and 0x0 for RANGE_REGWEN. Expected response (`secure_dma.adoc`, RANGE_REGWEN; `dma.hjson`, RANGE_VALID; `sep-programming.adoc`, zero fill procedure): RANGE_REGWEN reads 0x9, BASE and LIMIT keep the values written before the lock and RANGE_VALID keeps 1. In a drawn order write 0x6, 0xF, 0x9 and 0x0 to RANGE_REGWEN. After each write, write a new value to BASE and to LIMIT and 0 to RANGE_VALID, then read BASE, LIMIT, RANGE_VALID and RANGE_REGWEN (CHK-DMA-LOCK).
. L3, lock after an error. After a cold reset write ADDR_SPACE_ID = 0x77, BASE = 0x10000000, LIMIT = 0x1003FFFF and RANGE_VALID = 1, and read the four registers (the same control as in step 12). Write the legal fields with TOTAL_DATA_SIZE = 0 and GO to force the size error, and wait with a bound for STATUS.ERROR=1 (control). Write 0x9 to RANGE_REGWEN and repeat the writes and the unlock attempts of steps 12 and 13.
. L4, non-zero ADDR_HI. For SRC_ADDR_HI, then for DST_ADDR_HI, run one trial after a cold reset. Program the range, ADDR_SPACE_ID = 0x77 and the source words as in step 2, and fill the destination and its guards with sentinel words. Write the L2 addresses, TOTAL_DATA_SIZE and CHUNK_DATA_SIZE equal to the drawn L4 size, TRANSFER_WIDTH = 2, INCREMENT=1 and WRAP=0 on both sides, the drawn non-zero value to the ADDR_HI register of the trial and 0 to the other one (error injection; point: SRC_ADDR_HI or DST_ADDR_HI; kind: non-zero with legal LO addresses). Write CONTROL with OPCODE 0, INITIAL_TRANSFER=1 and GO=1.
. L4. Poll STATUS until DONE=1 or ERROR=1 with a bounded wait. Read ERROR_CODE and log STATUS and ERROR_CODE (`OBS-DMA-HI-ADDR`). Read back the destination and both guards (CHK-DMA-HI-ADDR).
. L4. Recovery with no reset, as in step 9: write 1 to STATUS.ERROR, run the DMA clean state, write CONTROL with GO=0, read back the destination and both guards a second time (CHK-DMA-HI-ADDR), fill the destination and its guards with sentinel words again and run the same copy with both ADDR_HI registers 0. Poll DONE with a bounded wait and compare the destination with the source.
. L4. Control: after a cold reset run the same copy with both ADDR_HI registers 0 of step 15. Poll DONE with a bounded wait and compare the destination with the source.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DMA-IRQ
|Source 9 asserts for a completed copy only with DMA_DONE enabled, source 10 for a multi-chunk copy only with DMA_CHUNK_DONE enabled, and source 11 for an error only with DMA_ERROR enabled. A disabled source never asserts (`secure_dma.adoc`, INTR_ENABLE; `interrupts.adoc`, SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map). For the zero-size event the lines of sources 9 and 10 are logged when enabled and must read 0 when disabled. For the single-chunk event the line of source 10 must read 0 with INTR_ENABLE.DMA_CHUNK_DONE set or clear (`secure_dma.rdl:327-328`; `dma.adoc:129-131`); CHK-DMA-IRQ-SINGLE grades the same cell with its STATUS and INTR_STATE reads. A DUT that ignores INTR_ENABLE asserts a disabled line, and a DUT that routes the event to another source fails the twin. Anchored on `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`. Control: the twin trial for the same event, in the same seed, with the bit of the event set, shows the assertion. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-IRQ PASS seed=<n> intr_en=0x<e> event=<done\|chunk\|error> src9=<a> src10=<b> src11=<c> expect=<x>/<y>/<z> twin=<a2>/<b2>/<c2>`. A logged line shows `-` in `expect`.
|CHK-DMA-IRQ-SINGLE
|A single-chunk copy (CHUNK_DATA_SIZE equal to TOTAL_DATA_SIZE, no hardware handshake) raises no chunk-done report: every STATUS read of the trial shows CHUNK_DONE=0, INTR_STATE.DMA_CHUNK_DONE reads 0 at the end of the trial and the line of source 10 stays low, with INTR_ENABLE.DMA_CHUNK_DONE set and clear (`secure_dma.rdl:327-328`; `dma.adoc:129-131`; OpenTitan DMA `programmers_guide.md:39`: "Since interrupts are implemented as status bits, they are cleared by writing a '1' to the corresponding bit in the STATUS register", so the interrupt is the status bit; `dma.adoc:129-131` rules over OpenTitan DMA `programmers_guide.md:35`, which states an interrupt after each chunk). A DUT that raises CHUNK_DONE for a single chunk shows 1 in STATUS, in INTR_STATE or on source 10. Anchor: STATUS and INTR_STATE readback and `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` index 9 (approved). Control: the multi-chunk trial of the same seed with DMA_CHUNK_DONE enabled shows source 10 high, else CTRL-MISSING. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-IRQ-SINGLE PASS seed=<n> intr_en=0x<e> chunk_en=<0 or 1> status_chunk_done_reads=0 intr_chunk_done=0 src10=0 intr_state=0x<s> control_mc_src10=1`.
|CHK-DMA-SIZE-ERR
|Each size-error kind sets STATUS.ERROR, ERROR_CODE.SIZE_ERROR and INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR, asserts PIC source 11, and after a write of 1 to STATUS.ERROR, with no reset, ERROR and ERROR_CODE read 0 and a legal transfer completes with DONE=1, ERROR=0 and a destination equal to the source (`secure_dma.adoc`, ERROR_CODE.SIZE_ERROR: TRANSFER_WIDTH invalid, zero size, or inline hashing without 32-bit width; `dma.hjson`, ERROR_CODE). A DUT that accepts a zero size or a narrow hash shows ERROR=0, and a DUT that stays in the error state shows ERROR or ERROR_CODE set after the clear, or no DONE for the recovery copy. Anchored on frontdoor reads of STATUS, ERROR_CODE and INTR_STATE and on the line of source 11. Control: hashing at width encoding 2 completes with ERROR clear in the same seed. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-SIZE-ERR PASS seed=<n> kind=<total\|chunk\|both\|hash_w0\|hash_w1> error=1 size_error=1 intr_dma_error=1 src11=1 error_cleared=1 code_cleared=1 recovery_done=1 control_width_ok=1`.
|CHK-DMA-INTR-RELEASE
|For each size-error kind, after the write of 1 to STATUS.ERROR, INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR reads 0 and the line of source 11 is low (OpenTitan DMA `programmers_guide.md:39`: "Since interrupts are implemented as status bits, they are cleared by writing a '1' to the corresponding bit in the STATUS register"; `dma.adoc:131-132`: "Status completion and error bits are write-one-to-clear"; `secure_dma.rdl:8-22`: INTR_STATE is read-only to software). A DUT that keeps INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR set after the write shows 1. Anchor: INTR_STATE readback and `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` index 10 (approved). Control: INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR reads 1 before the write, else CTRL-MISSING.
